## Quality signals

Computed from each package's files and its `Bluefile.lock`, never claimed; `gen/quality.b` computes them again and fails on any cell that differs. `docs` is the public definitions with a `@doc` over all of them, `tests` the `test` blocks, `reach` the capabilities the lock records from the package's `reach` declaration (`—` when it declares none, `pure` when it declares an empty reach), `heni` the mutants caught over the mutants run, summed over the package's rows in `heni/ratio.baseline` (`—` when it has none), `conformance` the lock checks held: the lock is present, its `bluefile_b3` is the BLAKE3 of the Bluefile, and its manifest names the Bluefile's package and version. `model-eval` is `—` for every package: H9 scores a model once per release over `eval/tasks`, and no task belongs to a package (`scored_per_release`).

## angou

`angou` 0.1.1 · needs: kazu, retsu

angou (暗号) — number theory and the classical ciphers built on it.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `angou::is_prime(n)` |  |  |  |  |

| `angou::primes_below(n)` |  |  |  |  |
| `angou::prime_factors(n)` |  |  |  |  |
| `angou::factor_from(n, k)` |  |  |  |  |

| `angou::carmichael_lambda(n)` |  |  |  | carmichael_lambda: Carmichael's lambda: the exponent of the group of units mod n, i.e. the |
| `angou::fermat_probable_prime(n, a)` |  |  |  | fermat_probable_prime: Fermat's test to base a. A `false` is a PROOF that n is composite; a `true` |

## baito

`baito` 0.1.0 · needs: retsu

baito (バイト) — bytes as data: integers at a declared width and order, and record layouts that say where each field sits.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `baito::kind_width(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_width: the bytes a scalar kind takes, nil when the word names no scalar. |
| `baito::signed?(kind)` |  |  |  | signed?: whether a scalar kind reads its top bit as a sign. |
| `baito::int_bytes(n, count, order?)` |  |  |  | int_bytes: the low `count` bytes of an integer in two's complement, little first unless `order` is `:big`. |
| `baito::bytes_int(bytes, at, count, order?, sign?)` |  |  |  | bytes_int: the integer `count` bytes hold at position `at`, signed when `sign` is true. Reading past the end raises. |
| `baito::fits?(n, kind)` |  |  |  | fits?: whether an integer can be held by a scalar kind. |
| `baito::align_up(n, alignment)` |  |  |  | align_up: the first multiple of `alignment` at or past `n`. |
| `baito::compound_kind(kind)` |  |  |  | compound_kind: what shape a field kind has: `:scalar`, `:layout`, or a list's head. |
| `baito::kind_size(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_size: the bytes a field kind takes, and raises naming a kind that is none. |
| `baito::kind_alignment(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_alignment: where a field kind must start: its own width for a scalar, 1 for bytes and text, the widest member for a nested layout. |
| `baito::layout(fields, opts?)` |  |  |  | layout: a record layout from `[name, kind]` pairs, each field at the next offset its alignment allows and the whole rounded up to its widest member, as a C struct. `{packed: true}` puts each field straight after the last and adds no padding. |
| `baito::width(l)` |  |  |  | width: the bytes a record of this layout takes. |
| `baito::field_of(l, name)` |  |  |  | field_of: the placed field of this name, and raises naming the fields a layout has when there is none. |
| `baito::offset(l, path)` |  |  |  | offset: where a field starts, a dotted list of names walking into nested layouts. |
| `baito::scalar_bytes(value, kind, order, name)` |  |  |  | scalar_bytes: an integer field's bytes, refused by the field's name when it does not fit. |
| `baito::text_bytes_padded(value, count, name)` |  |  |  | text_bytes_padded: a text field's bytes, NUL padded, refused when no NUL fits. |
| `baito::raw_bytes_padded(value, count, name)` |  |  |  | raw_bytes_padded: a bytes field's bytes, zero padded. |
| `baito::array_bytes(value, kind, count, order, name)` |  |  |  | array_bytes: an array field's bytes, an absent item as zeros. |
| `baito::field_bytes(value, kind, order, name)` |  |  |  | field_bytes: any field's bytes, by its kind. |
| `baito::pack(l, record, order?)` |  |  |  | pack: a record as this layout's bytes, an absent field as zeros and the padding zeroed. A value that does not fit its field raises by the field's name. |
| `baito::text_of(bytes)` |  |  |  | text_of: the text before a field's first NUL. |
| `baito::slice_bytes(bytes, at, count)` |  |  |  | slice_bytes: bytes from a position, raising when they run short. |
| `baito::field_value(bytes, at, kind, order)` |  |  |  | field_value: a field's value at a position, by its kind. |
| `baito::unpack(l, bytes, at?, order?)` |  |  |  | unpack: the record a layout's bytes hold at position `at`. Bytes that run short raise :decode_error. |

## bunsho

`bunsho` 0.1.0 · needs: fairu, kazu, moji, retsu

bunsho (文書) — Markdown as a first-class value: `.md` text parsed into one typed tree whose node kinds are the ones CommonMark, GitHub Flavored Markdown and mdast agree on, the tree rendered back in one canonical formatting, and the tree read by node, by kind and by section.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bunsho::ascii_punctuation` |  |  |  | ascii_punctuation: The characters a backslash escapes, and the ones that decide whether a run of `*` or `_` opens or closes. |
| `bunsho::punct?(c)` |  |  |  | punct?: Whether one character is ASCII punctuation. |
| `bunsho::space?(c)` |  |  |  | space?: Whether one character is white space, or nil, which stands for the edge of the text. |
| `bunsho::leading_spaces(s)` |  |  |  | leading_spaces: How many spaces open a line. |
| `bunsho::blank?(s)` |  |  |  | blank?: Whether a line holds nothing but spaces. |
| `bunsho::dropped(s, n)` |  |  |  | dropped: A line with its first `n` characters removed. |
| `bunsho::unindented(s, n)` |  |  |  | unindented: A line with up to `n` of its leading spaces removed. |
| `bunsho::run_of(cs, i, c)` |  |  |  | run_of: How many times the character `c` repeats from index `i` of the characters `cs`. |
| `bunsho::fence_open(s)` |  |  |  | fence_open: The opening fence a line holds, as `{char:, size:, indent:, info:}`, or nil: three or more backticks or tildes after at most three spaces, and for backticks an info string with no backtick. |
| `bunsho::fence_close?(s, open)` |  |  |  | fence_close?: Whether a line closes the fence `open`: at most three spaces, then at least as many of the same character, then nothing. |
| `bunsho::heading_open(s)` |  |  |  | heading_open: The ATX heading a line holds, as `{depth:, text:}`, or nil; a closing run of `#` after a space is dropped. |
| `bunsho::thematic_break?(s)` |  |  |  | thematic_break?: Whether a line is a thematic break: three or more of one of `-`, `*` or `_`, with spaces between them allowed. |
| `bunsho::setext_depth(s)` |  |  |  | setext_depth: The heading depth a line underlines the paragraph above it with: 1 for `=`, 2 for `-`, or nil. |
| `bunsho::quote_line?(s)` |  |  |  | quote_line?: Whether a line opens with a blockquote marker. |
| `bunsho::quote_content(s)` |  |  |  | quote_content: A blockquote line without its marker and the one space after it, with how many characters went. |
| `bunsho::item_open(s)` |  |  |  | item_open: The list item marker a line holds, as `{ordered:, mark:, start:, indent:, width:, empty:}`, or nil: `-`, `+` or `*`, or up to nine digits and `.` or `)`, then a space or the end of the line. `width` is the column the item's content starts at. |
| `bunsho::block_tags` |  |  |  | block_tags: The HTML tag names that open an HTML block which may interrupt a paragraph. |
| `bunsho::tag_name(t)` |  |  |  | tag_name: The tag name that follows `<` or `</` at the start of a text, lower case, or "". |
| `bunsho::html_open(s)` |  |  |  | html_open: The kind of HTML block a line opens: `:comment` (ends at `-->`), `:raw` (`script`, `pre`, `style` or `textarea`, ends at its closing tag), `:block` (a block tag, ends at a blank line), `:tag` (one whole tag alone on the line, ends at a blank line, never interrupts a paragraph), or nil. |
| `bunsho::html_end?(s, kind, start)` |  |  |  | html_end?: Whether a line ends an HTML block of `kind` that started with `start`. |
| `bunsho::cells_of(s)` |  |  |  | cells_of: The cells of a table row, split at each `\|` that no backslash escapes, the row's outer pipes dropped and each `\\\|` read as `\|`. |
| `bunsho::delimiter_row(s)` |  |  |  | delimiter_row: The column alignments a table's delimiter row states, one of `:left`, `:center`, `:right` or nil each, or nil when the line is not a delimiter row. |
| `bunsho::table_at?(lines, i)` |  |  |  | table_at?: Whether the line at `i` heads a table: it holds a `\|`, and the next line is a delimiter row with as many cells. |
| `bunsho::interrupts?(lines, i)` |  |  |  | interrupts?: Whether the line at `i` ends a paragraph above it: a blank line, a fence, an ATX heading, a thematic break, a blockquote, an HTML block that may interrupt, a list item with content (an ordered one only from 1), or a table. |
| `bunsho::open_paragraph?(texts)` |  |  |  | open_paragraph?: Whether a container's collected lines end inside a paragraph, so that the next line may continue it lazily: the last line is not blank and no fence is open. |
| `bunsho::source_lines(text)` |  |  |  | source_lines: The lines of a text, each numbered from 1 and starting at column 1. |
| `bunsho::start_of(l)` |  |  |  | start_of: The position of a line's first character that is not a space, as `[line, column]`. |
| `bunsho::fenced_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | fenced_at: The fenced code block that opens at line `i`, and the index after it: its closing fence, or the end of the container when it never closes. |
| `bunsho::indented_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | indented_at: The indented code block that opens at line `i`, and the index after it; trailing blank lines are left to the container. |
| `bunsho::last_filled(lines, i, stop)` |  |  |  | last_filled: The index after the last line from `i` before `stop` that is not blank. |
| `bunsho::html_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | html_at: The HTML block that opens at line `i`, and the index after it. |
| `bunsho::table_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | table_at: The table that opens at line `i`, and the index after it: rows run to a blank line or a line that opens another block. |
| `bunsho::table_row(cells, width, line)` |  |  |  | table_row: A row of a table on source line `line` from its cells, padded with empty cells or cut to `width`. |
| `bunsho::paragraph_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | paragraph_at: The paragraph that opens at line `i`, or the setext heading it is, and the index after it. |
| `bunsho::heading_node(depth, text, at)` |  |  |  | heading_node: A heading of `depth` whose text is `text`; its anchor is filled in once the whole document is read. |
| `bunsho::quote_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | quote_at: The blockquote that opens at line `i`, and the index after it: its lines without their markers, and lines that lazily continue a paragraph inside it. |
| `bunsho::same_list?(a, b)` |  |  |  | same_list?: Whether an item marker continues the list another marker started: both bullets with one character, or both ordered with one delimiter. |
| `bunsho::item_lines(lines, i)` |  |  |  | item_lines: The lines of the list item that opens at line `i`, its content shifted to column 1, and the index after them: lines indented to its width, blank lines, and lines that lazily continue its paragraph. |
| `bunsho::list_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | list_at: The list that opens at line `i`, and the index after it: items with one kind of marker, blank lines between them allowed, loose when a blank line separates two items or two blocks of one item. |
| `bunsho::block_at(lines, i)` |  |  |  | block_at: The block that opens at line `i` of a container's lines, and the index after it. |
| `bunsho::blocks_of(lines)` |  |  |  | blocks_of: The blocks of a container's lines, and whether a blank line separates two of them, as `{out:, gaps:}`. |
| `bunsho::front_of(text)` |  |  |  | front_of: The front matter a text opens with, as its `[key, value]` pairs (a line that is not `key: value` is `[line, nil]`), and the lines after it; nil pairs when the text has none. |
| `bunsho::parse(text, format?)` |  |  |  | parse: The tree of a Markdown text: a `:root` whose `front` holds its front matter pairs (nil without any) and whose children are its blocks. Every text parses; what is not markup is text. `format` is `:markdown`, the default; any other raises `:parse_error`. |
| `bunsho::plain_text(nodes)` |  |  |  | plain_text: The text a reader sees in a list of inline nodes, markup removed. |
| `bunsho::anchor_of(text)` |  |  |  | anchor_of: The anchor GitHub gives a heading's text: lower case, spaces as hyphens, ASCII punctuation and common symbols dropped, letters of every script kept. |
| `bunsho::with_anchors(tree)` |  |  |  | with_anchors: The tree with every heading's anchor filled in, in document order; a repeated anchor takes `-1`, `-2` and so on, as GitHub numbers them. |
| `bunsho::numbered_headings(node, names, next)` |  |  |  | numbered_headings: The tree with the headings met in document order from `next` given the anchors `names` in turn, and the index after the last one used, as `[next, tree]`. |
| `bunsho::next_of(text, subs, from)` |  |  |  | next_of: The first index at or after `from` where any of the texts `subs` occurs in `text`, or -1. |
| `bunsho::code_span_end(text, cs, i)` |  |  |  | code_span_end: The index after the code span whose backtick run starts at `i`, or -1 when no run of the same length closes it. |
| `bunsho::closing_run(text, cs, from, k)` |  |  |  | closing_run: The index after the first run of exactly `k` backticks at or after `from`, or -1. |
| `bunsho::bracket_close(text, cs, from, depth)` |  |  |  | bracket_close: The index of the `]` that closes the `[` before `from`, past escapes, code spans and nested brackets, or -1. |
| `bunsho::unescaped(t)` |  |  |  | unescaped: Text with each backslash before ASCII punctuation removed. |
| `bunsho::spaces_from(cs, i)` |  |  |  | spaces_from: The first index at or after `i` that is not a space or a line break. |
| `bunsho::destination(cs, p)` |  |  |  | destination: The target and title of a link whose `(` is at `p - 1`, and the index after its `)`, as `{target:, title:, end:}`, or nil when what follows is not a destination. |
| `bunsho::flanking(cs, i, k)` |  |  |  | flanking: Whether the run of `k` delimiters at `i` is left-flanking and right-flanking, as `[left, right]`. |
| `bunsho::can_open?(cs, i, k, d)` |  |  |  | can_open?: Whether the run of `k` copies of `d` at `i` may open emphasis: `*` when left-flanking, `_` when left-flanking and not inside a word. |
| `bunsho::can_close?(cs, i, k, d)` |  |  |  | can_close?: Whether the run of `k` copies of `d` at `i` may close emphasis: `*` when right-flanking, `_` when right-flanking and not inside a word. |
| `bunsho::closer(text, cs, from, d, use)` |  |  |  | closer: The index of the run of `d` that closes emphasis of size `use` opened before `from`: a run that may close, exactly one long for emphasis and at least two for strong, past escapes and code spans; -1 when none does. |
| `bunsho::tilde_closer(text, cs, from)` |  |  |  | tilde_closer: The index of the next run of exactly two `~` that may close a strikethrough, or -1. |
| `bunsho::alpha?(c)` |  |  |  | alpha?: Whether one character is an ASCII letter. |
| `bunsho::scheme_of(t)` |  |  |  | scheme_of: The URI scheme an autolink's text starts with, two to 32 letters, digits, `+`, `.` or `-` from a letter up to a `:`, or nil. |
| `bunsho::line_of(cs, i, line)` |  |  |  | line_of: The source line of index `i` of an inline text that starts on line `line`. |
| `bunsho::with_text(acc, t, next)` |  |  |  | with_text: An inline state with `t` added to the text not yet made a node, reading on from `next`. |
| `bunsho::flushed(acc)` |  |  |  | flushed: An inline state with its pending text made a `:text` node. |
| `bunsho::with_node(acc, node, next)` |  |  |  | with_node: An inline state with its pending text flushed, then `node` added, reading on from `next`. |
| `bunsho::specials` |  |  |  | specials: The characters that may begin inline markup; a run of any others is plain text. |
| `bunsho::code_step(text, cs, acc, i)` |  |  |  | code_step: The inline state after the backtick run at `i`: a code span when a run of its length closes it, else the run as text. |
| `bunsho::link_step(text, cs, line, acc, i)` |  |  |  | link_step: The inline state after the `[` or `![` at `i`: a link or an image when a bracketed label and a destination follow, else the character as text. |
| `bunsho::angle_step(text, cs, line, acc, i)` |  |  |  | angle_step: The inline state after the `<` at `i`: an autolink, an HTML comment, an HTML tag, or the character as text. |
| `bunsho::emphasis_step(text, cs, line, acc, i)` |  |  |  | emphasis_step: The inline state after the run of `*` or `_` at `i`: emphasis or strong when the run may open and a run that may close follows, else the run as text. |
| `bunsho::tilde_step(text, cs, line, acc, i)` |  |  |  | tilde_step: The inline state after the run of `~` at `i`: a strikethrough when the run is two long, may open, and a closing pair follows, else the run as text. |
| `bunsho::break_step(acc, i)` |  |  |  | break_step: The inline state after the line break at `i`: a hard break when two spaces end the line, else a soft break kept in the text; spaces before the break go either way. |
| `bunsho::inline_step(text, cs, n, line, acc, i)` |  |  |  | inline_step: The inline state after the markup, or the run of plain text, that starts at index `i`. |
| `bunsho::inlines(text, line)` |  |  |  | inlines: The inline nodes of a block's text, which starts on source line `line`: text, emphasis, strong, strikethrough, code spans, hard breaks, links, images, autolinks and inline HTML. |
| `bunsho::blank_line` |  |  |  | blank_line: The separator between two blocks: one empty line. |
| `bunsho::line_start_escaped(l)` |  |  |  | line_start_escaped: One line of rendered paragraph text with a backslash put where its start would otherwise open a block: a heading, a blockquote, a list item, or a setext underline. |
| `bunsho::char_escaped(c, before, after, where)` |  |  |  | char_escaped: One character of a text node as canonical Markdown writes it, given the characters before and after it (nil at the node's edge) and where it is: `:paragraph`, `:heading`, `:cell` or `:label`. |
| `bunsho::text_escaped(t, where, starts_line)` |  |  |  | text_escaped: A text node as canonical Markdown writes it: each character escaped where it would otherwise be read as markup, and in a paragraph each line start escaped where it would open a block. `starts_line` says whether the node begins a line. |
| `bunsho::code_span(t, where)` |  |  |  | code_span: Code as a code span: fenced by one more backtick than its longest run, with a space inside each end when it starts or ends with a backtick or a space; inside a table cell each `\|` is escaped. |
| `bunsho::target_text(t)` |  |  |  | target_text: A link destination as canonical Markdown writes it: bare, or in angle brackets when it holds a space, a bracket or an unbalanced parenthesis. |
| `bunsho::title_text(t)` |  |  |  | title_text: A link title as canonical Markdown writes it after the destination, or "" when there is none. |
| `bunsho::render_inline(node, where, starts_line)` |  |  |  | render_inline: One inline node as canonical Markdown, `where` it is, and whether it begins a line. |
| `bunsho::render_inlines(nodes, where)` |  |  |  | render_inlines: Inline nodes as canonical Markdown, `where` they are. |
| `bunsho::heading_text(node)` |  |  |  | heading_text: A heading as canonical Markdown: its depth in `#`, then its text on one line, with a backslash before a closing run of `#` that would otherwise be dropped. |
| `bunsho::fence_for(t)` |  |  |  | fence_for: The fence a code block is written with: one more backtick than the longest run in its text, and never fewer than three. |
| `bunsho::indented_lines(t, w)` |  |  |  | indented_lines: Text with every line after the first indented by `w` spaces, an empty line kept empty. |
| `bunsho::list_text(node, other)` |  |  |  | list_text: A list as canonical Markdown: `-` bullets or `1.` numbers from its start (`*` and `1)` when `other`, so that two lists in a row stay two), each item's blocks indented under its marker, a blank line between items and blocks when the list is loose. |
| `bunsho::table_text(node)` |  |  |  | table_text: A table as canonical Markdown: the header row, the delimiter row from the alignments, then the rows, each cell between `\| ` and ` \|`. |
| `bunsho::render_block(node, other)` |  |  |  | render_block: One block as canonical Markdown, without a line break after it; `other` asks a list for its other markers. |
| `bunsho::render_blocks(nodes, sep)` |  |  |  | render_blocks: Blocks as canonical Markdown, joined by `sep`; a list that follows a list of its kind takes the other markers, so the two stay two lists. |
| `bunsho::render(tree, format?)` |  |  |  | render: The canonical Markdown of a tree: its front matter between `---` lines, then its blocks with one empty line between them, and a line break at the end; "" for an empty tree. `format` is `:markdown`, the default; any other raises `:render_error`. |
| `bunsho::walk(tree)` |  |  |  | walk: Every node under a tree, in document order, each before its children. |
| `bunsho::extract(tree, kinds)` |  |  |  | extract: Every node of `kinds` in a tree, in document order; `kinds` is one kind (`:heading`, `:link`, `:code`, `:fact`, `:table` or any other) or a list of them, so the links and images a link gate reads come in one pass. |
| `bunsho::section(tree, anchor)` |  |  |  | section: The section of a tree under the top-level heading whose anchor is `anchor`: the blocks after it up to the next heading of its depth or above, without the heading; nil when no top-level heading has that anchor. |
| `bunsho::map_nodes(tree, f)` |  |  |  | map_nodes: The tree with `f` applied to every node, children first, the root last. |
| `bunsho::outside?(t)` |  |  |  | outside?: Whether a link target leaves the project: a URL with a scheme, a mail address, an anchor in the same page, or an absolute path. |
| `bunsho::rebase(tree, from, to)` |  |  |  | rebase: The tree moved from directory `from` to directory `to`, both relative to one root: every relative link and image target rewritten to reach the same file, its anchor kept. |
| `bunsho::path_join_rel(dir, target)` |  |  |  | path_join_rel: A relative path written in directory `dir`, as a path from the root. |
| `bunsho::bare(tree)` |  |  |  | bare: The tree without the source positions of its nodes, for comparing two trees by what they say. |
| `bunsho::law_failures(pages)` |  |  |  | law_failures: The pages whose text breaks one of the two laws, each as `[name, law]`: rendering is a fixed point (`render(parse(render(t))) == render(t)`), and rendering loses no structure (`parse(render(parse(x)))` is `parse(x)`, positions aside). |
| `bunsho::hand_corpus` |  |  |  | hand_corpus: The hard cases both laws are held to, as [name, text] pages: nested and unclosed fences, lists in lists and two lists in a row, inline markup with its escapes, GFM tables, and front matter. |

## burauza

`burauza` 0.1.0 · needs: gamen, kyohi, moji, retsu

burauza (ブラウザ) — the browser: what a blue page needs from the browser, generated.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `burauza::install_seam` |  |  |  | install_seam: registers the service worker, and does nothing where a browser has none. |
| `burauza::ink_seam` |  |  |  | ink_seam: draws what a pointer traces on a canvas and sends the strokes when asked. |
| `burauza::upload_seam` |  |  |  | upload_seam: sends a chosen or dropped file as the raw body of one request. |
| `burauza::place_seam` |  |  |  | place_seam: places marks on a page, moves and sizes them, and sends them as fractions of the page. |
| `burauza::engine_seam` |  |  |  | engine_seam: runs blue in the page: fetches the engine and the program's sources a mount names, runs the program with no import, and puts the section it renders in the mount and its receipt beside it. |
| `burauza::rows` |  |  |  | rows: the closed table of browser code: each name with its text. |
| `burauza::seam_names` |  |  |  | seam_names: The closed table of browser code, by name. |
| `burauza::seam(name)` |  |  |  | seam: The browser code of one row of the table, refusing a name it does not hold. |
| `burauza::loader(names)` |  |  |  | loader: The page's one script: the install row and the rows asked for, in table order. |
| `burauza::background_of(theme)` |  |  |  | background_of: a theme's background as hex. |
| `burauza::manifest(spec, theme)` |  |  |  | manifest: A web app manifest as JSON text, its colours from the theme. |
| `burauza::worker(spec)` |  |  |  | worker: The service worker's text. Pages come from the network and fall back to the offline page; the shell is cached by version; anything else, and every write, never touches the cache, so a document is not left behind in a browser. |
| `burauza::page_policy(base)` |  |  |  | page_policy: The page security policy an installable page needs on top of a base. |
| `burauza::engine_policy(base)` |  |  |  | engine_policy: The page security policy a page that runs the engine needs on top of a base: its own script, its own fetches, and wasm compiled from them. |
| `burauza::spec_fixture` |  |  |  | spec_fixture: a worker declaration for the tests. |

chozou (貯蔵, storage) — what a service keeps and reads back.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| `chozou::kiibariu::password(file)` |  |  |  | password: the password a password file holds, or nil when there is no file. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::connect(opts)` |  |  | `B0083` | connect: a connection opened from a settings record. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::close(c)` |  |  | `B0083` | close: close a connection. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::get_key(c, key)` |  |  | `B0083` | get_key: the text stored at a key, or nil. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::ttl_ok?(ttl_ms)` |  |  |  | ttl_ok?: whether a TTL is nil or a positive number of milliseconds. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::set_command(key, value, ttl_ms)` |  |  |  | set_command: the SET command for a value and a TTL in milliseconds (nil: none). |
| `chozou::kiibariu::set_key(c, key, value, ttl_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | set_key: store text at a key, with a TTL in milliseconds or nil. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::del(c, keys)` |  |  | `B0083` | del: remove keys; how many existed. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::pttl(c, key)` |  |  | `B0083` | pttl: the milliseconds a key has left; -1 when it never expires, nil when the key is absent. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::publish(c, channel, message)` |  |  | `B0083` | publish: send a message on a channel; how many subscribers got it. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::subscribe(c, channels)` |  |  | `B0083` | subscribe: turn a connection into a subscriber of channels. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::pushed(reply)` |  |  |  | pushed: a pushed pub/sub reply as {channel:, payload:}, nil for any other reply. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::next_message(c, wait_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | next_message: {channel:, payload:} for the next message, or nil when none came in time. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::scan_step(c, cursor, pattern, batch)` |  |  | `B0083` | scan_step: one SCAN call; [next cursor, keys]. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::scan_each(c, pattern, batch, f)` |  |  |  | scan_each: call f with each batch of keys matching a pattern; how many keys were seen. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::scan(c, pattern, batch)` |  |  |  | scan: every key matching a pattern. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::versioned_key(namespace, version_hash, entity, id)` |  |  |  | versioned_key: a cache key that carries the model version's hash. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::prefix(namespace, version_hash, entity)` |  |  |  | prefix: the part of a versioned key before the id. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::field_types` |  |  |  | field_types: the field types a strict schema names. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::of_type?(type, v)` |  |  |  | of_type?: whether a value is of a schema field type, judged by teeburu's strict model check. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::check(schema, rec)` |  |  |  | check: the record when it holds to a strict schema (chozou::teeburu::kv_check: every field present and typed, no field the schema does not declare unless `strict: false`), else one :bad_value raise naming every finding. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::stored(schema, rec)` |  |  |  | stored: a record checked against a schema and written as JSON. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::loaded(schema, text)` |  |  |  | loaded: stored JSON read back and checked against a schema; nil stays nil. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::put(c, schema, key, rec, ttl_ms)` |  |  |  | put: check a record against a schema and store it as JSON. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::fetch(c, schema, key)` |  |  |  | fetch: the record at a key, checked against a schema; nil when absent. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::plan_ok(plan)` |  |  |  | plan_ok: an upgrade plan with two different key prefixes, else a :bad_option raise. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::kept_ttl(ttl)` |  |  |  | kept_ttl: the TTL a rewrite keeps: a positive PTTL, else nil for no expiry. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::rewrite_commands(new_key, text, ttl, old_key)` |  |  |  | rewrite_commands: the SET of the upgraded record (keeping the TTL) and the DEL of the old key. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::upgraded(plan, text)` |  |  |  | upgraded: an old entry's JSON checked, transformed and checked against the new schema. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::read_upgrading(c, plan, id)` |  |  | `B0083` | read_upgrading: the record for an id under the new schema, upgrading an old entry on the way. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::batch_rewrite(c, plan, keys)` |  |  | `B0083` | batch_rewrite: one SCAN batch of old keys rewritten under the new prefix; how many moved. |
| `chozou::kiibariu::upgrade_eager(c, plan, batch)` |  |  |  | upgrade_eager: rewrite every old entry to the new schema in SCAN batches, keeping each TTL; how many moved. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::nats_url(url)` |  |  |  | nats_url: a nats:// url, else a :bad_option raise. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::api(stream, verb)` |  |  |  | api: the JetStream API subject for a stream verb. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::name_ok?(name)` |  |  |  | name_ok?: whether a stream, consumer or table name has no dot, space or wildcard. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::need_name(what, name)` |  |  |  | need_name: a name that is ok, else a :bad_option raise naming what it names. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::answer(text)` |  |  |  | answer: a JetStream API reply read as a record; an error reply raises :jetstream. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::stream_config(name, subjects)` |  |  |  | stream_config: the JetStream config of a file-backed stream over subjects. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::ensure_stream(c, name, subjects)` |  |  |  | ensure_stream: declare a stream over subjects, created when absent; its info. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::declared(c, name, body)` |  |  | `B0083` | declared: create a stream, or update it when its name is already in use. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::publish(c, subject, payload, wait_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | publish: publish to a stream's subject and wait for its ack: {stream:, seq:}. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::consumer_config(stream, name, filter, ack_wait_ms)` |  |  |  | consumer_config: the config of a durable consumer with explicit acks. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::durable(c, stream, name, filter, ack_wait_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | durable: declare a durable pull consumer with explicit acks on a stream. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::deliveries(reply)` |  |  |  | deliveries: how many times JetStream has delivered a message, read from its ack subject. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::delivered(m)` |  |  |  | delivered: a pulled message with its delivery count. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::pull_request(batch, wait_ms)` |  |  |  | pull_request: the body of a pull for a batch, expiring after wait_ms. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::gather(sub, batch, wait_ms, got)` |  |  | `B0083` | gather: messages read from a pull's inbox until the batch is full, a status arrives or time runs out. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::fetch(c, stream, name, batch, wait_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | fetch: up to batch messages from a durable consumer, waiting at most wait_ms. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::ack(c, m)` |  |  | `B0083` | ack: tell the stream a message is done. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::nak(c, m, delay_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | nak: tell the stream to deliver a message again, after delay_ms when it is not nil. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::outbox_ddl(table)` |  |  |  | outbox_ddl: the table the outbox writes to. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::outbox_insert(table)` |  |  |  | outbox_insert: the statement that records one event in the outbox. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::in_transaction(pg, f)` |  |  | `B0083` | in_transaction: run f between BEGIN and COMMIT, rolling back and raising again when it raises. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::write_with_outbox(pg, table, change, events)` |  |  | `B0083` | write_with_outbox: run a change and record its events in the same transaction; the change's rows. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::drain_select(table, batch)` |  |  |  | drain_select: the statement that locks the next batch of outbox events. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::drain_delete(table, ids)` |  |  |  | drain_delete: the statement that removes drained events by id. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::drain(pg, js, table, batch, wait_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | drain: publish up to batch outbox events to the stream, each acked, and delete them; how many moved. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::settle(c, handler, m)` |  |  |  | settle: run the handler on one message and ack it; nak and raise again when the handler raises. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::work(url, stream, name, handler, batch, wait_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | work: a worker's loop: fetch, run the handler on each message, ack it; a raise naks and ends the worker. |
| `chozou::sutoriimu::consumer(url, stream, name, handler, opts)` |  |  |  | consumer: a supervisor running workers for a durable consumer; each worker restarted when its handler raises. |
| `chozou::teeburu::quoted(ident)` |  |  |  | quoted: an identifier as a Postgres quoted identifier, each inner double quote doubled. |
| `chozou::teeburu::enabled?(settings)` |  |  |  | enabled?: whether the settings name a database, that is a database.url that is set and not empty. |
| `chozou::teeburu::open(settings)` |  |  | `B0083` | open: a Postgres connection from the settings' database.url, its search path set to database.schema; settings with no database raise :no_database. |
| `chozou::teeburu::insert_statement(table, row)` |  |  |  | insert_statement: the parameterised INSERT for one row, as the SQL and its parameters in column order; the statement returns the row it writes. |
| `chozou::teeburu::insert(conn, table, row)` |  |  | `B0083` | insert: write one row into a table over a connection and return the row as stored; with no connection the runtime refuses it. |
| `chozou::teeburu::rows(conn, sql, params)` |  |  | `B0083` | rows: the rows a parameterised query returns over a connection; with no connection the runtime refuses it. |
| `chozou::teeburu::latest_statement(table, n)` |  |  |  | latest_statement: the SELECT for the n newest rows of a table, newest id first. |
| `chozou::teeburu::latest(conn, table, n)` |  |  |  | latest: the n newest rows of a table over a connection, newest id first; with no connection the runtime refuses it. |
| `chozou::teeburu::types` |  |  |  | types lists what a model field may be declared as. |
| `chozou::teeburu::pg_type(t)` |  |  |  | pg_type: the Postgres column type a field type is stored as. |
| `chozou::teeburu::widening?(from, to)` |  |  |  | widening?: whether a retype keeps every value: a whole number into a wider number or into text, a uuid into text. |
| `chozou::teeburu::field_of(spec)` |  |  |  | field_of: a field declaration as a record: a bare type becomes `{type:}`, and nullable unless `null: false`. |
| `chozou::teeburu::entity_whys(name, e)` |  |  |  | entity_whys: why one entity declaration cannot stand: a key that is not a field, an unknown type, an allow other than :destructive. |
| `chozou::teeburu::model(name, spec)` |  |  |  | model: a data model as a versioned value: entities with a key, typed fields, indexes and relations, and the intent a diff cannot see (`renamed_from`, `split_from`, `merged_from`, and `drop` entries with `allow: :destructive`); its hash names the version. |
| `chozou::teeburu::version_hash(m)` |  |  |  | version_hash: sixteen hex characters of the BLAKE3 of a model's canonical JSON, the hash a cache key and a lock pin carry. |
| `chozou::teeburu::declared_drop(to, what)` |  |  |  | declared_drop: the drop entry the new model declares for what a step removes, or nil. |
| `chozou::teeburu::drop_step(to, what)` |  |  |  | drop_step: a drop of what a new version removes, carrying the allow its drop entry declares. |
| `chozou::teeburu::key_type(m, entity)` |  |  |  | key_type: the type of an entity's key in a model, which a relation's column takes. |
| `chozou::teeburu::relation_column(rel)` |  |  |  | relation_column: the column a relation is stored in. |
| `chozou::teeburu::field_steps(name, old, new, to)` |  |  |  | field_steps: the steps between one entity's old and new declaration: renames, retypes, added and dropped fields, indexes and relations. |
| `chozou::teeburu::sources_of(spec)` |  |  |  | sources_of: the entities a new entity takes its data from: what it was renamed, split or merged from. |
| `chozou::teeburu::evolve(from, to)` |  |  |  | evolve: the typed steps from one model version to the next: add_entity, add_field, rename, retype, add_index, split, merge and drop, entities in name order; a version that does not move forward is refused. |
| `chozou::teeburu::destructive?(s)` |  |  |  | destructive?: whether a step can lose data: every drop, and a retype that narrows. |
| `chozou::teeburu::where_of(s)` |  |  |  | where_of: the place a step acts on, as text. |
| `chozou::teeburu::data_add?(s)` |  |  |  | data_add?: whether a step adds a field or an entity that a dropped one might really be, renamed. |
| `chozou::teeburu::meets?(drop, add)` |  |  |  | meets?: whether an undeclared drop and an add stand in the same place: a field dropped and one added in the same entity, or an entity dropped while another is added. |
| `chozou::teeburu::judge(steps)` |  |  |  | judge: the steps that stand and the refusals, each scoped to its step: a drop with no allow met by an add with no declared rename refuses both, and any other destructive step without `allow: :destructive` is refused alone. |
| `chozou::teeburu::ident(x)` |  |  |  | ident: a Postgres identifier, quoted. |
| `chozou::teeburu::sql_value(v)` |  |  |  | sql_value: a value as a Postgres literal, through kueri's expression renderer. |
| `chozou::teeburu::column_sql(name, rec)` |  |  |  | column_sql: one column's definition: its name, its Postgres type, NOT NULL unless nullable, and a constant default. |
| `chozou::teeburu::index_name(entity, cols)` |  |  |  | index_name: the name an index on some columns of an entity takes. |
| `chozou::teeburu::change(id, step, transaction, statements, lock_timeout)` |  |  |  | change: one migration change: its id, the step it lowers, whether it runs inside a transaction, and its statements, the lock timeout first. |
| `chozou::teeburu::keyset_loop(table, key, key_sql_type, batch, statement)` |  |  |  | keyset_loop: a batched keyset walk over a table in key order, each batch committed on its own, running a statement over that batch's keys. |
| `chozou::teeburu::pg_options(opts)` |  |  |  | pg_options: the lowering's options with their defaults: a five-second lock timeout and batches of a thousand rows. |
| `chozou::teeburu::step_id(to, s, n)` |  |  |  | step_id: the changeset id of a step: model, version, position, step and place. |
| `chozou::teeburu::pg_create(s, from, to, o, base)` |  |  |  | pg_create: the changes that create a new entity's table with its indexes and foreign keys, then copy each source's rows in keyset batches when the entity is split or merged from others. |
| `chozou::teeburu::pg_add_field(s, to, o, base)` |  |  |  | pg_add_field: the changes that add a column: one instant ADD COLUMN for a constant default; for a relation the foreign key NOT VALID then VALIDATE; for a backfill the column, a keyset backfill and, when NOT NULL, a CHECK NOT VALID, VALIDATE, SET NOT NULL; a NOT NULL column with neither default nor backfill is refused. |
| `chozou::teeburu::pg_step(s, from, to, o, n)` |  |  |  | pg_step: one judged step lowered to its Postgres changes, the fastest safe form for each: CONCURRENTLY outside a transaction, NOT VALID then VALIDATE, a constant default with no rewrite, a batched keyset backfill, lock_timeout on every change; a step Postgres cannot carry safely is refused by name. |
| `chozou::teeburu::postgres(from, to, opts)` |  |  |  | postgres: the Postgres changes for the steps the judge lets stand between two versions, in step order, with the judge's refusals beside them; a refused step never reaches the database. |
| `chozou::teeburu::keifu(changes)` |  |  |  | keifu: changes as a keifu changelog, the Liquibase XML shinka applies in process: one changeSet per change, each statement its own sql element, and runInTransaction false where a change must run outside a transaction. |
| `chozou::teeburu::kv_entity(name, ent)` |  |  |  | kv_entity: one strict Valkey entity checked and given its defaults: JSON encoding, upgrade on read, strict; indexes, relations and an encoding not rendered yet are refused by name. |
| `chozou::teeburu::kv_model(name, spec)` |  |  |  | kv_model: a strict Valkey model: a versioned model whose entities each carry an encoding, a TTL, an upgrade mode and strictness, evolved by the same steps as a table. |
| `chozou::teeburu::holds?(type, v)` |  |  |  | holds?: whether a stored value is of a field type, as JSON carries it. |
| `chozou::teeburu::kv_findings(entity, rec)` |  |  |  | kv_findings: every way a stored record breaks a strict entity: not a record, a missing field, a value of the wrong type, and, unless the entity is loose, a field it does not declare. |
| `chozou::teeburu::kv_check(entity, rec)` |  |  |  | kv_check: the record when it holds to a strict entity, else one :bad_value raise naming every finding. |
| `chozou::teeburu::kv_upgrade(steps, entity)` |  |  |  | kv_upgrade: what an old stored record of an entity becomes under the next version, from the judged steps: renamed fields moved, added fields given their default, fields widened to text converted, dropped fields removed; a step Valkey cannot carry is refused by name. |

## fairu

`fairu` 0.1.1 · needs: moji, retsu

fairu (ファイル) — files: paths and the files they name.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fairu::absolute(path)` |  |  |  | absolute: the path made absolute against the current directory. |
| `fairu::with_temp_dir(label, f)` |  |  |  | with_temp_dir: `f` called with a new, empty directory under TMPDIR named after `label`; the directory is removed when `f` returns or raises, and the answer is what `f` answers. For a directory one block uses; `temp_dir(label)` alone keeps it until the run ends. |
| `fairu::on_blue_path(rel)` |  |  |  | on_blue_path: every existing path `rel` names below a bidama root: each BLUE_PATH entry, then `bidamas/` and `../../bidamas/` under the current directory, then the current directory. Where a bidama's test finds its fixtures, wherever blue runs it. |
| `fairu::fixture(package, name)` |  |  |  | fixture: the text of a bidama's fixture, `<package>/fixtures/<name>` found on the bidama roots; a fixture no root holds is refused by its path. |
| `fairu::home` |  |  |  | home: the home directory of the user. |
| `fairu::parts(path)` |  |  |  | parts: the path split into its parts. |
| `fairu::stem(path)` |  |  |  | stem: the name without its final extension. |
| `fairu::normalize(path)` |  |  |  | normalize: the path with each `.` part, each empty part and each `dir/..` pair removed; a `..` that climbs above where the path starts is kept, and a leading `/` stays. |
| `fairu::relative(path, base)` |  |  |  | relative: the path to `path` as a file in the directory `base` writes it, both paths relative to one root. |
| `fairu::write_tree(root, fs)` |  |  |  | write_tree: write each [path, text] pair of `fs` under `root`, making the directories each needs, and answer the paths written. |

## fuuin

`fuuin` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, moji, retsu, shomei

fuuin (封印) — k-of-n threshold signing: a group whose key no one share holds, signed by any k of its n shares in two rounds, RFC 9591 FROST over Ed25519.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `fuuin::generate(kind, opts)` |  |  | `B0083` | generate: a new threshold group from secure entropy, `max` shares of which any `min` sign together: {group:, key:, min:, max:, shares:}. :threshold is the one kind; another kind, or a threshold outside 2 <= min <= max, raises :bad_option. |
| `fuuin::offer(share, data)` |  |  | `B0083` | offer: one share's first round toward signing data, {nonces:, commitment:}. The commitment goes to whoever combines the signature; the nonces stay with the share and sign once. |
| `fuuin::sign(data, share, opts)` |  |  | `B0083` | sign: one share's signature share over data, {participant:, hex:}, from the nonces its offer kept and the commitments of the signers, at least the group's k with its own among them. The nonces are spent, so a second sign with them raises :refused. |
| `fuuin::combine(parts, opts)` |  |  | `B0083` | combine: the group's Ed25519 signature over data, in hex, from at least k signature shares and the commitments they signed for, checked under the group's key before it is answered. Fewer than k raises :refused, and a share that does not verify raises :tampered naming its participant. |

| `gitto::merge(repo, ref, opts?)` |  |  |  |  |
| `gitto::current_branch(repo)` |  |  |  |  |
| `gitto::push(repo, opts?)` |  |  |  |  |
| `gitto::fixture(name)` |  |  |  |  |
| `gitto::save(repo, name, text, message)` |  |  |  |  |

## gurafu

`gurafu` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, moji, ran, retsu, shuugou

gurafu (グラフ) — directed graphs as values: build them, ask them, order them.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gurafu::edge_ok(e)` |  |  |  | edge_ok: an edge when it is a pair of nodes, else a :bad_option raise. |
| `gurafu::graph(edges, opts)` |  |  |  | graph: a graph from edge pairs; `nodes:` adds nodes with no edge, and `directed: false` adds each edge both ways. |
| `gurafu::nodes(g)` |  |  |  | nodes: every node of a graph, sorted. |
| `gurafu::neighbors(g, node)` |  |  |  | neighbors: the nodes one edge away from a node, sorted; [] for a node the graph does not hold. |
| `gurafu::edges(g)` |  |  |  | edges: every edge of a graph as a [from, to] pair, sorted. |
| `gurafu::has_edge?(g, from, to)` |  |  |  | has_edge?: whether a graph holds the edge from one node to another. |
| `gurafu::add_node(g, node)` |  |  |  | add_node: a graph that also holds a node. |
| `gurafu::add_edge(g, from, to)` |  |  |  | add_edge: a graph that also holds the edge from one node to another, adding both nodes. |
| `gurafu::remove_edge(g, from, to)` |  |  |  | remove_edge: a graph without one edge; its nodes stay. |
| `gurafu::remove_node(g, node)` |  |  |  | remove_node: a graph without a node and every edge at it. |
| `gurafu::reverse(g)` |  | yes |  | reverse: a graph with every edge turned around. |
| `gurafu::subgraph(g, keep)` |  |  |  | subgraph: a graph restricted to some nodes and the edges between them. |
| `gurafu::leaves(g)` |  |  |  | leaves: the nodes no edge leaves, sorted. |
| `gurafu::spread(g, frontier, seen)` |  |  |  | spread: the nodes found by following edges out of a frontier, given those already seen. |
| `gurafu::reachable(g, start)` |  |  |  | reachable: every node a start node reaches by one edge or more, sorted; the start is in it only when it lies on a cycle. |
| `gurafu::in_degrees(g)` |  |  |  | in_degrees: for each node, how many edges reach it. |
| `gurafu::peel(g, indeg, ready, done)` |  |  |  | peel: the layers of a graph from a ready layer on, each node placed once every edge into it is placed. |
| `gurafu::layers(g)` |  |  |  | layers: the acyclic part of a graph in layers: sources first, then each node one layer past its deepest upstream, each layer sorted. |
| `gurafu::back_walk(back, path, seen)` |  |  |  | back_walk: a cycle found by following predecessors among nodes that all have one, from the path walked so far. |
| `gurafu::cycle(g)` |  | yes |  | cycle: one cycle of a graph as its nodes in edge order, starting at the smallest, or nil when the graph has none. |
| `gurafu::cycle_text(c)` |  |  |  | cycle_text: a cycle written as its path back to where it starts. |
| `gurafu::topo(g)` |  |  |  | topo: every node ordered so each edge points forward, in layers from the sources, each layer sorted; a cycle is refused with :refused, naming it. |
| `gurafu::depth(g, node)` |  |  |  | depth: how many edges lie on the longest path from a source to a node; a cycle is refused as topo refuses it. |
| `gurafu::random_edges(seed, nodes_count, n, forward)` |  |  |  | random_edges: n edges over nodes 0 to size - 1 drawn from a seed; with `forward: true` each edge points from a smaller node to a larger one. |
| `gurafu::respects?(g, order)` |  |  |  | respects?: whether an order puts the start of every edge of a graph before its end. |
| `gurafu::real_cycle?(g, c)` |  |  |  | real_cycle?: whether a list of nodes is a cycle of a graph: each one has an edge to the next, and the last to the first. |
| `gurafu::acyclic?(g)` |  |  |  | acyclic?: whether no node of a graph reaches itself, judged by reachable alone. |

## hadome

`hadome` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, moji, retsu

hadome (歯止め) — a baseline ratchet: recorded debt may shrink or hold, never grow.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hadome::row(kind, key, size)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::measurement(key, size)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::sized(kind, rest)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::parse_line(line)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::parse(text)` |  |  |  | parse: a baseline file to rows. |
| `hadome::recorded(rows, kind, key)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::judge(rows, kind, key, measured)` |  |  |  | judge: whether a measure grew past its recorded size. |
| `hadome::judge_within(rows, kind, key, measured, permille)` |  |  |  | judge_within: judge, holding a measure up to permille thousandths past its row. |
| `hadome::failure(rows, kind, m, permille)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::failures(rows, kind, measurements)` |  |  |  | failures: every key that grew or is new. |
| `hadome::failures_within(rows, kind, measurements, permille)` |  |  |  | failures_within: every key that is new or grew past its row by more than permille thousandths. |
| `hadome::keys_of(rows, kind)` |  |  |  |  |
| `hadome::orphans(rows, kind, live)` |  |  |  | orphans: recorded keys that no longer exist. |
| `hadome::shrunk(rows, kind, measurements)` |  |  |  | shrunk: keys that fell below their record and can be tightened. |
| `hadome::render(kind, measurements)` |  |  |  | render: measurements as a baseline file, stable order. |
| `hadome::receipted(old, receipt, rows)` |  |  |  | receipted: a baseline's text, keeping the old text's comment lines, then the receipt line, then rows. |
| `hadome::example_baseline` |  |  |  |  |

## happa

`happa` 0.1.1 · needs: gurafu, kousei, kyohi, moji, retsu

happa (葉っぱ) — a template's leaves: their declaration, split, graph, change, schedule and verdicts, as pure decisions.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `happa::name_end(cs, i)` |  |  |  | name_end: the first index at or after i that holds a dot or an opening bracket, or the end. |
| `happa::quoted(a, cs, i, buf)` |  |  |  | quoted: a quoted index key read from i to its closing quote, with the index after the closing bracket. |
| `happa::bare(a, cs, i)` |  |  |  | bare: an unquoted index key read from i to its closing bracket, with the index after it. |
| `happa::steps_from(a, cs, i, acc)` |  |  |  | steps_from: the steps of an address from index i on, each a name and its index key or nil. |
| `happa::steps(address)` |  |  |  | steps: an address read into its steps; a malformed address raises :parse_error, never a guess. |
| `happa::key(address)` |  |  |  | key: a resource address's leaf key: its first index key, else its top module, else the address itself. |
| `happa::strip_index(address)` |  |  |  | strip_index: an address with every index key dropped. |
| `happa::data?(address)` |  |  |  | data?: whether an address names a data source, at the top or inside a module. |
| `happa::key_id(k)` |  |  |  | key_id: text that orders leaf keys as the operator does: keyed, then module, then address, then by value. |
| `happa::managed(addresses)` |  |  |  | managed: the addresses that are not data sources. |
| `happa::classify(addresses, p95_ms, budget_ms)` |  |  |  | classify: whether a state is fat: more than one leaf key, or a cycle p95 over its budget. |
| `happa::root_of(parent, id)` |  |  |  | root_of: the root a key's chain of parents ends at. |
| `happa::joined(parent, edge)` |  |  |  | joined: the parent map after one reference edge joins two leaves, the smaller key becoming the root. |
| `happa::split(addresses, refs)` |  | yes |  | split: the leaves of a state: its managed addresses grouped by leaf key, then joined along reference edges between keys; a repeated address is refused. |
| `happa::le64(n)` |  |  |  | le64: a count as its eight little-endian bytes. |
| `happa::multiset_hash(addresses)` |  |  |  | multiset_hash: the BLAKE3 of a list of addresses taken as a multiset: the count, then each address sorted with its length, so order never matters and no two lists concatenate alike. |
| `happa::bijection(before, leaves)` |  |  |  | bijection: whether the leaves hold exactly the managed addresses of the state before the split: equal multiset hashes and no address in two leaves. |
| `happa::alnum?(c)` |  |  |  | alnum?: whether a character is an ASCII letter or digit. |
| `happa::slug(raw)` |  |  |  | slug: a key as a DNS label part: ASCII letters and digits lowercased, every other run one dash, no dash at either end, and "leaf" when nothing is left. |
| `happa::suffix(k)` |  |  |  | suffix: eight hex characters of the BLAKE3 of a key's kind and value, that tell apart keys whose slugs collide. |
| `happa::name_cap` |  |  |  | name_cap: the longest object name a leaf may have, a DNS label's 63. |
| `happa::names(parent, keys)` |  |  |  | names: the object name of each leaf key under a parent, in order: parent and slug, with a hash suffix when two keys share a slug or the name is too long. |
| `happa::name(parent, k, keys)` |  |  |  | name: the object name of one leaf key among the keys of its parent. |
| `happa::canonical(v)` |  |  |  | canonical: a value as canonical JSON text: object keys sorted, a comma after every member, so equal values give equal text. |
| `happa::digest(catalog, own, refs)` |  |  |  | digest: the content hash of a leaf's inputs: the catalog's variables, the leaf's own and its references, each canonical, nil marking one absent. |
| `happa::changed(current, recorded)` |  |  |  | changed: the leaves whose recorded digest differs from the current one, sorted; a leaf with no recorded digest has not changed. |
| `happa::graph(leaves, refs)` |  |  |  | graph: the leaf graph from leaf names and [leaf, upstream] references, each edge pointing from the upstream to the leaf; a cycle is refused with :refused, naming the cycle and every leaf it holds. |
| `happa::order(g)` |  |  |  | order: the apply order of a leaf graph, every leaf after its upstreams, in layers, each sorted by name. |
| `happa::destroy_order(g)` |  |  |  | destroy_order: the reverse of the apply order: dependents before their upstreams. |
| `happa::upstreams(g, leaf)` |  |  |  | upstreams: the leaves a leaf reads from, sorted. |
| `happa::dependents(g, held)` |  |  |  | dependents: every leaf that reads, directly or through others, from any of some leaves, sorted. |
| `happa::blocked(ups, ready, applying)` |  |  |  | blocked: which upstreams hold a leaf back: those not ready, where a mocked output lets a plan through but never an apply; sorted, each named once. |
| `happa::classes` |  |  |  | classes: the work classes in priority order: an approved apply, a changed leaf's plan, a leaf whose upstream became ready, a retry, a drift plan. |
| `happa::ranked(candidates, priority)` |  |  |  | ranked: candidates in the order they are offered a slot: by class, drift oldest first, then upstreams first by depth, then by name; with priority off, by arrival then name. |
| `happa::drift_slots(scope_cap, share_permille)` |  |  |  | drift_slots: how many of a scope's slots drift may hold: its share of the cap, at least one and, past a cap of one, never all; none when the share is zero. |
| `happa::bump(m, k)` |  |  |  | bump: a count map with one key raised by one. |
| `happa::fits?(c, limits, f)` |  |  |  | fits?: whether one more candidate fits the scope cap, the drift reserve and the per-credential cap, given what is in flight. |
| `happa::admit(candidates, limits, in_flight)` |  |  |  | admit: who runs next: candidates offered slots in ranked order under the global cap, each scope's cap, the drift reserve and the per-credential cap, given the work in flight; the names admitted, in order. |
| `happa::offsets(names, interval)` |  |  |  | offsets: each leaf's place in a drift interval: the leaves ranked by the BLAKE3 of their names and spread evenly across it, so no stretch of the interval holds more than its share. |
| `happa::last_slot(offset, now, interval)` |  |  |  | last_slot: the start of a leaf's most recent drift slot at a moment, or nil before its first slot. |
| `happa::drift_due(leaves, now, interval)` |  |  |  | drift_due: the leaves whose drift slot has come since their last check, sorted by name; a leaf never checked is due from its first slot. |
| `happa::skip_reasons` |  |  |  | skip_reasons: the typed world facts that let a fat template run as one, the annotation's values. |
| `happa::skip_annotation` |  |  |  | skip_annotation: the annotation a template declares its skip reason in. |
| `happa::skip_reason(raw)` |  |  |  | skip_reason: the typed reason an annotation's value names before any colon, or nil when it names none. |
| `happa::leaves_path(workspace)` |  |  |  | leaves_path: the leaf declaration a workspace's leaves are written in. |
| `happa::admission(classification, skip, mode, never_reconciled, fix?)` |  |  |  | admission: whether a template is admitted: a leaf always; a fat one when admission is off, when it declares a typed skip reason, or when it already reconciled; a new fat one is refused naming its workspace's leaf declaration, and under :generate with a draft entry to commit. |
| `happa::ceil_div(a, b)` |  |  |  | ceil_div: whole-number division rounded up. |
| `happa::p95(samples)` |  |  |  | p95: the nearest-rank 95th percentile of the newest 32 cycle times, or nil when there are none. |
| `happa::bucket_up(ms)` |  |  |  | bucket_up: a duration rounded up to the next whole second, so jitter under a second never moves the floor. |
| `happa::without_nil(m)` |  |  |  | without_nil: a record without the fields whose value is nil, as a status field omits them. |
| `happa::ratchet(prev, leaf_keys, fat, p95_ms, tolerance)` |  |  |  | ratchet: a template's next `status.leaf`: the leaf key count and cycle p95 measured, the floor that may only fall, and every regression past it (the p95 within a tolerance in percent). |
| `happa::stall_after_ms` |  |  |  | stall_after_ms: how long a planning or applying leaf may go without a heartbeat before it is stalled, unless the leaf says otherwise. |
| `happa::health(leaf, now_ms)` |  |  |  | health: one leaf's verdict and alert verdict at a moment: rate limited until a time, failed, stalled when its heartbeat is older than its stall window, else its phase; failed and stalled leaves fire an alert. |
| `happa::holding?(state)` |  |  |  | holding?: whether a leaf state holds its dependents back: failed, stalled or rate limited. |
| `happa::counted(xs, cap)` |  |  |  | counted: a status list capped at a number of names, with how many were left out. |
| `happa::judge(leaves, cap)` |  |  |  | judge: the parent's partial state over its leaves: Partial when any leaf failed, stalled or is blocked by one that holds it, Converged when every leaf is ready, else Progressing; counts per state, and the held and blocked leaves named, up to a cap per list. |
| `happa::slice(rendered, addresses)` |  |  |  | slice: a leaf's Terraform JSON cut from the whole render: only the resources and modules its addresses name, every other block kept whole. |
| `happa::inputs(declaration, values)` |  |  |  | inputs: each leaf's input slice in the keyed form: the shared inputs and the collection cut to the one entry the leaf's key names; an entry with no key, or a key held twice, is refused. |
| `happa::share_ok(v)` |  |  |  | share_ok: a check that a value is a fraction from zero up to, not including, one. |
| `happa::count_ok(lo)` |  |  |  | count_ok: a check that a value is a whole number of at least a floor. |
| `happa::cap_schema` |  |  |  | cap_schema: each leaf setting's fields: a cap, a share, a mode, the rate budget and the queue. |
| `happa::cap_defaults` |  |  |  | cap_defaults: the value each leaf setting takes when nothing declares it. |
| `happa::cap_offs` |  |  |  | cap_offs: the value each leaf setting takes when it is configured off: the monolithic or unpaced form, never a deletion. |
| `happa::setting(kind, fields)` |  |  |  | setting: one leaf setting's entry: its kind is the setting, its name says where it came from. |
| `happa::caps(declaration)` |  |  |  | caps: the leaf settings of §9 from a declaration of entries: each judged alone, a bad entry refused and its default kept, an entry configured off taking its off value; then the cross-entry rules, each refusing only its own entry. |
| `happa::same?(a, b)` |  |  |  | same?: whether two values render as the same JSON, the form the operator's answers are recorded in. |
| `happa::as_key(k)` |  |  |  | as_key: a recorded leaf key read back as happa writes it. |
| `happa::resource(type, name, attrs)` |  |  |  | resource: one Terraform resource as a part of a body. |
| `happa::data(type, name, attrs)` |  |  |  | data: one Terraform data source as a part of a body. |
| `happa::output(name, value)` |  |  |  | output: one Terraform output as a part of a body. |
| `happa::ref(type, name, attr)` |  |  |  | ref: an interpolation that reads one attribute of a resource; a data source's type starts with `data.`. |
| `happa::provider_sources` |  |  |  | provider_sources: each provider's local name and its registry source, the table pangea-operator's `ruby/provider_registry.rs` holds, so a JSON body declares what it uses. |
| `happa::provider_of(type)` |  |  |  | provider_of: the provider local name a resource type belongs to: everything before its first underscore. |
| `happa::part_whys(p)` |  |  |  | part_whys: why a part cannot stand in a body: its provider has no known source. |
| `happa::placed(block, p)` |  |  |  | placed: a block map with one more part in it, under its type when it has one. |
| `happa::body(parts)` |  |  |  | body: the Terraform JSON of a list of parts, with `terraform.required_providers` derived from the types it uses; a duplicate address or an unknown provider is a refusal of that part alone, and the rest stands. |
| `happa::aws_credentials(account)` |  |  |  | aws_credentials: a leaf's AWS credentials: the bootstrap session Secret until the Roles Anywhere trust anchor and profile ARNs exist, then a Roles Anywhere session; one ARN without the other is refused. |
| `happa::leaf_options` |  |  |  | leaf_options: the options `leaf` takes. |
| `happa::leaf(name, b, opts)` |  |  |  | leaf: an InfrastructureTemplate whose Terraform JSON rides inline (`dialect: json`) under the house defaults: magma, auto-approve, destroy protection, import on conflict, delete and replace refused; `disposable: true` lifts the protection and the refusal for a leaf whose resources come and go. |
| `happa::state(name)` |  |  |  | state: a PangeaNamespace whose state lives in the operator's own Postgres (`pangea_state` on 127.0.0.1, credentials in `pangea-system/pangea-postgres`). |
| `happa::manifests(objects)` |  |  |  | manifests: the multi-document text a workspace commits: one JSON object per document, a line break at the end. |

## heni

`heni` 0.1.1 · needs: deeta, hikisuu, kansuu, moji, retsu, shisutemu

heni (変異) — mutation testing for a blue package: each mutation changes one literal in a fresh copy of the package, runs its tests, and must make them fail.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `heni::hn_apply(text, find, replace)` | `heni::apply` | yes | `B0013` | hn_apply: {text} with `find` replaced, or {error} when it does not occur exactly once. |
| `heni::verdict(applied, status)` | `hn_verdict` (old) |  |  | verdict: The verdict for one mutation from its test run's status. nil means the |
| `heni::mutations(parsed)` | `hn_mutations` (old) |  |  | mutations: Mutations from parsed JSON: [{name, file, find, replace}]. |
| `heni::stranded(dir, muts)` | `hn_stranded` (old) |  |  | stranded: the mutations that no longer apply to the package in `dir`, each as {name, detail}: its file is gone or its `find` does not occur exactly once. No test runs. |
| `heni::stranded_text(package, rows)` | `hn_stranded_text` (old) |  |  | stranded_text: one line per stranded mutation, named by package and mutation. |
| `heni::package_name(dir)` | `hn_package_name` (old) |  |  | package_name: The package's name: the last segment of its directory. |
| `heni::copy(src, dest)` | `hn_copy` (old) |  |  | copy: Copy every file under `src` to `dest`, keeping relative paths. |
| `heni::tree_root(dir)` | `hn_tree_root` (old) |  |  | tree_root: the bidama a package directory belongs to: the nearest directory at or above it holding a Bluefile, so a level is copied with its root. |
| `heni::bluefile_at_or_above(dir)` | `hn_bluefile_at_or_above` (old) |  |  | bluefile_at_or_above: the nearest directory at or above `dir` that holds a Bluefile, or nil when none does. |
| `heni::level_path(pkg_dir)` | `hn_level_path` (old) |  |  | level_path: a package directory's path below the directory its bidama sits in: `kazu` for a bidama, `tree/kid` for a level. |
| `heni::fresh(pkg_dir, tmp, tag)` | `hn_fresh` (old) |  |  | fresh: A fresh root holding one copy of the bidama the package belongs to, its whole tree: <tmp>/heni-<now>-<tag>/<bidama>. |
| `heni::hn_test(blue, root, name, test_file)` | `heni::test` |  | `B0013` `B0083` | hn_test: Run the copy's tests with the fresh root first on BLUE_PATH. The capture. |
| `heni::status(cap)` | `hn_status` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::run(spec)` | `hn_run` (old) |  |  | run: spec: {package, mutations, blue, test, tmp}. Returns the report. |
| `heni::one(spec, name, test_file, i, m)` | `hn_one` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::report_text(r)` | `hn_report_text` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::detail(x)` | `hn_detail` (old) |  |  |  |
| `heni::ok?(r)` | `hn_ok?` (old) |  |  | ok?: whether every mutation was caught. |
| `heni::score(r)` | `hn_score` (old) |  |  | score: a run's caught ratio, the share of mutations its tests caught, as {caught, total}: every mutation run counts in total, only a caught one in caught. |
| `heni::fell?(recorded, measured)` | `hn_fell?` (old) |  |  | fell?: whether a measured caught ratio is below a recorded one, compared without division; a package whose mutations all vanished fell too. |
| `heni::survivors(r)` | `hn_survivors` (old) |  |  | survivors: the names of the mutations a run's tests survived. |
| `heni::sound?(r)` | `hn_sound?` (old) |  |  | sound?: whether a run measured anything a ratio can stand on: the control passed and no mutation was refused or blind. |
| `heni::cli` | `hn_cli` (old) |  |  | cli: heni's command line: the package and its mutations file, and which blue and which test file to run. |
| `heni::main` | `hn_main` (old) |  | `B0084` | main: the command's entry point: argv read by heni's cli through hikisuu, which answers help and completion and refuses a bad line with exit 64. |
| `heni::mutate(a)` | `hn_mutate` (old) |  |  | mutate: one heni run from a parsed command line: every mutation run, the report on stdout, and an error when the run is not clean. |
| `heni::finds(a)` | `hn_finds` (old) |  |  | finds: the `--finds` run: every stranded mutation on stdout, and an error when there is one. |
| `heni::run_all(a)` | `hn_run_all` (old) |  |  | run_all: the mutation run: every mutation tested, the report on stdout, and an error when the run is not clean. |
| `heni::end_to_end` | `hn_end_to_end` (old) |  |  |  |

## hikisuu

`hikisuu` 0.1.1 · needs: jisho, kyohi, moji, retsu

hikisuu (引数) — command-line arguments: one declared spec is parsed into a record, rendered as help, and answered as shell completion.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hikisuu::flag(args, name)` |  |  |  | flag: the value after a flag in an argument list, or nil. |
| `hikisuu::flag_text(key)` |  |  |  | flag_text: the spelling of a flag key on the command line. |
| `hikisuu::kind_of(default)` |  |  |  | kind_of: the kind a flag default implies: switch, int or text. |
| `hikisuu::flag_record(key, entry)` |  |  |  | flag_record: a flag entry as one record of key, text, kind, default, about, min and value. |
| `hikisuu::flags_of(flags)` |  |  |  | flags_of: the flag records a flags map declares, in key order. |
| `hikisuu::slot_record(entry)` |  |  |  | slot_record: a positional entry as one record of name, about, many and optional. |
| `hikisuu::command_record(entry)` |  |  |  | command_record: a command entry, a bare name or a record, as one record of name, about, flags and positional. |
| `hikisuu::commands_of(spec)` |  |  |  | commands_of: the command records a spec declares; empty when it has none. |
| `hikisuu::shape(spec, command)` |  |  |  | shape: the flags and positionals in force for a command of a spec, nil for none, refusing a key declared twice. |
| `hikisuu::help_wanted?(args)` |  |  |  | help_wanted?: whether a line asks for help before any `--`. |
| `hikisuu::read_value(f, raw)` |  |  |  | read_value: a flag's text value read as its kind, with the fault named when it does not read. |
| `hikisuu::split_flag(arg)` |  |  |  | split_flag: a `--name=value` argument as its name and value; value nil without `=`. |
| `hikisuu::walk(args, flags, acc)` |  |  |  | walk: the flags, positional words and problems a line holds, read left to right. |
| `hikisuu::problem(acc, text)` |  |  |  | problem: an accumulator with one more problem. |
| `hikisuu::value(acc, key, v)` |  |  |  | value: an accumulator with one flag's value set. |
| `hikisuu::place(words, slots, values, problems)` |  |  |  | place: positional words bound to their slots, with a problem for each missing or extra word. |
| `hikisuu::slot_label(s)` |  |  |  | slot_label: how help and errors name a positional: upper case, with dots when it takes many. |
| `hikisuu::command_names(spec)` |  |  |  | command_names: the names of a spec's commands joined for a sentence. |
| `hikisuu::pick_command(args, spec)` |  |  |  | pick_command: the command a line names and the arguments after it, or a problem. |
| `hikisuu::sound(spec)` |  |  |  | sound: a spec checked whole, its own flags and every command's, before any line is read; returns the spec. |
| `hikisuu::parse(args, spec)` |  |  |  | parse: a command line read by a spec into one record of every flag, positional and the command; every fault raised together as `:usage`. |
| `hikisuu::usage_line(spec, command)` |  |  |  | usage_line: the one-line synopsis of a spec, or of one of its commands. |
| `hikisuu::flag_left(f)` |  |  |  | flag_left: a flag's left column in help: its text, and its value word when it takes one. |
| `hikisuu::flag_right(f)` |  |  |  | flag_right: a flag's right column in help: its about text and a default worth showing. |
| `hikisuu::columns(heading, rows)` |  |  |  | columns: rows of a left and right text laid out as two aligned columns under a heading. |
| `hikisuu::render(spec, command)` |  |  |  | render: the help text of a spec, or of one of its commands. |
| `hikisuu::complete(args, spec)` |  |  |  | complete: the words that may follow a partial command line, for the last word as typed so far. |
| `hikisuu::matching(typed, candidates)` |  |  |  | matching: the candidates that start with what was typed, sorted. |
| `hikisuu::script(spec, shell)` |  |  |  | script: the completion script a shell sources for a program, asking the program itself through `__complete`. |
| `hikisuu::outcome(args, spec)` |  |  |  | outcome: what a command line asks of a program: help text, completion words, a usage refusal with its exit status, or the record to run with. |
| `hikisuu::run_with(args, spec, f, io)` |  |  |  | run_with: one program run through its spec against given sinks for stdout, stderr and exit; returns what the program returned, or nil. |
| `hikisuu::command(spec, f)` |  |  | `B0083` | command: the program's door: argv read by the spec; help and completion printed, a bad line refused on stderr with exit 64, otherwise f called with the record. |
| `hikisuu::souji_like` |  |  |  | souji_like: a spec shaped like souji's, the fixture the tests read. |
