## hon

`hon` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, moji, mokuroku, retsu, shisutemu

hon (本) — a project's book: its pages, each of one Diátaxis kind, rendered into the files a reader or an agent opens.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hon::page_kinds` |  |  |  | page_kinds: The four Diátaxis kinds, in the order a book shows them. |
| `hon::front_matter_lines(text)` |  |  |  | front_matter_lines: The lines between a page's opening `---` and the next `---`, or [] when the page has none. |
| `hon::front_matter(text)` |  |  |  | front_matter: A page's front matter as a map from each key to its text. |
| `hon::front_matter_text(kind, summary)` |  |  |  | front_matter_text: The front matter block for a page of `kind` with a one-line `summary`, ending in a blank line. |
| `hon::front_matter_of(pairs)` |  |  |  | front_matter_of: A front matter block from [key, value] pairs, in order, ending in a blank line. |
| `hon::quoted?(v)` |  |  |  | quoted?: Whether `v` is one double-quoted YAML scalar. |
| `hon::plain_scalar?(v)` |  |  |  | plain_scalar?: Whether YAML reads `v` as the plain text it is: no `: ` or ` #` inside, no `:` at its end, no indicator character first. |
| `hon::yaml_value(v)` |  |  |  | yaml_value: `v` as YAML reads it back: plain when it can be, else double-quoted. |
| `hon::yaml_unquote(v)` |  |  |  | yaml_unquote: The text of a front matter value, without the double quotes YAML needs. |
| `hon::front_matter_refusals(pages)` |  |  |  | front_matter_refusals: One refusal for each front matter line, of each page given as [path, text], that YAML would not read as the text it shows. |
| `hon::page_body(text)` |  |  |  | page_body: A page without its front matter. |
| `hon::page_kind(text)` |  |  |  | page_kind: The kind a page's front matter declares, as a keyword, or nil when it declares none the book knows. |
| `hon::kind_refusals(pages)` |  |  |  | kind_refusals: One refusal for each page, given as [path, text], that declares no kind or a kind the book does not know. |
| `hon::doc_pages(root)` |  |  |  | doc_pages: Every Markdown page under `root/docs`, as [path from root, text], sorted by path. |
| `hon::normal_path(path)` |  |  |  | normal_path: A relative path with each `.` step and each `dir/..` pair removed. |
| `hon::common_steps(a, b)` |  |  |  | common_steps: How many leading steps two split paths share. |
| `hon::relative_path(from, path)` |  |  |  | relative_path: The path to `path` as a file in directory `from` writes it, both paths relative to one root. |
| `hon::path_in(dir, target)` |  |  |  | path_in: The root-relative path of `target` written in a file in directory `dir`. |
| `hon::outside_link?(target)` |  |  |  | outside_link?: Whether a link target leaves the project: a URL, a mail address, an anchor or an absolute path. |
| `hon::rebased_target(target, from, to)` |  |  |  | rebased_target: A link target written in directory `from`, rewritten to reach the same file from directory `to`. |
| `hon::rebased_links(text, from, to)` |  |  |  | rebased_links: Markdown text moved from directory `from` to directory `to`, each relative link rewritten to reach the same file. |
| `hon::fence_line?(line)` |  |  |  | fence_line?: Whether a line opens or closes a fenced code block. |
| `hon::outside_fences(text, f)` |  |  |  | outside_fences: The text with `f` applied to every line that is not inside a fenced code block. |
| `hon::demoted(text, n)` |  |  |  | demoted: The text with every heading outside code moved `n` levels down. |
| `hon::page_title(body, fallback)` |  |  |  | page_title: The text of a page body's first `# ` heading, or `fallback` when it has none. |
| `hon::without_title(body)` |  |  |  | without_title: A page body without its `# ` heading and the blank lines around it. |
| `hon::lead_sentence(body)` |  |  |  | lead_sentence: The first line of prose in a page body, used when its front matter gives no summary. |
| `hon::lead_and_sections(body)` |  |  |  | lead_and_sections: [lead, sections] of a page body: the text before its first `## ` heading, and the text from it on. |
| `hon::page_of(path, text)` |  |  |  | page_of: A page's record from its path and text: path, kind, title, summary, order, role, runs and body. |
| `hon::kind_rank(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_rank: Where a kind stands in the book's order, from 0. |
| `hon::book_order(pages)` |  |  |  | book_order: Pages in the book's order: by kind, then by their `order`, then by path. |
| `hon::kind_heading(kind)` |  |  |  | kind_heading: The heading a book gives the part of one kind. |
| `hon::project_of(pages, manifest)` |  |  |  | project_of: A project record from its pages, given as [path, text], and the manifest of its Bluefile.lock. |
| `hon::project_at(root)` |  |  |  | project_at: The project whose root is `root`: its pages under `docs/` and its Bluefile.lock manifest. |
| `hon::generated_outputs(project)` |  |  |  | generated_outputs: The paths the project's Bluefile declares as generated files. |
| `hon::generated_files(project)` |  |  |  | generated_files: Every file the project's Bluefile renders from its sources: |
| `hon::page_with_role(project, role)` |  |  |  | page_with_role: The project's page whose front matter names `role`, or nil. |
| `hon::listed_pages(project, kind)` |  |  |  | listed_pages: The pages a book lists under `kind`: every page of that kind except the introduction and the book itself. |
| `hon::rendered_path(page)` |  |  |  | rendered_path: Where a reader finds a page: the agents page is read as the AGENTS.md it renders, every other page at its own path. |
| `hon::page_link(page, to)` |  |  |  | page_link: A list item linking to a page from directory `to`, with its summary. |
| `hon::book_map(project, level, to)` |  |  |  | book_map: Every listed page grouped by kind under headings of `level`, each linked from directory `to`. |
| `hon::rendered_notice(sources)` |  |  |  | rendered_notice: The HTML comment that opens a rendered file and names its sources and the command that rewrites it. |
| `hon::page_body_at(page, to)` |  |  |  | page_body_at: A page's body with its links rewritten for a file in directory `to`. |
| `hon::paragraphs(parts)` |  |  |  | paragraphs: Text joined from parts, one blank line between each and a line break at the end. |
| `hon::readme_text(project)` |  |  |  | readme_text: README.md: the introduction's lead, a quickstart from the first tutorial, the introduction's sections, and a map of the book. |
| `hon::readme_map(project)` |  |  |  | readme_map: The book's pages as a README lists them: each tutorial, how-to and reference page in the open, and the explanation pages folded behind one line that counts them. |
| `hon::build_section(project)` |  |  |  | build_section: How to build and test the project, read from its Bluefile.lock: the checks, the generated files and the commands. |
| `hon::book_line(project)` |  |  |  | book_line: The sentence that names the project's book, or empty text when it has none. |
| `hon::book_pointer(project)` |  |  |  | book_pointer: Where a reader finds the book and the index of every page, as one paragraph: an agent file stays short and points into the book. |
| `hon::agents_text(project)` |  |  |  | agents_text: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md: the agents page's lead, the build commands from the Bluefile, the page's sections, and where the book is. |
| `hon::llms_text(project)` |  |  |  | llms_text: llms.txt: the project's name as the heading, its summary as a quote, then one section of links per kind, explanation last as Optional. |
| `hon::llms_full_text(project)` |  |  |  | llms_full_text: llms-full.txt: the introduction, then the full text of every hand-written tutorial, how-to and reference page, each opened by its path. |
| `hon::doc_parts(name, lines)` |  |  |  | doc_parts: [prose, examples] of a definition's `@doc` lines: the prose with the leading `name: ` removed, and each indented example line without its indent. |
| `hon::entry_section(pkg, entry)` |  |  |  | entry_section: The reference section for one definition of package `pkg`: its qualified signature, its doc prose and its examples as a blue block. |
| `hon::reference_text(title, records)` |  |  |  | reference_text: The reference of every package in `records` (mokuroku's catalogue records): each package's purpose, then each definition's signature, doc and examples. |
| `hon::heading_anchor(text)` |  |  |  | heading_anchor: The anchor GitHub gives a heading: lower case, spaces as hyphens, other punctuation dropped. |
| `hon::link_from(to, target)` |  |  |  | link_from: A link to `target` written in directory `to`: a URL as it is, a project path made relative. |
| `hon::reference_links(builtins, records, to, reference, catalog)` |  |  |  | reference_links: A map from each name a reader may write in code to the place the reference documents it: a built-in, given as [name, topic title], under its topic in `reference`; a package, and each of its definitions, under the package in `catalog`. Paths are written from directory `to`. |
| `hon::called_name(span)` |  |  |  | called_name: The name a code span calls, when the span is a call: the text before its first parenthesis. |
| `hon::linked_code(line, links)` |  |  |  | linked_code: A line with each inline code span that names an entry of `links` turned into a link to it; a span already inside a link is left alone. |
| `hon::chapters(project)` |  |  |  | chapters: The book's numbered chapters: every listed tutorial, then every listed how-to, each as [number, page]. |
| `hon::chapter_heading(chapter)` |  |  |  | chapter_heading: A chapter's heading text: its number and its title. |
| `hon::book_contents(project)` |  |  |  | book_contents: The book's table of contents: each part with its chapters linked to their anchors, and the reference and explanation pages linked from directory `to`. |
| `hon::chapter_text(chapter, links, to)` |  |  |  | chapter_text: One chapter as the book prints it: a numbered heading, then the page body with its headings moved under it, its links rewritten for directory `to`, and its code names linked to the reference. |
| `hon::book_text(project, links, to)` |  |  |  | book_text: The book in Markdown, written to a file in directory `to`: its contents, the tutorial and how-to chapters in full, and the reference and explanation pages as linked lists. |
| `hon::fenced_blocks(body)` |  |  |  | fenced_blocks: Every fenced code block in a page body, as a record of its language, its arguments and its code. |
| `hon::block_arg(args, key)` |  |  |  | block_arg: The value of argument `key=value` among a block's arguments, or nil. |
| `hon::command_words(line)` |  |  |  | command_words: The words of a console command, with single and double quotes grouping words as a shell reads them. |
| `hon::console_steps(code)` |  |  |  | console_steps: The commands of a console block, each as [command line, expected output lines]. |
| `hon::runnable_page?(page)` |  |  |  | runnable_page?: Whether the docs check runs a page's blocks: every tutorial and how-to, and any page whose front matter says `runs: true`. |
| `hon::run_command(blue, dir, words)` |  |  | `B0083` | run_command: Run one console command in directory `dir` with blue at `blue`: leading NAME=value words set the environment, and a leading `blue` is the blue under test. |
| `hon::trimmed_output(text)` |  |  |  | trimmed_output: Command output without its trailing line breaks, as the book shows it. |
| `hon::step_failure(line, expected, cap, failing)` |  |  |  | step_failure: Why one console step does not hold, or nil. A step holds when its command exits 0 and, if the page shows output, prints exactly it; in a block marked `fails`, when its command exits non-zero and prints every line the page shows. |
| `hon::run_console(blue, root, here, steps, failing)` |  |  |  | run_console: Run a console block's steps in the sandbox `root`, from the directory `here` inside it; returns [here, failures]. `cd` moves `here`; every other step is judged by `step_failure`. |
| `hon::inspect_text(text)` |  |  |  | inspect_text: Text shown in a failure message as the list of its lines, so a line break or a trailing space is visible. |
| `hon::write_block_file(path, code)` |  |  |  | write_block_file: Write a block's code to `path`: a blue file through the formatter, any other file as written. |
| `hon::run_blocks(blue, scratch, page)` |  |  | `B0083` | run_blocks: Run every blue and console block of one page, in order, in a fresh directory under `scratch`; returns the failures, each naming the page. A block with `file=PATH` is written there; a blue block without one runs, with `blue test` when it holds a test; a console block with the argument `fails` must fail; a console block with the argument `host` needs the network or nix and is shown, not run. |
| `hon::builtin_topics(reference)` |  |  |  | builtin_topics: Each built-in name with the title of its topic, as [name, title], from the JSON `blue reference` prints. |
| `hon::published_reference(project)` |  |  |  | published_reference: Where the language reference is read: the project's own `docs/REFERENCE.md` when it has one, else blue's published copy. |
| `hon::book_links(blue, root, project, to)` |  |  | `B0083` | book_links: The code names a book written in directory `to` links: every built-in, from the reference that `blue` at `blue` prints, and every definition of the project's own packages under `root`. |
| `hon::fact_names` |  |  |  | fact_names: The facts a page may hold in a `hon` block, each named by the block's first word: `commands` and `language` from the reference the rendering blue prints, `packages` from the project's catalogue, and `include PAGE#ANCHOR` for one section of another page. |
| `hon::fact_line(code)` |  |  |  | fact_line: The fact a `hon` block names, as [name, arguments]: the words of its first line. |
| `hon::facts_in(body)` |  |  |  | facts_in: The facts a page body names, one [name, arguments] for each of its `hon` blocks, in order. |
| `hon::with_facts(body, render)` |  |  |  | with_facts: A page body with each `hon` block replaced by the text `render` gives for the fact it names; every other block is kept as written. |
| `hon::heading_level(line)` |  |  |  | heading_level: The level of a Markdown heading line, or 0 for any other line. |
| `hon::section_of(body, anchor)` |  |  |  | section_of: The text under the heading of `body` whose anchor is `anchor`, up to the next heading of its level or above, without the heading itself; nil when no heading has that anchor. |
| `hon::included_text(project, dir, target)` |  |  |  | included_text: The section `target` names, written `page#anchor` from directory `dir`, with its links rewritten for `dir`; raises naming what is missing when the page or the section does not exist. |
| `hon::first_sentence(text)` |  |  |  | first_sentence: The first sentence of a help text, ending in a full stop. |
| `hon::command_table(commands)` |  |  |  | command_table: The commands `blue reference` lists, as a Markdown table: each as it is typed, with the first sentence of its help. |
| `hon::language_table(reference)` |  |  |  | language_table: The size of the language as `blue reference` states it, read from the tables the implementation runs on: its names by arbiter, its forms, operators and reserved words, its check rules by fix, its Bluefile words and its commands. |
| `hon::package_line(record, catalog)` |  |  |  | package_line: One package as a list item: its name linked to its section of the catalogue at `catalog`, then the purpose its `@moduledoc` states. |
| `hon::package_list(records, catalog)` |  |  |  | package_list: The project's packages, counted, then one line each, linked into the catalogue at `catalog`. |
| `hon::fact_sources(blue, root, project)` |  |  | `B0083` | fact_sources: What the facts of a project's pages read, gathered once: the reference that `blue` at `blue` prints, when a page names `commands` or `language`, and the catalogue of the project's packages under `root`, when a page names `packages`. |
| `hon::fact_text(sources, project, page, fact)` |  |  |  | fact_text: The text one fact renders in `page`, from the gathered `sources`; raises naming the page when the fact is unknown or its source is absent. |
| `hon::with_facts_of(project, sources)` |  |  |  | with_facts_of: The project with every `hon` block of every page rendered from the gathered `sources`. |
| `hon::fact_refusals(project)` |  |  |  | fact_refusals: One refusal for each `hon` block of the project's pages that names no fact hon renders, or includes a page or a section that does not exist; each names its page, and every other block still renders. |
| `hon::unreachable_address?(line)` |  |  |  | unreachable_address?: Whether a line links where a reader of this repository cannot follow: a link to a `pleme-io/` repository readers cannot open, in any case, or a path under its design directory (`theory`, then a slash) that names an upper-case document. |
| `hon::address_refusals(files)` |  |  |  | address_refusals: One refusal for each line of each file, given as [path, text], that links where a reader cannot follow; each names its file and line, and every other line passes. |
| `hon::rendering(name, root, to)` |  |  |  | rendering: The text of one rendering of the project at `root`, by name: `:readme`, `:agents`, `:llms`, `:llms_full`, or `:book` for a book written in directory `to`. |
| `hon::write_rendering(name, out)` |  |  |  | write_rendering: Write one rendering of the project at $GEN_ROOT (default `.`) to $GEN_OUT (default `out`): what a Bluefile `generate` runs. |
| `hon::sample_pages` |  |  |  | sample_pages: The pages of a small sample project the tests render: an introduction, a tutorial, a how-to, an agents page and an explanation. |
| `hon::sample_project` |  |  |  | sample_project: The sample pages as a project, with a manifest that declares a catalogue, a check and a generated README. |
| `hon::fact_pages` |  |  |  | fact_pages: The pages of a sample project the fact tests render: an introduction that includes a section of an implementer's page. |

## ibuki

`ibuki` 0.1.0 · needs: jisho, kyohi, moji, ran, retsu, seikika, shomei, shuugou

ibuki (息吹) — the plane an app docks into, as pure decisions: the five structural classes and a docking check over the reach `blue lock` measured, the Flash channel's token bucket, the Tide channel's LEDBAT controller, and a Strand, a last-writer-wins map whose every transition is a hash-linked record of its input and output roots.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ibuki::classes` |  |  |  | classes: the five structural classes of the plane, in order, each with the host capabilities its ceiling admits. |
| `ibuki::class_named(name)` |  |  |  | class_named: the class record of a class name, or a :not_found raise naming the five. |
| `ibuki::atoms` |  |  |  | atoms: the vision's CapSet atoms, in its order, each with the host capability that holds it as `blue lock` records it, nil for the pure atom; the garasu canvas comes from the vision's vocabulary. |
| `ibuki::atom_named(name)` |  |  |  | atom_named: the atom record of an atom name, or a :not_found raise naming the atoms. |
| `ibuki::threshold_ok(t)` |  |  |  | threshold_ok: a k-of-n threshold when 1 <= k <= n and n >= 2, else a :bad_option raise. |
| `ibuki::classify(shape)` |  |  |  | classify: the class a program's shape belongs to: a threshold makes a synod, two parties an airlock, moving a nomad, merged state a strand, and none of these a crystal. |
| `ibuki::reach(lock)` |  |  |  | reach: the host capabilities a locked program reaches through the host: the join of floors `blue lock` recorded, less each capability a provider serves for the whole program or for every package whose floor needs it. |
| `ibuki::admit(name, reached)` |  |  |  | admit: the refusals of a reach under a class's ceiling, one :denied per capability past it; empty when the reach fits. |
| `ibuki::dock(shape, lock)` |  |  |  | dock: the docking verdict for a shape and a lock: its class, the reach the lock measured, and the refusals of that reach under the class's ceiling. |
| `ibuki::airlock_frame(a, b)` |  |  |  | airlock_frame: the frame two parties meet in: the capabilities both ceilings grant, never the disk, so neither the filesystem nor the append-only store it holds. |
| `ibuki::flash(opts)` |  |  |  | flash: a Flash channel's token bucket: `rate` bytes a second (16384), a `burst` it may spend at once (one second's worth), packets of at most `mtu` bytes (1280), starting full at `at_ms` (0). |
| `ibuki::refill(bucket, now_ms)` |  |  |  | refill: a bucket's tokens, in thousandths of a byte, at `now_ms`; a clock that steps back adds nothing. |
| `ibuki::flash_send(bucket, bytes, now_ms)` |  |  |  | flash_send: the bucket's verdict on a packet at `now_ms`: :sent with the spent bucket, :wait with the milliseconds until it can send, or :refused past the MTU. |
| `ibuki::tide(opts)` |  |  |  | tide: a Tide channel's LEDBAT controller (RFC 6817): a `target_ms` of queuing delay (100), `gain` (1), `mss` (1280), `min_cwnd` and `init_cwnd` packets (2), `allowed_increase` packets an ack (1), `base_minutes` of base-delay history (10), a `filter` of current delays (4), and `collapse_ms` (2): at that queuing delay the window falls to its floor; `collapse_ms: nil` configures it off. |
| `ibuki::newest(xs, keep)` |  |  |  | newest: the last `keep` items of a list, all of them when it is shorter. |
| `ibuki::base_update(base, minute, delay, keep)` |  |  |  | base_update: the per-minute minima of one-way delay, the newest `keep` minutes, after a sample at `minute`. |
| `ibuki::floor_window(t)` |  |  |  | floor_window: the smallest window a Tide keeps, in bytes. |
| `ibuki::queuing(t)` |  |  |  | queuing: the queuing delay a Tide last measured: the least of its current delays less its base delay, nil before any sample. |
| `ibuki::tide_ack(t, ack)` |  |  |  | tide_ack: the Tide after an ack of `bytes` with a one-way `delay_ms` at `now_ms`: the window moves toward the target queuing delay, at most `allowed_increase` packets past `flight`, never under its floor, and falls to the floor at `collapse_ms`. |
| `ibuki::tide_loss(t)` |  |  |  | tide_loss: the Tide after a loss: its window halved, never under its floor. |
| `ibuki::wins?(a, b)` |  |  |  | wins?: whether one register cell beats another: the later `at`, then the greater writer, then the greater value text, so every two replicas pick the same cell. |
| `ibuki::put(state, key, value, at, by)` |  | yes |  | put: a last-writer-wins map with one key written, when the cell wins over the one it holds. |
| `ibuki::merge(a, b)` |  |  |  | merge: two last-writer-wins maps joined: each key holds the winning cell of either; commutative, associative and idempotent. |
| `ibuki::state_root(state)` |  |  |  | state_root: the root of a Strand's map: seikika's root of its canonical bytes, so two maps share a root only when they are one value, kind for kind. |
| `ibuki::strand(opts)` |  |  |  | strand: an empty Strand under a protocol label (`lww-map/1`), naming its transition's engine `def_hash`: the caller's, else the engine's identity of `step` as `def_hash` reads it, which is nil where the engine identifies no program. |
| `ibuki::written(state, op)` |  |  |  | written: the map one Strand op leaves: a :put written in, a :merge joined, anything else a :bad_option raise. |
| `ibuki::steps_between(before, after)` |  |  |  | steps_between: how many keys hold a different cell after a transition than before it. |
| `ibuki::step(s, op)` |  |  |  | step: the Strand after one op, `{op: :put, key:, value:, at:, by:}` or `{op: :merge, state:}`, with the record that links it: its input and output roots, the op's hash, the cells it wrote and the previous head. |
| `ibuki::records(s, ops)` |  |  |  | records: every record a run of ops writes onto a Strand, in order. |
| `ibuki::replay(s, ops)` |  |  |  | replay: the Strand after every op, in order. |
| `ibuki::verify?(s, rs, head)` |  |  |  | verify?: whether records link from a Strand's genesis to `head`, each naming the head before it. |
| `ibuki::random_state(seed, n, keys)` |  |  |  | random_state: a last-writer-wins map drawn from a seed: n writes over `keys` keys by three writers. |

## jisho

`jisho` 0.1.0 · needs: retsu

jisho (辞書) — dictionaries: maps as values, read and changed by key, each change a new map.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `jisho::keys(m)` |  |  |  |  |
| `jisho::values(m)` |  |  |  |  |
| `jisho::entries(m)` |  |  |  |  |
| `jisho::size(m)` |  |  |  |  |
| `jisho::empty?(m)` |  | yes |  | empty?: Whether xs is nil or has no elements. |
| `jisho::has?(m, key)` |  | yes |  | has?: Whether the key is present. A plain map goes to jisho. |
| `jisho::put(m, key, value)` |  | yes |  | put: Create or replace an entry; a plain map goes to jisho, which returns a new map. |
| `jisho::delete(m, key)` |  | yes |  | delete: Make an entry absent; absent counts as done. A plain map goes to jisho; in_space("gitto", repo) deletes a branch or tag. |
| `jisho::need(m, key)` |  | yes |  | need: One entry, raising :not_found when absent. A plain map goes to jisho. |
| `jisho::update(m, key, f)` |  | yes |  | update: Replace an entry with f of the old value; raises :not_found when absent. A plain map goes to jisho. |
| `jisho::select(m, wanted)` |  |  |  |  |
| `jisho::get_any(m, keys)` |  |  |  | get_any: The value at the first of `keys` that holds one, or nil; a field read under its preferred name, then its fallbacks. |
| `jisho::push_at(m, k, v)` |  |  |  | push_at: A new map whose list at key k gains v at its end; an absent key starts the list. |
| `jisho::append_at(m, k, xs)` |  |  |  | append_at: A new map whose list at key k gains every item of xs at its end; an absent key starts the list. |
| `jisho::from_entries(pairs)` |  |  |  |  |
| `jisho::merge(a, b)` |  |  |  |  |

| `junjo::insert_with(cmp, x, xs)` |  |  |  | insert_with(cmp, x, xs) puts x into the list xs, sorted by cmp, before the |
| `junjo::sort_with(cmp, xs)` |  |  |  | sort_with(cmp, xs) orders xs by cmp, stably, by insertion; cmp answers -1, 0 |

| `junjo::min_by(key, xs)` |  |  |  | min_by: item with the least key. |
| `junjo::max_by(key, xs)` |  |  |  | max_by: item with the greatest key. |
| `junjo::compare_values(a, b)` |  |  |  | compare_values: The three-way comparison the rest of the package is phrased in terms of. |
| `junjo::compare_lists(a, b)` |  |  |  | compare_lists: Lexicographic order over lists: the first differing element decides, and a |
| `junjo::merge_sorted(a, b)` |  |  |  | merge_sorted: Merge two ALREADY-SORTED lists into one sorted list. |
| `junjo::merge_sorted_by(key, a, b)` |  |  |  |  |
| `junjo::is_sorted_by(key, xs)` |  |  |  |  |
| `junjo::is_sorted_desc(xs)` |  |  |  |  |

| `junjo::insert_sorted_by(key, x, xs)` |  |  |  | insert_sorted_by: Insert into a list already sorted BY KEY, before the first equal key. |
| `junjo::sort_stable_by(key, xs)` |  |  |  | sort_stable_by: A STABLE sort by key — equal keys come out in input order. |
| `junjo::merge_sort(xs)` |  |  |  | merge_sort: Merge sort: split, sort each half, merge. Also stable, since merge_sorted |
| `junjo::dedupe_sorted(xs)` |  |  |  | dedupe_sorted: Drop ADJACENT duplicates. On a sorted list that is every duplicate; on an |
| `junjo::lower_bound_from(xs, target, lo, hi)` |  |  |  |  |

| `junjo::lower_bound(xs, target)` |  |  |  | lower_bound: The FIRST index at which target could be inserted without breaking the order. |
| `junjo::upper_bound(xs, target)` |  |  |  | upper_bound: The LAST such index — one past the final occurrence of target. |
| `junjo::count_sorted(xs, target)` |  |  |  | count_sorted: How many times target occurs, in O(log n) rather than a scan. |
| `junjo::contains_sorted(xs, target)` |  |  |  |  |
| `junjo::median_of_three(a, b, c)` |  |  |  | median_of_three: The median of three values, which is the standard cheap pivot choice. |
| `junjo::nth_smallest(n, xs)` |  |  |  | nth_smallest: 0-indexed, to agree with `nth` rather than with English: nth_smallest(0, xs) |
| `junjo::nth_by(order, n, xs)` |  |  |  | nth_by(order, n, xs) is the item at index n of order(xs), or nil when n is out |
| `junjo::nth_largest(n, xs)` |  |  |  |  |
| `junjo::top_n(n, xs)` |  |  |  | top_n: The n largest, largest first. Asking for more than there is yields everything |
| `junjo::take_by(order, n, xs)` |  |  |  | take_by(order, n, xs) is the first n items of order(xs), and none when n is |
| `junjo::bottom_n(n, xs)` |  |  |  |  |
| `junjo::argsort(xs)` |  |  |  | argsort: The indices of xs in sorted order: nth(nth(0, argsort(xs)), xs) is the |
| `junjo::rank(xs)` |  |  |  | rank: Where each element LANDS in sorted order — the inverse permutation of |
| `junjo::is_strictly_sorted(xs)` |  |  |  | is_strictly_sorted: Sorted AND without duplicates. `is_sorted` says yes to [1, 1]; this does not, |
| `junjo::merge_all_sorted(xss)` |  |  |  | merge_all_sorted: k-way merge: fold the pairwise merge over a list of sorted lists. |
| `junjo::insert_lex(x, xss)` |  |  |  |  |
| `junjo::sort_lists(xss)` |  |  |  | sort_lists: Sort a list OF LISTS lexicographically. |

## junni

`junni` 0.1.0 · needs: moji, retsu

junni — the pure words of blue's full-text index (the subject of the `sakuin` verb space, docs/verbs): text analyzed into tokens, documents inverted into postings, and a question's terms ranked by BM25 over them.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `junni::flush(acc, cur)` |  |  |  | flush adds the token being read to the tokens, when there is one. |
| `junni::split_tokens(text)` |  |  |  | split_tokens cuts lower-case text at every character that is not an ASCII letter or digit. |
| `junni::analyze(text, opts)` |  |  |  | analyze: The index tokens of a text, in order: lower case, cut at every character that is not an ASCII letter or digit, the stop words dropped (`opts[:stop]` replaces STOP). |
| `junni::postings_of(by_term, id, tokens)` |  |  |  | postings_of adds one document's term counts to the postings by term. |
| `junni::invert(docs, opts)` |  |  |  | invert: An in-memory inverted index of `docs`, each `[id, text]`: per term the `[id, count]` of every document holding it, per document its token count, the document count and the mean length. `opts` is analyze's. |
| `junni::lookup(postings, term)` |  |  |  | lookup: The ids of the documents that hold `term`, after analysis, in the order they were inverted; [] for a term no document holds. |
| `junni::idf(df, n)` |  |  |  | idf is BM25's inverse document frequency of a term held by `df` of `n` documents; never negative. |
| `junni::term_score(tf, len, mean, w)` |  |  |  | term_score is one posting's BM25 contribution: its count, its document's length against the mean, and the term's idf. |
| `junni::scores_of(acc, postings, term)` |  |  |  | scores_of adds one query term's contributions to the scores by id. |
| `junni::score(postings, terms, opts)` |  |  |  | score: The documents that hold any of `terms` (each analyzed), ranked by BM25 (K1, B): `[{id, score}]`, highest first, ties by id; `opts[:limit]` keeps the first that many. |

## kaaneru

`kaaneru` 0.1.0 · needs: baito, jisho, retsu, shisutemu, shuugou

kaaneru (カーネル) — the Linux kernel, spoken from blue: maps, programs, links and ring buffers over `bpf(2)`, with every struct a `baito` layout and no C library in the path.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kaaneru::commands` |  |  |  | commands: `enum bpf_cmd`, each by its number. |
| `kaaneru::prog_kinds` |  |  |  | prog_kinds: `enum bpf_prog_type`, each by its number. |
| `kaaneru::attach_kinds` |  |  |  | attach_kinds: `enum bpf_attach_type`, each by its number. |
| `kaaneru::kind_id(table, name)` |  |  |  | kind_id: the kernel's number for a name in one of its tables, :command, :map, :prog or :attach, from `include/uapi/linux/bpf.h` at 6.16. An unknown name raises. |
| `kaaneru::errno_name(n)` |  |  |  | errno_name: the name of an errno, or its number as text when this table does not hold it. |
| `kaaneru::sleepable` |  |  |  | sleepable: `BPF_F_SLEEPABLE`, which a :syscall program must load with. |
| `kaaneru::map_create_layout` |  |  |  | map_create_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_MAP_CREATE reads it. |
| `kaaneru::map_elem_layout` |  |  |  | map_elem_layout: `union bpf_attr` as the map element commands read it. |
| `kaaneru::prog_load_layout` |  |  |  | prog_load_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_PROG_LOAD reads it, through `prog_token_fd`. |
| `kaaneru::test_run_layout` |  |  |  | test_run_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_PROG_RUN reads it. |
| `kaaneru::obj_layout` |  |  |  | obj_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_OBJ_PIN and BPF_OBJ_GET read it. |
| `kaaneru::link_layout` |  |  |  | link_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_LINK_CREATE reads it for most hooks. |
| `kaaneru::kprobe_multi_layout` |  |  |  | kprobe_multi_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_LINK_CREATE reads it for kprobe_multi. |
| `kaaneru::raw_tracepoint_layout` |  |  |  | raw_tracepoint_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_RAW_TRACEPOINT_OPEN reads it. |
| `kaaneru::info_layout` |  |  |  | info_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_OBJ_GET_INFO_BY_FD reads it. |
| `kaaneru::prog_info_layout` |  |  |  | prog_info_layout: the front of `struct bpf_prog_info`, through its name. |
| `kaaneru::seam_kind(kind)` |  |  |  | seam_kind: the width the seam writes a scalar kind at. |
| `kaaneru::field_slot(l, name, value)` |  |  |  | field_slot: one record field as the seam's `[offset, kind, value]`. |
| `kaaneru::struct_fields(l, pairs)` |  |  |  | struct_fields: a record over a layout as the seam's fields: an integer at its width, {in: bytes}, {out: capacity} and {inout: bytes} as a buffer's address, {strings: names} as an array of pointers to them, {fd: handle} as a descriptor. Absent fields stay zero. |
| `kaaneru::refused_data(answer, log)` |  |  |  | refused_data: what a kernel refusal carries: the errno, its name, the log. |
| `kaaneru::checked(answer, what, log?)` |  |  |  | checked: an answer the kernel accepted, or the refusal for one it did not. |
| `kaaneru::call_bpf(command, l, pairs, fd_result)` |  |  | `B0083` | call_bpf: one `bpf(2)` command over a layout and a record. |
| `kaaneru::btf_load_layout` |  |  |  | btf_load_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_BTF_LOAD reads it. |
| `kaaneru::load_btf(bytes, log?)` |  |  |  | load_btf: a BTF blob into the kernel: {fd:}, what `create_map` names as `{btf_object:}`. Its checker runs here; a refusal names why, the checker's log in its data. |
| `kaaneru::create_map(spec)` |  |  |  | create_map: a kernel map from a `kaaneru::bpf::declare_map` shape: the shape plus {fd:}. `{flags:}` in the shape sets `map_flags`, and `{value_type_id:, btf_object:}` name the kernel's type a :struct_ops map holds and the BTF object `load_btf` made for it. |
| `kaaneru::map_btf_fields(spec)` |  |  |  | map_btf_fields: the fields that tie a map to its types: `{btf_fd:, btf_key:, btf_value:}` in the shape, or the BTF object a struct_ops map names. |
| `kaaneru::pack_name(name)` |  |  |  | pack_name: an object name as the kernel takes it: at most 15 of the letters, digits, `_` and `.` it allows, the rest changed to `_`. |
| `kaaneru::map_put(m, key, value, flags?)` |  |  |  | map_put: a key's value set in a kernel map, both as bytes. `flags` 1 refuses an existing key and 2 a missing one. |
| `kaaneru::map_get(m, key)` |  |  |  | map_get: a key's value bytes in a kernel map, nil when the map holds none. A per-CPU map answers one value per possible CPU, joined, and `{value_bytes:}` sizes the buffer. |
| `kaaneru::map_delete(m, key)` |  |  |  | map_delete: a key taken out of a kernel map: true when it was there, false when it was not. |
| `kaaneru::next_key(m, key)` |  |  |  | next_key: the key after this one in a kernel map, nil past the last. |
| `kaaneru::map_keys(m)` |  |  |  | map_keys: every key a kernel map holds, as bytes, in the kernel's order. |
| `kaaneru::fields_bound(program, table)` |  |  |  | fields_bound: a program with its kernel fields bound from BTF, refused when it reads some and has no table. |
| `kaaneru::patched(program, maps)` |  |  | `B0083` | patched: a program's instructions with each map reference holding its descriptor's number. |
| `kaaneru::slot_count(i)` |  |  |  | slot_count: the slots an instruction takes. |
| `kaaneru::load_fields(kind, encoded, opts, log)` |  |  |  | load_fields: BPF_PROG_LOAD's record for a program's bytes. |
| `kaaneru::only_attach(kind)` |  |  |  | only_attach: the attach type a program kind must name at load because the kernel allows it no other, nil for a kind that names none or several. |
| `kaaneru::attach_number(attach)` |  |  |  | attach_number: what `expected_attach_type` holds: an attach type's number, or a :struct_ops program's member index as given. |
| `kaaneru::func_info_fields(btf_fd, rows)` |  |  |  | func_info_fields: the fields that tie a program to its BTF: each function by its slot and type. |
| `kaaneru::line_info_fields(rows)` |  |  |  | line_info_fields: the fields that give a loaded program its source lines: each row by its slot and its strings' offsets in the program's BTF. No rows, no fields. |
| `kaaneru::insn_line?(row)` |  |  |  | insn_line?: whether a verifier log line is one instruction, `12: (bf) r1 = r2`. |
| `kaaneru::verifier_said(log)` |  |  |  | verifier_said: what a verifier log says of its refusal: {insn:, reason:}, `insn` the slot it last read, nil when it read none, and `reason` the lines after it, without the source lines and tallies around them. The kernel keeps only the failing path in the log, so the last instruction is the one refused. |
| `kaaneru::source_words(at, insn)` |  |  |  | source_words: a refusal's place as a message reads it: `file:line:col`, or the slot when no source is known. |
| `kaaneru::log_text(answer)` |  |  |  | log_text: the verifier's log from an answer, empty when there is none. |
| `kaaneru::take_until_nul(bytes)` |  |  |  | take_until_nul: bytes up to the first NUL. |
| `kaaneru::load_attempt(kind, encoded, opts, log)` |  |  |  | load_attempt: one BPF_PROG_LOAD, with a log of this many bytes. |
| `kaaneru::load(program, opts)` |  |  |  | load: an assembled program into the kernel: {fd:, kind:, maps:, name:}. `opts` takes {kind:, maps:, name:, license:, attach:, attach_btf_id:, btf_fd:, func_info:, signed_division:}, `kind` a program type such as :xdp, `maps` what `create_map` made, `btf_fd` what `load_btf` made. The verifier runs here, and its log is in the refusal's data. Below 6.12 it first refuses a signed division that can reach the CPU's divide (`division_refusal`); `signed_division: :trust` turns that off for a program whose author has ruled out the lowest value by -1. |
| `kaaneru::refused_load(answer, name, program)` |  |  |  | refused_load: the refusal for a load the verifier turned down: the blue line and the reason in its message, and {insn:, reason:, at:} with the log in its data. `at` is {file:, line:, col:, text:}, nil for a program that knows no lines. |
| `kaaneru::install(compiled, opts?)` |  |  |  | install: a compiled program into the kernel: its maps created, then the program loaded against them. {prog:, maps:}, each map by its place in `compile`'s list and findable by `map_named`. `opts` adds {attach:, attach_btf_id:} for the hooks that name their target at load. |
| `kaaneru::with_program_btf(opts, program_btf, shape)` |  |  |  | with_program_btf: load options with a program's BTF, its functions and its source lines. |
| `kaaneru::load_with_lines(program, opts, compiled)` |  |  |  | load_with_lines: a refused plain program loaded again with its BTF and source lines, so the verifier's own log names blue's lines. A plain program that loads carries no BTF; this is the one path that gives it some. The answer stays the kernel's: a second refusal raises with the richer log, and an acceptance is the program. |
| `kaaneru::map_with_types(m, program_btf, shape)` |  |  |  | map_with_types: a map's shape with its BTF ids, when its program has BTF and names both for it. A plain map of such a program stays plain. |
| `kaaneru::hook_of(table, spec)` |  |  |  | hook_of: the attach type and BTF id a hook's spec names: {fentry:}, {fexit:} or {fmod_ret:} a kernel function, {lsm:} a hook, {iter:} an iterator such as "task". |
| `kaaneru::install_hook(compiled, table, spec)` |  |  |  | install_hook: a compiled :tracing or :lsm program loaded onto a kernel function and attached: `spec` {fentry: name}, {fexit: name}, {fmod_ret: name}, {lsm: hook} or {iter: name}, the target found in `table`, this kernel's BTF. {prog:, maps:, link:}. The attachment needs no netlink and no perf event. |
| `kaaneru::map_named(installed, name)` |  |  |  | map_named: the map of this name among those `install` made. |
| `kaaneru::context_fields(kind, ctx)` |  |  |  | context_fields: BPF_PROG_RUN's context fields for a program kind. A :raw_tracepoint takes its arguments in and answers none, a :syscall program's context comes back in place, and the rest name a second buffer for the answer. |
| `kaaneru::run(p, input)` |  |  |  | run: a loaded program once through BPF_PROG_RUN, attached to nothing: {ret:, packet:, ctx:, duration_ns:}. `input` takes {packet:, ctx:, repeat:}, each packet at least 14 bytes. A kernel gives no such run to an :lsm program, and runs a :tracing one by calling its own test functions. |
| `kaaneru::prog_id(p)` |  |  |  | prog_id: the kernel's id of a loaded program, what `bpftool prog show id` names. |
| `kaaneru::prog_info(p)` |  |  |  | prog_info: what the kernel holds of a loaded program: its id, name, `btf_id` and `nr_func_info` among them. |
| `kaaneru::pin(object, path)` |  |  |  | pin: a map, program or link at a path under a bpffs, where it outlives this process until the file is removed. |
| `kaaneru::open_pinned(path)` |  |  |  | open_pinned: a descriptor for what a bpffs path holds. |
| `kaaneru::map_int(live, name, key)` |  |  |  | map_int: the integer a map of an installed program holds for an integer key, nil when it holds none. Each width comes from the map's shape. `kaaneru::bpf::map_int` is the same read on the machine. |
| `kaaneru::kernel_absence` |  |  |  | kernel_absence: why a program cannot load here, nil when one can: it loads the smallest XDP program and closes it. A test passes the answer to `tesuto::skip_for`. |
| `kaaneru::hooks` |  |  |  | hooks: every hook `attached` takes, with the program kind it needs and what names its target. |
| `kaaneru::hook_row(compiled, spec)` |  |  |  | hook_row: the row of `hooks` that one hook spec names for a compiled program. A spec that names no hook or two, and a program of another kind, each raise :bad_option naming what fits. |
| `kaaneru::linked(prog, row, target)` |  |  |  | linked: a loaded program's link to the hook of `row`, for every hook that does not load onto a kernel function. |
| `kaaneru::released(live)` |  |  |  | released: an installed program's link, maps, program and BTF closed, each at most once. |
| `kaaneru::attached(compiled, spec, body)` |  |  |  | attached: `compiled` loaded onto one hook while `body` runs on its live record, {prog:, maps:, link:}; the answer is what `body` answers. Whatever `body` does, the link, the maps and the program close. `spec` is one row of `hooks`: {xdp: 3}, {tracepoint: ["sched", "sched_process_exec"]}, {lsm: "file_open"}. |
| `kaaneru::attach(p, kind, target?, opts?)` |  |  |  | attach: a loaded program to a hook through BPF_LINK_CREATE: `kind` an attach type such as :xdp or :tcx_ingress, `target` an interface index or a descriptor's holder, `opts` {flags:, target_btf_id:, cookie:}. The answer is {fd:, kind:}, and the attachment lives as long as that descriptor or its pin. |
| `kaaneru::open_cgroup(path)` |  |  | `B0083` | open_cgroup: a descriptor on a cgroup v2 directory, what a cgroup program attaches to. A path with no `cgroup.controllers` raises :cgroup_absent: cgroup v2 is not mounted there, or no such cgroup exists. |
| `kaaneru::attach_cgroup(p, kind, path)` |  |  |  | attach_cgroup: a loaded program to the cgroup at `path` through BPF_LINK_CREATE: `kind` an attach type such as :cgroup_inet_egress, :cgroup_inet_sock_create or :cgroup_device. The link holds the cgroup, so its descriptor closes here. |
| `kaaneru::netfilter_layout` |  |  |  | netfilter_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_LINK_CREATE reads it for a netfilter hook. |
| `kaaneru::netfilter_family(family)` |  |  |  | netfilter_family: the kernel's number for a netfilter family, :ipv4 or :ipv6. |
| `kaaneru::netfilter_hook(hook)` |  |  |  | netfilter_hook: the kernel's number for a netfilter hook. |
| `kaaneru::attach_netfilter(p, spec)` |  |  |  | attach_netfilter: a :netfilter program to a hook of the current network namespace: `spec` {family:, hook:, priority:}. The program answers 1 to accept a packet and 0 to drop it. |
| `kaaneru::uprobe_multi_layout` |  |  |  | uprobe_multi_layout: `union bpf_attr` as BPF_LINK_CREATE reads it for uprobe_multi. |
| `kaaneru::attach_uprobes(p, path, offsets, opts?)` |  |  |  | attach_uprobes: a kprobe program to file offsets in an executable through one uprobe_multi link, as `install` made it with {attach: :trace_uprobe_multi}. `opts` {retprobe: true} fires on return, and {pid:} holds it to one process. `entry_offset_of` finds an executable's first instruction. |
| `kaaneru::entry_offset(head, table, entry_size, count)` |  |  |  | entry_offset: the file offset of a 64-bit ELF's first instruction, from its 64-byte header and its program headers: the `PT_LOAD` segment that holds the entry address places it. An entry no segment holds raises. |
| `kaaneru::entry_offset_of(path)` |  |  |  | entry_offset_of: the file offset of an executable's first instruction, what `attach_uprobes` takes. A file that is no 64-bit ELF raises. |
| `kaaneru::iterate(link, path)` |  | yes |  | iterate: what an iterator program writes for one run: the link pinned at `path` under a bpffs, read to its end, and unpinned. `install_hook` with {iter: name} makes the link. |
| `kaaneru::attach_sampling(p, period_ns)` |  |  |  | attach_sampling: a :perf_event program run on CPU 0 every `period_ns` of its clock, through one software perf event and a link on it. `detach` ends both. |
| `kaaneru::struct_ops_flag_link` |  |  |  | struct_ops_flag_link: `BPF_F_LINK`: a :struct_ops map whose registration a link holds, so closing the link ends it. |
| `kaaneru::placed_at(bytes, at, part)` |  |  |  | placed_at: bytes with a run of them written at an offset. |
| `kaaneru::struct_ops_value(table, shape, ops, members)` |  |  |  | struct_ops_value: the bytes of a `bpf_struct_ops_<ops>` map value: each program's descriptor number at its member's offset, and each given value at its own, a text as its bytes and an integer as four. |
| `kaaneru::install_struct_ops(table, spec)` |  |  | `B0083` | install_struct_ops: a kernel operations table implemented in blue, registered through one link, its map naming a one-type BTF object since the kernel asks for one: `spec` {ops:, name:, programs:, values:}. `ops` names the kernel's struct such as "tcp_congestion_ops", `programs` pairs each member with what `compile` made for kind :struct_ops, and `values` pairs each other member with its text or integer. The maps the programs name are made once and shared. {maps:, programs:, table:, link:}; `detach` of the link unregisters it. A program needs no BTF of its own: a member's types come from the kernel's. |
| `kaaneru::unique_by_name(specs)` |  |  |  | unique_by_name: map shapes with each name once, the first of each kept. |
| `kaaneru::attach_kprobes(p, symbols, retprobe?)` |  |  |  | attach_kprobes: a kprobe program to kernel functions by name through one kprobe_multi link, on their return when `retprobe` is true. The program loads with {attach: :trace_kprobe_multi}, and a kernel without FPROBE answers EOPNOTSUPP. |
| `kaaneru::perf_attr_layout` |  |  |  | perf_attr_layout: the front of `struct perf_event_attr`, through `config2`. |
| `kaaneru::tracepoint_id(category, name)` |  |  |  | tracepoint_id: a kernel tracepoint's number from tracefs, what perf_event_open takes. |
| `kaaneru::attach_tracepoint(p, category, name)` |  |  |  | attach_tracepoint: a tracepoint program to `category/name`, through one perf event and a link on it. A tracepoint program runs for the whole system whichever CPU its event names, and a second event for the same program is refused with EEXIST. `detach` ends both. |
| `kaaneru::attach_event(p, fields)` |  |  | `B0083` | attach_event: a program linked to a new perf event, the event kept for `detach`. |
| `kaaneru::pmu_type(pmu)` |  |  |  | pmu_type: a perf event source's type number, from sysfs. |
| `kaaneru::retprobe_bit(format)` |  |  |  | retprobe_bit: the `config` bit a kprobe or uprobe PMU reads as "on return", from its format text. |
| `kaaneru::attach_kprobe(p, symbol, retprobe?)` |  |  |  | attach_kprobe: a kprobe program to one kernel function by name, on its return when `retprobe` is true, through a perf event and a link. It needs no FPROBE, which `attach_kprobes` does, and a NixOS 25.11 kernel leaves FPROBE off. |
| `kaaneru::attach_raw_tracepoint(p, name?)` |  |  |  | attach_raw_tracepoint: a raw tracepoint or BTF-enabled program by its tracepoint's name; for an fentry, fexit or LSM program the name is nil and the kernel takes the target from the program's BTF id. |
| `kaaneru::detach(link)` |  |  | `B0083` | detach: an attachment ended, by closing its descriptors. |
| `kaaneru::close_object(object)` |  |  | `B0083` | close_object: a map or program's descriptor closed now, rather than when its last reference goes. |
| `kaaneru::open_events(m)` |  |  | `B0083` | open_events: a consumer of a ring buffer map, whose `max_entries` is its size in bytes. |
| `kaaneru::next_event(ring, timeout_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | next_event: the next record's bytes from `open_events`, waiting at most `timeout_ms`; nil when none came. |
| `kaaneru::drain(ring)` |  |  | `B0083` | drain: every record a consumer has now, oldest first. |
| `kaaneru::kernel_release` |  |  |  | kernel_release: the running kernel's release, from procfs. |
| `kaaneru::division_traps?(release)` |  |  |  | division_traps?: whether a kernel of this release lets `sdiv` or `smod` of the lowest value by -1 reach the CPU's divide, which traps and panics the kernel. Measured: 6.6.142 does, in a machine, and 6.12.93 does not, on plo. Every line between is out of support and counts as trapping. The fix is 7dd34d7b, "bpf: Fix a sdiv overflow issue", in 6.12. |
| `kaaneru::division_site?(i)` |  |  |  | division_site?: whether an instruction is a signed division or remainder whose divisor can be -1: a register, or the immediate -1. |
| `kaaneru::division_refusal(insns, release, mode)` |  |  |  | division_refusal: why this kernel must not load these instructions, or nil. A signed division with a divisor that can be -1 traps the CPU on a kernel below the fix, and the kernel panics. Pure: `mode` is `load`'s `signed_division`, and :trust turns the refusal off. |
| `kaaneru::refuse_division(insns, mode)` |  |  |  | refuse_division: raise :division_trap when `division_refusal` has a reason. The release is read only for a program that holds such a division. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::op_table(rows, name, what)` |  |  |  | op_table: the number a name has in a table of rows, raising on a name the table lacks. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sizes` |  |  |  | sizes: the access sizes and the bits each sets in an opcode. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu_ops` |  |  |  | alu_ops: the ALU operations and the bits each sets in an opcode. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::regular_ops` |  |  |  | regular_ops: the ALU operations that take a register or an immediate in either width: every operation but the sign extending moves. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu_offsets` |  |  |  | alu_offsets: the ALU operations the offset field tells apart from their plain forms. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump_ops` |  |  |  | jump_ops: the jump conditions and the bits each sets in an opcode. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::atomic_ops` |  |  |  | atomic_ops: the atomic operations and the immediate each is. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper_names` |  |  |  | helper_names: the kernel's helper functions in the order of their numbers, from `__BPF_FUNC_MAPPER` in `include/uapi/linux/bpf.h` at 6.16. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper_id(name)` |  |  |  | helper_id: the number of a kernel helper, raising on a name the kernel does not have. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::r(n)` |  |  |  | r: register n as an operand, where a bare integer is an immediate. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::reg_of(x)` |  |  |  | reg_of: a register's number from `r(n)` or a bare number. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::reg?(x)` |  |  |  | reg?: whether an operand is a register rather than an immediate. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::imm32_of(v)` |  |  |  | imm32_of: an immediate, raising on one that takes more than 32 bits. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::insn(code, dst, src, off, imm)` |  |  |  | insn: one instruction from its five fields. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu_at(class, op, dst, operand)` |  |  |  | alu_at: an ALU instruction in a class, 7 for 64-bit and 4 for 32-bit. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu(op, dst, operand)` |  |  |  | alu: a 64-bit arithmetic or logic instruction: add sub mul div or and lsh rsh mod xor mov arsh sdiv smod movsx8 movsx16 movsx32. The operand is `r(n)` or an immediate. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu32(op, dst, operand)` |  |  |  | alu32: the 32-bit form of `alu`, whose result clears the register's upper half. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::neg(dst, bits?)` |  |  |  | neg: the arithmetic negation of a register, over 64 bits, or over 32 when `bits` says so. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::swap_bits(bits)` |  |  |  | swap_bits: a byte swap's width, raising on one that is not 16, 32 or 64. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::swap(order, dst, bits)` |  |  |  | swap: a register's byte order changed at 16, 32 or 64 bits: `:be` to big-endian, `:le` to little-endian, `:any` reversed whatever the host's order, the last from cpu v4. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump_at(class, op, dst, operand, target)` |  |  |  | jump_at: a jump instruction in a class, 5 for 64-bit and 6 for 32-bit. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump(op, dst, operand, target)` |  |  |  | jump: go to `target`, a label or an offset, when a 64-bit condition holds: eq gt ge set ne sgt sge lt le slt sle. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump32(op, dst, operand, target)` |  |  |  | jump32: the form of `jump` that compares the low 32 bits. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ja(target, wide?)` |  |  |  | ja: go to `target` always; `wide` true gives the 32-bit offset of cpu v4. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::call(helper)` |  |  |  | call: a kernel helper by name or number. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::call_local(target)` |  |  |  | call_local: a subprogram of this program at a label. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::func_ref(dst, target)` |  |  |  | func_ref: a subprogram of this program at a label, into a register, as a callback's argument. A callback needs its program's BTF. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::call_kfunc(btf_id)` |  |  |  | call_kfunc: a kernel function by its BTF id. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exit_program` |  |  |  | exit_program: return to the caller, with r0 the value. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::size_code(size)` |  |  |  | size_code: the bits an access size sets in an opcode. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load(size, dst, base, off)` |  |  |  | load: a value of size w, h, b or dw from memory at a register plus an offset, zero extended. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load_signed(size, dst, base, off)` |  |  |  | load_signed: a value of size w, h or b from memory, sign extended, an instruction from cpu v4. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::store(size, base, off, value)` |  |  |  | store: a register or an immediate to memory at a register plus an offset. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::atomic(op, size, base, off, src)` |  |  |  | atomic: an atomic read-modify-write of size w or dw: add or and xor fetch_add fetch_or fetch_and fetch_xor xchg cmpxchg. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::imm64(dst, value)` |  |  |  | imm64: a whole 64-bit value into a register, the one instruction that takes two slots. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::wide_ref(dst, source, key, value)` |  |  |  | wide_ref: a two-slot load whose value the loader or the assembler fills in, marked with what it names. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_ref(dst, name)` |  |  |  | map_ref: the map named `name` into a register, resolved when the program is loaded. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_value_ref(dst, name, off)` |  |  |  | map_value_ref: the address of a place in the value of an array map named `name`, resolved when the program is loaded. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load_abs(size, off)` |  |  |  | load_abs: the legacy read of a packet at an absolute offset into r0, for socket filters. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load_ind(size, base, off)` |  |  |  | load_ind: the legacy read of a packet at a register plus an offset into r0, for socket filters. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::label(name)` |  |  |  | label: a position other instructions name. It takes no slot. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::wide?(i)` |  |  |  | wide?: whether an instruction takes two slots. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::slots_of(i)` |  |  |  | slots_of: the slots an item takes: two for `imm64`, none for a label or a source line mark. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::line_mark(line, col)` |  |  |  | line_mark: a position in source the following instructions came from. It takes no slot. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::flat_items(items)` |  |  |  | flat_items: nested lists of items as one list. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::label_table(items)` |  |  |  | label_table: each label's slot, and the slot past the last item. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::target_offset(labels, target, pc)` |  |  |  | target_offset: a jump's offset from its target, a label or already an offset. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::offset16(n)` |  |  |  | offset16: an offset, raising on one past the 16 bits a jump holds. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::resolved(i, labels, pc)` |  |  |  | resolved: an instruction with its target written into its offset. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::assemble(items)` |  |  |  | assemble: instructions and labels, nested lists flattened, to a program: labels resolved to offsets, each map reference recorded as a relocation {at:, map:}, `at` counted in slots, and each `line_mark` recorded in `lines` as {at:, line:, col:}. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::slot_bytes(code, dst, src, off, imm)` |  |  |  | slot_bytes: one 8-byte slot from its five fields. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::encode_insn(i)` |  |  |  | encode_insn: one instruction's bytes: 8, or 16 for `imm64`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::encode(program)` |  |  |  | encode: an assembled program's bytes, as the kernel takes them. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::slot_at(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | slot_at: the slot at a byte position, as an instruction. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::decode(bytes)` |  |  |  | decode: instructions from their bytes, `imm64`'s two slots read as one. A length that is no whole number of slots raises :decode_error. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::decode_slot(bytes, count, acc)` |  |  |  | decode_slot: one step of `decode`: the next instruction, and where the one after starts. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::class_of(code)` |  |  |  | class_of: an opcode's class. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::source_reg?(code)` |  |  |  | source_reg?: whether an opcode takes a register as its source. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::named_of(rows, value)` |  |  |  | named_of: the name a table gives a number. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu_op_of(i)` |  |  |  | alu_op_of: the operation an ALU instruction performs, its offset field read. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu_sign(op)` |  |  |  | alu_sign: the assignment operator LLVM writes for an ALU operation. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump_sign(op)` |  |  |  | jump_sign: the comparison LLVM writes for a jump condition. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::signed_text(n)` |  |  |  | signed_text: an offset as LLVM writes it inside a memory operand. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::relative_text(n)` |  |  |  | relative_text: a jump's offset as LLVM writes it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::access_name(code)` |  |  |  | access_name: the C type LLVM writes for an opcode's access size. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::access_bytes(code)` |  |  |  | access_bytes: the bytes an opcode's access size takes. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu_text(i)` |  |  |  | alu_text: an ALU instruction as LLVM writes it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump_text(i)` |  |  |  | jump_text: a jump, call or exit as LLVM writes it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::atomic_text(i)` |  |  |  | atomic_text: an atomic instruction as LLVM writes it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::text(given)` |  |  |  | text: an instruction as `llvm-mc` writes BPF assembly, which is how the encoder is checked against LLVM. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sized_word(code)` |  |  |  | sized_word: the cast LLVM writes for a legacy packet read. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::disassemble(bytes)` |  |  |  | disassemble: a program's bytes as lines of `text`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::mask32` |  |  |  | mask32: the low 32 bits set. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::low32(v)` |  |  |  | low32: a value's low 32 bits, as an unsigned number. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sign32(v)` |  |  |  | sign32: a value's low 32 bits, read as signed. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sign_from(v, bits)` |  |  |  | sign_from: a value's low bits, read as signed. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::signed_div(a, b)` |  |  |  | signed_div: the quotient RFC 9669 states for `sdiv`, truncating, zero for a zero divisor. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::magnitude(a)` |  |  |  | magnitude: a value's distance from zero, the most negative value itself. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::signed_mod(a, b)` |  |  |  | signed_mod: the remainder RFC 9669 states for `smod`, taking the dividend's sign. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu64_value(op, a, b)` |  |  |  | alu64_value: what a 64-bit ALU operation answers. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::alu32_value(op, a, b)` |  |  |  | alu32_value: what a 32-bit ALU operation answers, its upper half clear. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::byte_swapped(v, bits)` |  |  |  | byte_swapped: a value's low bytes in reverse order. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::truncated(v, bits)` |  |  |  | truncated: a value's low bits. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::jump_taken?(op, a, b)` |  |  |  | jump_taken?: whether a jump condition holds. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::stack_top` |  |  |  | stack_top: the address the machine's frame pointer starts at. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ctx_base` |  |  |  | ctx_base: the address the machine holds a program's context at. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_base` |  |  |  | packet_base: the address the machine's packet region starts at. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_base` |  |  |  | map_base: the address the machine's map values start at. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::in_region?(st, addr, count)` |  |  |  | in_region?: whether a machine's memory holds these bytes. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::read_mem(st, addr, count)` |  |  |  | read_mem: an integer from a machine's memory, a fault outside every region. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::write_mem(st, addr, count, value)` |  |  |  | write_mem: an integer into a machine's memory, a fault outside every region. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::write_bytes_at(st, addr, bytes)` |  |  |  | write_bytes_at: bytes into a machine's memory. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::read_bytes_at(st, addr, count)` |  |  |  | read_bytes_at: bytes from a machine's memory. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::add_region(st, from, count)` |  |  |  | add_region: a machine with one more range of addresses it may touch. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::reg_value(st, n)` |  |  |  | reg_value: a register's value in a machine. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::operand_of(st, i)` |  |  |  | operand_of: an instruction's source: a register's value or its immediate. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::advance(st, n)` |  |  |  | advance: a machine with its program counter moved on. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_alu(st, i)` |  |  |  | exec_alu: a machine after one ALU instruction. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_jump(st, i)` |  |  |  | exec_jump: a machine after one jump, call or exit. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::low32_for(op, v)` |  |  |  | low32_for: a 32-bit jump's operand, signed when its condition is. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_load(st, i)` |  |  |  | exec_load: a machine after one load. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_store(st, i)` |  |  |  | exec_store: a machine after one store. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_atomic(st, i)` |  |  |  | exec_atomic: a machine after one atomic instruction. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_exit(st)` |  |  |  | exec_exit: a machine after an exit: done at the top frame, back in its caller otherwise. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::entered_frame(st, back, target)` |  |  |  | entered_frame: a machine inside a new frame: a fresh stack, the callee-saved registers kept, and the program counter at the target. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::exec_call(st, i)` |  |  |  | exec_call: a machine after one call: a subprogram entered, or a helper answered and r1 to r5 cleared. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::arg(st, n)` |  |  |  | arg: a helper's argument, by its register. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_of(st, handle)` |  |  |  | map_of: the map a machine holds under a handle, a fault when it holds none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::key_text(bytes)` |  |  |  | key_text: a map key's bytes as the text the machine indexes by. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::array_kind?(m)` |  |  |  | array_kind?: whether a map holds a slot for every index. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::value_of(st, m, key)` |  |  |  | value_of: the address of a key's value in a machine's map, 0 when it holds none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper_lookup(st)` |  |  |  | helper_lookup: the machine's `map_lookup_elem`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::fresh_value(st, m)` |  |  |  | fresh_value: a new place for a map value, and the machine that holds it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper_update(st)` |  |  |  | helper_update: the machine's `map_update_elem`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::size_of_keys(st, m)` |  |  |  | size_of_keys: how many keys a machine's map holds. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper_delete(st)` |  |  |  | helper_delete: the machine's `map_delete_elem`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::next_random(st)` |  |  |  | next_random: the machine's `get_prandom_u32`, drawn from its seed so a run repeats. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::adjust_head(st)` |  |  |  | adjust_head: the machine's `xdp_adjust_head`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ring_output(st)` |  |  |  | ring_output: the machine's `ringbuf_output`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ring_reserve(st)` |  |  |  | ring_reserve: the machine's `ringbuf_reserve`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ring_submit(st)` |  |  |  | ring_submit: the machine's `ringbuf_submit`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::read_kernel(st)` |  |  |  | read_kernel: the machine's `probe_read_kernel`, -14 for an address it does not hold. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::spin_lock_taken(st)` |  |  |  | spin_lock_taken: a machine holding the lock r1 names. One program runs at a time here, so the lock is never contended; a second lock is a fault, as the verifier refuses one. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::spin_lock_given(st)` |  |  |  | spin_lock_given: a machine that gave up the lock r1 names. A lock it does not hold is a fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper_loop(st)` |  |  |  | helper_loop: `bpf_loop`: the callback in r2 run with an index and r3, until it answers 1 or r1 is reached; r0 is the runs made. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::loop_turn(acc, call_at)` |  |  |  | loop_turn: one run of a `bpf_loop` callback, from its frame to its return. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::model_helper(st, name)` |  |  |  | model_helper: the machine after one helper, a fault for a helper it does not model. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::step(st, by_slot)` |  |  |  | step: a machine after its next instruction. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::slot_table(insns)` |  |  |  | slot_table: each instruction by the slot it starts at. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::skb_kinds` |  |  |  | skb_kinds: the program types whose context is a `__sk_buff`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::network_layer?(kind)` |  |  |  | network_layer?: whether the kernel hands this program type a packet from its network header: its Ethernet header is pulled first, so a program sees 14 bytes less and an IPv4 header at offset 0. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_view(kind, frame)` |  |  |  | packet_view: the bytes of a frame that a program of this type sees. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::data_fields(kind)` |  |  |  | data_fields: where a machine holds a packet's `data` and `data_end` for this program type. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_ready(st, packet, kind?)` |  |  |  | packet_ready: a machine holding a packet behind the context its program type reads: `xdp_md` for XDP, a `__sk_buff` with its `len` and `protocol` for a socket buffer's. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::skb_ready(st, kind, frame, count)` |  |  |  | skb_ready: a machine whose `__sk_buff` holds the frame's length and protocol, as the kernel's test run fills them. Any other context is as it was. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ctx_ready(st, ctx)` |  |  |  | ctx_ready: a machine holding a program's context, with r1 at it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_ready(st, spec, handle)` |  |  |  | map_ready: a machine with one more map. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::with_relocs(insns, relocs, maps)` |  |  |  | with_relocs: the slot table with each map reference holding its map's handle. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::machine(program, input?)` |  |  |  | machine: a machine ready to run a program: {packet:} gives a program the frame through the context its type reads (`{kind:}` or the program's own, XDP when neither says), {ctx:} gives any other program its context bytes, {maps:} the map specs `declare_map` makes, and {seed:, now_ns:, pid_tgid:, uid_gid:} what the helpers read. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::declare_map(name, kind, key_size, value_size, max_entries)` |  |  |  | declare_map: a map's shape, as the machine and the kernel loader both read it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::declare_locked(name, kind, key_size, max_entries)` |  |  |  | declare_locked: a map whose every value is a spin lock and one 8-byte integer, the shape `locked_add` takes. It needs its program's BTF, which `install` makes. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_of(st)` |  |  |  | packet_of: the packet as the machine holds it now, from its `data` pointer to its `data_end`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_value(st, name, key)` |  |  |  | map_value: the value bytes a machine's map holds for a key, nil when it holds none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_int(st, name, key)` |  |  |  | map_int: the integer a machine's map holds for an integer key, nil when it holds none. `kaaneru::map_int` is the same read in the kernel. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::run(program, input?)` |  |  |  | run: a program to completion on a fresh machine: the machine at its end, with `ret` the value in r0. A step past `max_steps` (a million, or {max_steps:}) and a fault each raise :bpf_fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::tick(st, table, limit)` |  |  |  | tick: one counted step of `run`, a fault past the limit. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::compiled_only(word)` |  |  |  | compiled_only: the refusal a kernel word answers when it is called in the interpreter. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::xdp_aborted` |  |  |  | xdp_aborted: the XDP action that drops a packet and records a trace event. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::xdp_drop` |  |  |  | xdp_drop: the XDP action that drops a packet. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::xdp_pass` |  |  |  | xdp_pass: the XDP action that hands a packet to the network stack. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::xdp_tx` |  |  |  | xdp_tx: the XDP action that sends a packet back out of the interface it came in on. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_len(_ctx)` |  |  |  | packet_len: inside a compiled program, the packet's length in bytes. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_int(_ctx, _off, _size)` |  |  |  | packet_int: inside a compiled program, the `size`-byte integer (1, 2, 4 or 8) at an offset in the packet, in network order. An offset past the packet is a fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_put(_ctx, _off, _size, _value)` |  |  |  | packet_put: inside a compiled program, the `size`-byte integer written at an offset in the packet, in network order. An offset past the packet is a fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ctx_int(_ctx, _off, _size)` |  |  |  | ctx_int: inside a compiled program, the `size`-byte integer at a literal offset in the program's own context: a tracepoint's fields, a socket buffer's. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_get(_m, _key)` |  |  |  | map_get: inside a compiled program, a key's value in a map, nil when the map holds none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_put(_m, _key, _value)` |  |  |  | map_put: inside a compiled program, a key's value set in a map. A full map or an index past an array is a fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_add(_m, _key, _n)` |  |  |  | map_add: inside a compiled program, `n` added atomically to a key's value, an absent hash key starting from zero. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_delete(_m, _key)` |  |  |  | map_delete: inside a compiled program, a key taken out of a map: true when it was there. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::emit(_m, _values)` |  |  |  | emit: inside a compiled program, a record of 64-bit values sent through a ring buffer map. A full buffer drops the record. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::locked_add(_m, _key, _n)` |  |  |  | locked_add: in a compiled program, add to a `declare_locked` map's integer under its spin lock, wrapping, and answer the new total; a key the map does not hold is a fault. In the interpreter it raises. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::kernel_read(_pointer, _struct, _path, _size)` |  |  |  | kernel_read: inside a compiled program, the `size`-byte field a kernel pointer's struct holds, its offset taken from the running kernel's BTF when the program loads. A failed read or a field of another size on this kernel is a fault or a refusal, never a wrong value. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::helper(_name, _a?, _b?, _c?, _d?, _e?)` |  |  |  | helper: inside a compiled program, a kernel helper by name with integer arguments: its integer answer. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::refuse(what)` |  |  |  | refuse: the compile error that names what this position has no lowering for. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::out(st, items)` |  |  |  | out: a compiler state with more code. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::typed(st, t, rng?)` |  |  |  | typed: a compiler state with the type and range of the value in r0. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::slot_offset(slot)` |  |  |  | slot_offset: a stack slot's offset from the frame pointer. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::take_slot(st)` |  |  |  | take_slot: a compiler state with one more stack slot, refused past the verifier's 64. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::bump(st, key)` |  |  |  | bump: a record with one count raised. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::fresh(st)` |  |  |  | fresh: a compiler state with one more label. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::label_name(st)` |  |  |  | label_name: the name of the newest label. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::faulting(st)` |  |  |  | faulting: a compiler state whose program needs its fault path. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::fault_label(st)` |  |  |  | fault_label: the label a fault jumps to in the function being compiled. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::name_of(sym)` |  |  |  | name_of: a symbol's name. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::head_name(form)` |  |  |  | head_name: the name a form starts with, nil for anything else. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::short_name(qualified)` |  |  |  | short_name: a resolved name without its package. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::word_of(name)` |  |  |  | word_of: the word a resolved name is in this package, nil for another's. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::literal_of(st, form)` |  |  |  | literal_of: the integer a form is known to be when it compiles, nil when it is not known. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::or_empty(s)` |  |  |  | or_empty: a string, or an empty one for nil. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::constant_of(word)` |  |  |  | constant_of: the number of an action word. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load_constant(reg, value)` |  |  |  | load_constant: the instruction that puts an integer in a register. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::local_of(st, name)` |  |  |  | local_of: what the compiler holds for a local. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::env_with(st, name, place)` |  |  |  | env_with: a compiler state with one local bound. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::value_type(place)` |  |  |  | value_type: the type of a local. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load_local(st, place, _name)` |  |  |  | load_local: a compiler state with a local read into r0. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_symbol(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_symbol: a name lowered to its local, constant or truth value. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::need_int(st, what)` |  |  |  | need_int: a compiler state whose value is an integer, refused for one that may be absent. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::simple?(st, form)` |  |  |  | simple?: whether a form loads into a register with no other register touched. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::load_simple(st, form, reg)` |  |  |  | load_simple: a simple form loaded into a named register. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::two_operands(st, a, b, what)` |  |  |  | two_operands: two integer forms lowered, the first into r0 and the second into r1, with their ranges. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::small_enough?(rng)` |  |  |  | small_enough?: whether a range is known and small enough to add without care. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::added_range(a, b)` |  |  |  | added_range: the range of a sum, nil when it is not known. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::subtracted_range(a, b)` |  |  |  | subtracted_range: the range of a difference, nil when it is not known. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::multiplied_range(a, b)` |  |  |  | multiplied_range: the range of a product, nil when it is not known. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::proven(st)` |  |  |  | proven: a compiler state that counts one check proven away. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::checked_count(st)` |  |  |  | checked_count: a compiler state that counts one check emitted. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::overflow_check(st, code)` |  |  |  | overflow_check: a compiler state with an overflow test on r3 going to the fault path. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_add(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_add: `+`, its overflow check emitted unless both ranges prove it away. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_sub(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_sub: `-`, its overflow check emitted unless both ranges prove it away. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_mul(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_mul: `*` of operands whose ranges prove it fits, refused otherwise since a kernel program cannot check it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_mod(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_mod: `%` by a divisor known to be positive, keeping blue's never-negative answer. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_plain(st, form, op)` |  |  |  | gen_plain: a word that lowers to one ALU operation and needs no check. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_bit_and(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_bit_and: `bit_and`, a non-negative literal mask bounding the answer. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_shift(st, form, op, word)` |  |  |  | gen_shift: a shift, its distance checked unless its range proves it under 64. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_unsigned_divide(st, form, op, word)` |  |  |  | gen_unsigned_divide: an unsigned division, a zero divisor a fault as it is a raise in blue. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_compare(st, form, cond)` |  |  |  | gen_compare: a comparison, true or false in r0. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::two_operands_any(st, a, b)` |  |  |  | two_operands_any: two forms of one type lowered, the first into r0 and the second into r1. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::nil_test_of(form)` |  |  |  | nil_test_of: what a form tests for absence, nil when it is no such test. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_nil_test(st, target)` |  |  |  | gen_nil_test: `.nil?` on a local: its flag read, false for a value that is never absent. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_and(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_and: `and`, short-circuiting over true and false. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_or(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_or: `or`: short-circuiting over true and false, or a default for a value that may be absent. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_not(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_not: `not` over true and false. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::narrowing(st, cond, truth)` |  |  |  | narrowing: a compiler state where a condition proves its locals present. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::narrowed_names(cond, truth)` |  |  |  | narrowed_names: the locals a condition proves present when it has this truth. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::join_types(a, b)` |  |  |  | join_types: the type of an `if` from its arms, a loop's jump taking the other's. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::join_ranges(a, b, ta, tb)` |  |  |  | join_ranges: the range of an `if` from its arms. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_if(st, form, tail)` |  |  |  | gen_if: `if`, each arm narrowed by what its condition proves. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_begin(st, forms, tail)` |  |  |  | gen_begin: a sequence: the last form's value, and the tail position. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_define(st, form)` |  |  |  | gen_define: `define` of a local, rebinding in place when it exists. |
