| `kaaneru::bpf::bind(st, name)` |  |  |  | bind: the value in r0 bound to a name. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_call(st, name, args, tail)` |  |  |  | gen_call: a call to a definition: inlined, a loop in tail position, refused when it recurses otherwise. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::argument_held(st, arg)` |  |  |  | argument_held: one argument of a global subprogram, its value kept in a stack slot; a context takes no slot. It adds to `:held_args`, `{t:, slot:}` for each. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::argument_spilled(value)` |  |  |  | argument_spilled: the value in r0 kept in a new slot as the next argument. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::argument_loaded(row, reg)` |  |  |  | argument_loaded: the instruction that puts a held argument in its register. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_label(name)` |  |  |  | sub_label: the label a global subprogram starts at. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_global_call(st, name, f, args)` |  |  |  | gen_global_call: a call to a definition the options name global: its arguments in r1 up, then a real call. A subprogram that can fault gets one more argument, a pointer to a slot it sets, and the caller then faults too. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_compiled(st, name, f, kinds)` |  |  |  | sub_compiled: a compiler state holding a global subprogram's code, compiled once in a frame of its own. Its parameters' kinds are those of its first call; a later call of other kinds is refused. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_frame(params, kinds)` |  |  |  | sub_frame: the place each parameter of a global subprogram has, and what its first instructions keep: {env:, top:, keep:}. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::frame_bytes(slots)` |  |  |  | frame_bytes: the stack the verifier counts for a frame of this many slots, each frame at least 32 bytes and rounded up to 32. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_built(st, name, f, kinds)` |  |  |  | sub_built: a global subprogram compiled: its body in a fresh frame, and its record in `:subs`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_entered(st, name, env, top, fault_to)` |  |  |  | sub_entered: a compiler state at the start of a subprogram's body: a fresh frame with these locals, its own fault label, and only itself to tail-call. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_recorded(st, body, record)` |  |  |  | sub_recorded: a compiler state holding a compiled subprogram's record, with the counts its body raised. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::max_loops` |  |  |  | max_loops: the most turns `bpf_loop` takes, the kernel's `BPF_MAX_LOOPS`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::loop_fold(n, init, step)` |  |  |  | loop_fold: `step(i, acc)` run for each `i` from 0 below `n`, each answer the next `acc`, the last the answer. In a kernel program it is `bpf_loop` with `step` a callback, in the interpreter a fold, and both raise past `bpf_loop`'s limit. `step` is a top-level definition of two parameters, and in a kernel program it reaches maps and locals, never the packet. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::callback_built(st, name, f)` |  |  |  | callback_built: a `loop_fold` step compiled as a static function: `i` in r1 and a pointer to the caller's `[acc, fault]` in r2. Its answer goes to `acc` and it returns 0; a fault sets `fault` and returns 1, which stops the loop. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::callback_ready(st, step)` |  |  |  | callback_ready: a compiler state holding the callback `step` names, compiled once. A definition cannot be both a global subprogram and a callback, since it has one signature. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_loop_fold(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_loop_fold: `loop_fold`: the turns checked against `bpf_loop`'s limit, the value and fault flag in two adjacent slots the callback writes through a pointer, then the helper. A count past the limit and a fault in the step are this program's fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::stack_checked(own, below)` |  |  |  | stack_checked: a frame's total with the deepest chain below it, refused past the 512 bytes the verifier gives one chain of calls. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_inline(st, name, f, args)` |  |  |  | gen_inline: a definition's body in place, its parameters bound to the arguments. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_tail_call(st, f, args)` |  |  |  | gen_tail_call: a call to itself in tail position: the parameters rebound and a jump back. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_named(st, form)` |  |  |  | map_named: the map a form names, refused for anything not declared at the top level. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::scalar_size(count)` |  |  |  | scalar_size: the access size of a key or value this many bytes wide. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::key_on_stack(st, form)` |  |  |  | key_on_stack: a key lowered onto the stack, where a helper reads it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::fp_at(reg, at)` |  |  |  | fp_at: the instructions that point a register at a stack slot. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_map_get(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_map_get: `map_get`: the value in r0 and whether it is present in r1. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_map_put(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_map_put: `map_put`, a failed update a fault. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_map_add(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_map_add: `map_add`: an atomic add, an absent key inserted. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_locked_add(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_locked_add: `locked_add`: the value found, its spin lock taken, its integer added to, the lock given back, and the total in r0. r7 and r8 are the two registers a helper leaves alone that nothing else here uses. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_map_delete(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_map_delete: `map_delete`: true when the key was there. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_emit(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_emit: `emit`: a record of values on the stack, sent through the ring buffer. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_helper(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_helper: `helper`: a kernel helper by name, its arguments loaded into r1 to r5. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::literal_text(form, what)` |  |  |  | literal_text: a form that must be a literal string, refused otherwise. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::literal_path(form)` |  |  |  | literal_path: a field's path, a literal list of names. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_kernel_read(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_kernel_read: `kernel_read`: the field's offset left to be bound, then `probe_read_kernel`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_ctx_int(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_ctx_int: `ctx_int`, at a literal offset as the verifier requires. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::unsigned_range(count)` |  |  |  | unsigned_range: the range an unsigned integer of this many bytes has, nil for 8. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_bounds(st, off_form, count, kind)` |  |  |  | packet_bounds: the bounds check a packet access needs, leaving its address in r2. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::packet_fields(kind)` |  |  |  | packet_fields: where a program type's context holds `data` and `data_end`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_packet_int(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_packet_int: `packet_int`: a bounds check, a load, a swap from network order. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_packet_put(st, args)` |  |  |  | gen_packet_put: `packet_put`: a bounds check, a swap to network order, a store. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_packet_len(st)` |  |  |  | gen_packet_len: `packet_len`: `data_end` less `data`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_word(st, word, args)` |  |  |  | gen_word: one of this package's kernel words, refused for one with no lowering. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_at(st, form, tail)` |  |  |  | gen_at: a statement `blue ast --lines` marked, its code placed at its source line. The code after it is back at the line around it, so an inlined call does not move its caller. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen_list(st, form, tail)` |  |  |  | gen_list: a call form, by its head. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::gen(st, form, tail)` |  |  |  | gen: any form lowered, its value in r0 and its type in the state. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::declared_map_of(form)` |  |  |  | declared_map_of: the map a top-level form declares, nil for any other form. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::constant_value(consts, form)` |  |  |  | constant_value: the integer a top-level form is, nil when it is not one. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::plain_params(signature)` |  |  |  | plain_params: a definition's parameter names, a typed parameter by its name. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::collect_top(acc, form)` |  |  |  | collect_top: one top-level form added to what the compiler knows. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::entry_of(top, wanted)` |  |  |  | entry_of: the definition to compile by its short name, refused when there is none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::names_of(forms)` |  |  |  | names_of: the name of each function a file's forms define. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::start_state(kind)` |  |  |  | start_state: a compiler state with nothing lowered yet, the program's first instruction keeping the context in r6. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::form_of(text)` |  |  |  | form_of: the first form in text, as the compiler reads it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::with_map(body)` |  |  |  | with_map: what a body of resolved text answers on the machine, with a hash map `%root/M` declared and bound. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::source_form(forms)` |  |  |  | source_form: the file a marked tree names and its lines, from its `(%source PATH LINE ...)` form; nil for a tree with none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::compile_forms(forms, opts)` |  |  |  | compile_forms: a resolved program's forms to a kernel program: its assembled instructions, its maps, its functions, and a `report` counting the safety checks proven away and the ones emitted. `opts` takes {entry:, kind:, on_fault:, global:}: the definition to compile, the program type, the value returned on a fault, which is what a raise is at this position, and the definitions to make real global subprograms, each verified alone, where every other call inlines. A construct the position has no lowering for raises :bpf_compile naming it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sub_params(s)` |  |  |  | sub_params: a subprogram's parameter kinds as BTF will say them, its fault pointer last. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::global_names(top, entry, wanted)` |  |  |  | global_names: the resolved names of the definitions the options make global, refusing the entry and a name with no definition. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::with_funcs(program, rows)` |  |  |  | with_funcs: an assembled program told which of its labels start functions, the first at the start: each row {name:, label:, linkage:, params:, ret:}, a :static one being a callback's. The program's BTF follows from this. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_kinds` |  |  |  | map_kinds: `enum bpf_map_type`, each by its number. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::needs_btf?(program)` |  |  |  | needs_btf?: whether a program's load needs its BTF: more than one function, a callback, or a map with a spin lock. A plain program loads with none, as it always has. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ctx_struct(kind)` |  |  |  | ctx_struct: the kernel's name and size for the context of a program type, and an XDP context's members; nil when this table has none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::interned(acc, t)` |  |  |  | interned: a BTF table with one more type, and the id in `:id`, for a chain of them. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::ctx_pointer(acc, kind)` |  |  |  | ctx_pointer: a BTF table holding a pointer to a program type's context; a type with no named context has a pointer to void, which only its entry can take. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::param_type(acc, param, kind)` |  |  |  | param_type: a BTF table holding the type of one parameter kind, a fault pointer being a pointer to a `long long`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::func_btf(acc, f, kind, is_entry)` |  |  |  | func_btf: a BTF table holding one function and its proto; the entry's context may be void, a subprogram's may not. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_uint(acc, value)` |  |  |  | map_uint: a BTF table holding the type a BTF-defined map says an integer with: a pointer to an array of that many. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_member(acc, name, value)` |  |  |  | map_member: a BTF table with one member added to the map struct being built, 64 bits on from the last. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::map_btf(acc, m)` |  |  |  | map_btf: a BTF table holding one map's definition as libbpf reads `.maps`, and its variable: `:var`, and for a map with a spin lock `:key` and `:value`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::locked_members(acc, m)` |  |  |  | locked_members: the key and value members of a map with a spin lock: the value is the kernel's `struct bpf_spin_lock`, padding, then one integer. `:key` and `:value` hold their ids, which the kernel's map takes. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::key_type_name(count)` |  |  |  | key_type_name: the C name of an unsigned integer of this many bytes, as clang writes it in BTF. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::sections` |  |  |  | sections: each program type with the ELF section name libbpf reads it from, the first row of a type being the name written for it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::section_name(kind)` |  |  |  | section_name: the ELF section libbpf reads a program type from; a type it has no name for here raises. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::program_btf(program)` |  |  |  | program_btf: a program's own BTF: {types:, names:, bytes:, ext:, func_info:, map_types:, map_vars:}. `bytes` is the blob for `.BTF` and BTF_LOAD; `ext` the `.BTF.ext`; `func_info` `{at:, type:}` by slot for BPF_PROG_LOAD; `map_types` each map's key and value ids where it has them; `map_vars` each map's variable. A program that came from an object keeps the BTF it came with. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::fresh_btf(program)` |  |  |  | fresh_btf: a compiled or assembled program's BTF, made from its functions and maps. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::source_text(program, line)` |  |  |  | source_text: one line of a program's source, empty when the program has none there. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::line_rows(program)` |  |  |  | line_rows: a program's source lines as the kernel holds them: one row a slot in rising order, the last mark at a slot winning, the first slot and each function's start always covered, by the function's first mark. Each row is {at:, line:, col:, file:, text:}. A program with no marks has none. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::line_strings(rows)` |  |  |  | line_strings: the strings BTF must hold for these rows. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::line_col_word(line, col)` |  |  |  | line_col_word: a position as `bpf_line_info` packs it, the line above ten bits of column. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::line_info_rows(rows, offsets)` |  |  |  | line_info_rows: rows as BPF_PROG_LOAD reads them: each `at` a slot, its strings by their offsets in the program's BTF. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::line_info_sections(rows, funcs, section)` |  |  |  | line_info_sections: `.BTF.ext`'s view of a program's source lines, the entry's in its own section and the rest in `.text`, each `at` in bytes from its section's start. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::source_at(program, slot)` |  |  |  | source_at: where a slot of a compiled program came from: {file:, line:, col:, text:} of the last row at or before it, nil for a program that knows no lines. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::maps_section(acc, maps)` |  |  |  | maps_section: a BTF table with the `.maps` data section over each map's variable, when there are maps. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::license_section(acc, license)` |  |  |  | license_section: a BTF table with the `license` data section over its one variable, as clang writes `_license`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::bytes_section(acc, section, name, count)` |  |  |  | bytes_section: a BTF table with a data section over one variable, `char name[count]`, as clang writes it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::data_map_place(m)` |  |  |  | data_map_place: where a map a program references by value lives in an object, as libbpf reads global data: {section:, name:, size:}. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::data_maps_section(acc, maps)` |  |  |  | data_maps_section: a BTF table with a data section over each map a program references by value. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::core_tree(acc, name, rows)` |  |  |  | core_tree: a BTF table with one local struct built from the field paths under it, as CO-RE reads them: `rows` are {path:, size:, at:}. `:id` is the struct, `:size` its bytes, and `:placed` each row with its member indexes and its offset. A path that is both a field and a struct is refused by name. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::core_types(acc, fields)` |  |  |  | core_types: a BTF table with a local struct for each kernel struct a program reads, `:relos` each read as a CO-RE byte-offset relocation by slot, and `:offsets` the local offset each slot holds in an object. libbpf moves each to the running kernel's offset when it loads. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::core_sections(relos, funcs, section)` |  |  |  | core_sections: `.BTF.ext`'s view of a program's CO-RE relocations, the entry's in its own section and the rest in `.text`, each `at` in bytes from its section's start. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::func_info_sections(rows, section)` |  |  |  | func_info_sections: `.BTF.ext`'s view of a program's functions, the first in its own section and the rest in `.text`, each `at` in bytes from its section's start. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::apply_shape(program, extra)` |  |  |  | apply_shape: an assembled program with what compiling it found. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::patched_at(insns, wanted, imm)` |  |  |  | patched_at: instructions with one slot's immediate replaced. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::bind_fields(program, resolve)` |  |  |  | bind_fields: a program with each `kernel_read` field's offset filled in, from `resolve`, a function of a struct's name and a path answering {offset:, size:}. A field whose size on this kernel is not the program's raises :kernel_mismatch, where a wrong read would have gone on silently. This is a CO-RE relocation done before load, so a signed program can carry it. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::source_path(path)` |  |  |  | source_path: where `compile` reads a file: under `GEN_ROOT` when a check holds it there, else as given. A file in neither place raises :bpf_compile and names the directory it looked from. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::source_path_in(root, path)` |  |  |  | source_path_in: `source_path` under a root, or nil for none. With no root the path is the one given, spelled as given, since it names the file in a program's BTF and bpftool prints it back as `[file:PATH`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::compile(path, opts)` |  |  | `B0083` | compile: a blue file's definition to a kernel program, through the resolved tree `blue ast --resolved --lines` prints, so what compiles has passed every rule the check stage holds, and each instruction knows its source line. {lines: false} compiles with no line marks. See `compile_forms`. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::compiled_source(text, kind?)` |  |  |  | compiled_source: a compiled program from resolved text, entry `e`, faulting to 9. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::answer(text, packet?)` |  |  |  | answer: what resolved text answers on the machine. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::refusal_of(text)` |  |  |  | refusal_of: the compile error resolved text gets, nil when it compiles. |
| `kaaneru::bpf::global_program(text, names)` |  |  |  | global_program: a compiled program from resolved text, entry `e`, faulting to 9, with these definitions global. |
| `kaaneru::btf::kinds` |  |  |  | kinds: BTF's kinds in the order of their numbers, from `include/uapi/linux/btf.h`. |
| `kaaneru::btf::word_at(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | word_at: the little-endian 32-bit word at a byte position. |
| `kaaneru::btf::header_of(bytes)` |  |  |  | header_of: a blob's section offsets, each from the blob's start. |
| `kaaneru::btf::words_from(bytes, at, count)` |  |  |  | words_from: whole words from a byte position. |
| `kaaneru::btf::trailing_words(kind, vlen)` |  |  |  | trailing_words: the words a type of this kind number and member count takes past its three. |
| `kaaneru::btf::index_step(acc, words)` |  |  |  | index_step: one type added to the index, and where the next starts. |
| `kaaneru::btf::type_index(words)` |  |  |  | type_index: where each type starts and each name's ids, from the type words. |
| `kaaneru::btf::nul` |  |  |  | nul: the one-character string a string section separates names with. |
| `kaaneru::btf::strings_step(acc, name)` |  |  |  | strings_step: one name added to the string tables, at its byte offset. |
| `kaaneru::btf::string_tables(text)` |  |  |  | string_tables: the string section's names by offset and offsets by name. |
| `kaaneru::btf::table_of(words, strings)` |  |  |  | table_of: the type table from type words and string bytes, refusing strings that are not UTF-8. |
| `kaaneru::btf::read(bytes)` |  |  |  | read: a BTF blob's bytes as a table of its types. Bytes that are no BTF raise :decode_error. |
| `kaaneru::btf::open(path)` |  |  |  | open: the BTF blob a file holds, reading its sections as words and text rather than byte by byte. |
| `kaaneru::btf::vmlinux_path` |  |  |  | vmlinux_path: where a running kernel publishes its own types; `open` reads it. |
| `kaaneru::btf::name_of(b, offset)` |  |  |  | name_of: the name at a string offset, empty when there is none. |
| `kaaneru::btf::member_of(b, at)` |  |  |  | member_of: the struct member whose three words start here. |
| `kaaneru::btf::type_at(b, id)` |  |  |  | type_at: the type with this id as a record: {id:, kind:, name:, size:, type:}, and `members` for a struct or union. Id 0 is void; an id past the table is nil. |
| `kaaneru::btf::find(b, name, kind)` |  |  |  | find: the id of the type of this name and kind, nil when this table has none. |
| `kaaneru::btf::need(b, name, kind)` |  |  |  | need: the id of the type of this name and kind, and raises :not_found naming it when this table has none. |
| `kaaneru::btf::resolved(b, id)` |  |  |  | resolved: the type behind typedefs and qualifiers. |
| `kaaneru::btf::size_of(b, id)` |  |  |  | size_of: a type's size in bytes, through typedefs, qualifiers and arrays. A pointer is 8. |
| `kaaneru::btf::bit_offset(m, struct_kind_flag)` |  |  |  | bit_offset: a member's bit offset, a bitfield's size taken off when its struct says. |
| `kaaneru::btf::kind_flag_of(b, id)` |  |  |  | kind_flag_of: whether a struct's members carry bitfield sizes. |
| `kaaneru::btf::member_search(b, t, name)` |  |  |  | member_search: a member by name, searching anonymous unions and adding their offsets. |
| `kaaneru::btf::walk(b, at_type, bits, path)` |  |  |  | walk: a type and bit offset at the end of a path of member names. |
| `kaaneru::btf::field(b, struct_name, path)` |  |  |  | field: a named struct's field by its path of member names, through anonymous unions: {offset:, size:, type:}, offset and size in bytes. A bitfield, or a field this kernel's struct does not have, raises by name. |
| `kaaneru::btf::function_id(b, name)` |  |  |  | function_id: the id of a kernel function, what a tracing or LSM program names as its target. An LSM hook's function is `bpf_lsm_` and the hook's name. |
| `kaaneru::btf::member_index(b, struct_name, name)` |  |  |  | member_index: a member's place among its struct's own members, what a :struct_ops program names at load. A struct with no such member raises. |
| `kaaneru::btf::smallest` |  |  |  | smallest: the smallest BTF blob a kernel takes: one `int`. A :struct_ops map must name a BTF object of its own, whatever its value's type. |
| `kaaneru::btf::word_bytes(n)` |  |  |  | word_bytes: a word's four little-endian bytes. |
| `kaaneru::btf::sample` |  |  |  | sample: a small BTF blob by hand, for tests and examples: 1 `int` of 4 bytes; 2 struct `task` {state int at 0; pid at 32 bits; an anonymous union holding inner int at 64 bits}; 3 typedef `pid_t` of `int`; 4 that anonymous union; 5 func `run`; 6 a pointer to `int`. |
| `kaaneru::btf::int_type(name, count, is_signed?)` |  |  |  | int_type: an integer type of this many bytes, signed unless the last argument says not. |
| `kaaneru::btf::pointer_to(id)` |  |  |  | pointer_to: a pointer to the type with this id; 0 is void. |
| `kaaneru::btf::array_of(element, index, count)` |  |  |  | array_of: an array of `count` elements of a type, its index of a type. |
| `kaaneru::btf::proto_type(returns, params)` |  |  |  | proto_type: a function's type: what it returns and its parameters, each {name:, type:}. |
| `kaaneru::btf::sized_type(kind, name, count, rows)` |  |  |  | sized_type: a type of a size holding rows: :struct, each row {name:, type:, bits:} and an empty name making it anonymous, or :datasec, each row {type:, offset:, size:}. |
| `kaaneru::btf::linked_type(kind, name, type_id, linkage)` |  |  |  | linked_type: a :func or :var of a name, a type and a linkage: :static, :global or, for a function, :extern. |
| `kaaneru::btf::linkage_number(linkage)` |  |  |  | linkage_number: a linkage's number in BTF; a name that is none raises. |
| `kaaneru::btf::intern(types, t)` |  |  |  | intern: types with one more, or the same types when it is already there: {types:, id:}. |
| `kaaneru::btf::names_in(t)` |  |  |  | names_in: every name a type holds, in the order the string section lists them. |
| `kaaneru::btf::strings_of(types, names)` |  |  |  | strings_of: a string section and each name's offset, the empty name first: {bytes:, offsets:}. |
| `kaaneru::btf::info_word(kind, vlen)` |  |  |  | info_word: a type's second word: its kind's number, its count and its flag. |
| `kaaneru::btf::type_words(t, offsets)` |  |  |  | type_words: one type as its words, each name by its offset in `offsets`. A type of a kind this writer has no form for raises. |
| `kaaneru::btf::header_layout` |  |  |  | header_layout: `struct btf_header`. |
| `kaaneru::btf::encode(types, names?)` |  |  |  | encode: types, each made by the words above and its id its place from 1, as a BTF blob. `names` adds strings no type holds, such as the section names `.BTF.ext` names. |
| `kaaneru::btf::ext_header_layout` |  |  |  | ext_header_layout: `struct btf_ext_header`, through its CO-RE fields. |
| `kaaneru::btf::ext_section_words(s, offsets)` |  |  |  | ext_section_words: one ELF section's func_info as words: its name's offset, its count, then `[byte offset, type id]`. |
| `kaaneru::btf::ext_line_words(s, offsets)` |  |  |  | ext_line_words: one ELF section's line info as words: its name's offset, its count, then `[byte offset, file, text, position]`. |
| `kaaneru::btf::ext_core_words(s, offsets)` |  |  |  | ext_core_words: one ELF section's CO-RE relocations as words: its name's offset, its count, then `[byte offset, type, access text, kind]`. |
| `kaaneru::btf::encode_ext(types, names, sections, lines?, core?)` |  |  |  | encode_ext: the `.BTF.ext` for these types and names: each of `sections` {section:, funcs: [{at:, type:}]}, and each of `lines` {section:, lines: [{at:, file:, text:, pos:}]}, and each of `core` {section:, relos: [{at:, type:, access:, kind:}]}, `at` in bytes from the section's start, `pos` the line above ten bits of column. |
| `kaaneru::btf::ext_step(acc, bytes, b, record)` |  |  |  | ext_step: one section of a func_info read, and where the next starts. |
| `kaaneru::btf::read_ext(bytes, blob)` |  |  |  | read_ext: a `.BTF.ext`'s func_info by section, names read from the BTF blob beside it. Bytes that are no `.BTF.ext` raise :decode_error. |
| `kaaneru::btf::read_lines(bytes, blob)` |  |  |  | read_lines: a `.BTF.ext`'s line info by section, strings read from the BTF blob beside it, each row {at:, file:, text:, line:, col:}. A record longer than the 16 bytes the kernel reads keeps its first 16. |
| `kaaneru::btf::line_step(acc, bytes, b, record)` |  |  |  | line_step: one section of a line info read, and where the next starts. |
| `kaaneru::btf::read_core(bytes, blob)` |  |  |  | read_core: a `.BTF.ext`'s CO-RE relocations by section, each {at:, type:, access:, kind:}. An ext that holds none, or a header too short to, has none. |
| `kaaneru::btf::core_step(acc, bytes, b, record)` |  |  |  | core_step: one section of a CO-RE read, and where the next starts. |
| `kaaneru::btf::slice_after_header(bytes)` |  |  |  | slice_after_header: the first type's sixteen bytes of a blob. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::header_layout` |  |  |  | header_layout: `Elf64_Ehdr`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::section_layout` |  |  |  | section_layout: `Elf64_Shdr`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::symbol_layout` |  |  |  | symbol_layout: `Elf64_Sym`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::rel_layout` |  |  |  | rel_layout: `Elf64_Rel`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::ident_bytes` |  |  |  | ident_bytes: an ELF's first 16 bytes: its magic, 64-bit, little-endian, version 1. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::kind_number(table, name)` |  |  |  | kind_number: the number of a section's type, a symbol's type or binding, or a relocation, from each table in `elf.h` and `llvm/BinaryFormat/ELFRelocs/BPF.def`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::section_flags(names)` |  |  |  | section_flags: a section's flags from the names of its bits. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::symbols_ordered(symbols)` |  |  |  | symbols_ordered: a symbol table's order: every local before every global, as ELF requires. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::section_named(sections, name)` |  |  |  | section_named: the index of a section by name among those given, the null section being 0; a name that is none raises. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::symbol_bytes(s, strings, sections)` |  |  |  | symbol_bytes: one symbol's record, its name from the string table and its section by index. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::rel_bytes(r, ordered)` |  |  |  | rel_bytes: one relocation's record: {at:, symbol:, type:}, the symbol by name among those placed. A symbol that is none raises. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::placed_section(acc, s)` |  |  |  | placed_section: one section with its offset, the bytes before it padded to its alignment. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::section_record(s, strings, sections)` |  |  |  | section_record: one section's header. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::encode(sections, symbols)` |  |  |  | encode: sections and symbols as an ELF object's bytes, with its `.strtab` first and its `.symtab` last. A section is {name:, type:, flags:, align:, bytes:}, or a relocation section {name:, target:, rels: [{at:, symbol:, type:}]}; a symbol is {name:, bind:, type:, section:, value:, size:}. A name a relocation or a symbol reaches that is not there raises. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::text_at(strings, at)` |  |  |  | text_at: the text at an offset in a string section; an offset past it is empty. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::decode(bytes)` |  |  |  | decode: an ELF64 little-endian object's bytes as {header:, sections:, symbols:, rels:}. `rels` has each section's relocations by its name, each {at:, symbol:, type:, value:}. Bytes that are no such object, or that run short, raise :decode_error. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::symbols_of(sections)` |  |  |  | symbols_of: a decoded object's symbols, the null symbol kept so that indexes hold. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::symbol_read(bytes, i, names, sections)` |  |  |  | symbol_read: one symbol of a table: its section by name, a section symbol named for its section. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::rels_of(sections, symbols)` |  |  |  | rels_of: each relocation section's records, by the name of the section they apply to. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::section_of(o, name)` |  |  |  | section_of: a decoded object's section by name, nil when it has none. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::functions_of(p)` |  |  |  | functions_of: a program's functions, or for a bare assembled one the one function it is. A program with no name is `blue`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::split_at(p, funcs)` |  |  |  | split_at: the slots each function of a program starts and ends at: the entry's section, then `.text`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::function_at(funcs, slot)` |  |  |  | function_at: the function that starts at a slot, nil when none does. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::relocated(i, slot, world)` |  |  |  | relocated: one instruction as the object holds it, and its relocation: a map reference holds nothing until libbpf gives it a descriptor, a reference into a map's value holds its offset, a global call holds -1 and names its target, a reference to a static function names `.text` and holds the place in it, and a kernel field's instruction holds its local offset, which libbpf moves. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::relocated_call(i, slot, world)` |  |  |  | relocated_call: a call as the object holds it. A global function is named; a static one in the same section stays relative. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::coded(insns, from, world)` |  |  |  | coded: a run of instructions as the bytes a section holds and its relocations, each `at` in bytes from the section's start. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::slot_split(insns, at)` |  |  |  | slot_split: instructions before a slot and from it. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::function_symbols(funcs, slots, section)` |  |  |  | function_symbols: each function's symbol: the entry's in its section, the rest in `.text`, each sized to the next. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::map_symbols(maps, vars)` |  |  |  | map_symbols: each map's symbol, at its place in `.maps`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::object(p, opts?)` |  |  |  | object: a compiled or assembled program as an ELF object's bytes, in the shape clang gives the same program: the entry in the section libbpf names its type by, subprograms in `.text`, maps in `.maps`, `license`, `.BTF` and `.BTF.ext`. `opts` takes {section:} for a program whose section names a target, such as "fentry/do_sys_openat2", {license:}, and {line_info: false} to leave out the source lines `.BTF.ext` holds by default. {core: false} refuses a program whose kernel fields are unbound, where by default each is a CO-RE relocation libbpf moves to the running kernel's offset. A map a program references by value becomes its own `.data.NAME` section, as libbpf reads global data. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::value_maps(p)` |  |  |  | value_maps: the maps a program holds a reference into, each a one-entry array, which is what libbpf can make from a section. One also named as a map, or of another shape, is refused by name. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::data_map_part(m)` |  |  |  | data_map_part: the section a map referenced by value lives in, zero-filled. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::data_map_symbol(m)` |  |  |  | data_map_symbol: the symbol at the start of a map referenced by value. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::types_with_section(types, section)` |  |  |  | types_with_section: the `.BTF` bytes, the entry's section among its strings. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::ext_with_section(types, section, funcs)` |  |  |  | ext_with_section: the `.BTF.ext` bytes for an object whose entry sits in this section. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::section_kind(name)` |  |  |  | section_kind: the program type a section's name says, as libbpf reads it: the row of `kaaneru::bpf::sections` the name is, or begins with and a `/`; nil for one the table has no row for. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::map_rows(table, shape)` |  |  |  | map_rows: a BTF-defined map's members by name: an integer is a pointer to an array of that many, a type a pointer to it. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::has_lock?(table, id)` |  |  |  | has_lock?: whether a value type holds a `struct bpf_spin_lock`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::map_of_var(table, var_id)` |  |  |  | map_of_var: the map shape a `.maps` variable declares. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::maps_of(o)` |  |  |  | maps_of: the maps a decoded object's `.BTF` declares in `.maps`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::fixed_blob(o, blob)` |  |  |  | fixed_blob: the `.BTF` as the kernel takes it: a data section's size and each variable's place come from the object's symbols, as clang leaves them for libbpf to fill. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::fixed_datasec(words, o, table, t)` |  |  |  | fixed_datasec: type words with one data section's size and its variables' places filled in from symbols. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::replaced(xs, at, value)` |  |  |  | replaced: a list with the item at one place changed. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::linked_rel(i, rel, slot, _base, text_base)` |  |  |  | linked_rel: one instruction of a section given what its relocation names: a map named, a call or function reference made relative. `base` is where this section lands in the linked program, and `text_base` where `.text` does. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::refused_unlinked(insns)` |  |  |  | refused_unlinked: nil, or the refusal for an instruction still holding a relocation this reader could not link. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::after_data(section)` |  |  |  | after_data: the map a `.data.NAME` section is. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::data_maps_of(o)` |  |  |  | data_maps_of: the one-entry array each zero-filled `.data.NAME` section of a decoded object is. A section holding other bytes needs its contents written at load, which this reader has no form for, so it is left out and a reference to it is refused when the array is not found. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::fields_of(o, section, main_slots)` |  |  |  | fields_of: a decoded object's CO-RE relocations as the fields `kaaneru::bpf::bind_fields` fills: each by its slot in a linked program, its struct and its member names from the object's own BTF. An instruction this linker cannot patch, or a kind other than a byte offset, is refused by name: reading on with the local offset would read the wrong field. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::field_of_core(table, r, slot)` |  |  |  | field_of_core: one CO-RE relocation as a field. An anonymous member adds nothing to the path, as `kaaneru::btf::field` walks through it. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::fields_patchable(fields, insns)` |  |  |  | fields_patchable: the fields of a linked program, refusing one whose instruction holds its offset anywhere but an immediate. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::linked_section(s, rels, base, text_base)` |  |  |  | linked_section: a section's instructions placed from a slot, each relocation applied: {insns:, relocs:}. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::entry_section(o, name)` |  |  |  | entry_section: the program section of a decoded object: the named one, or else the first that holds code and is not `.text`. None raises by name. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::info_of(o, section, main_slots)` |  |  |  | info_of: an object's func_info as the linked program's rows, each `at` by slot, and each row's function: {funcs:, func_info:}. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::function_starts(o, info, main_slots)` |  |  |  | function_starts: the slots a linked program's functions start at, read from its func_info and its function symbols: the first slot, then each in `.text`, in order. An object that says nothing of `.text` has one function there. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::placed_rows(insns)` |  |  |  | placed_rows: a program's instructions, each `[slot, instruction]`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::reference_target(row)` |  |  |  | reference_target: the slot an instruction calls or takes the address of in this program, nil for any other instruction. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::start_holding(starts, slot)` |  |  |  | start_holding: the function start a slot falls in. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::reached_starts(starts, rows)` |  |  |  | reached_starts: the functions a program reaches from its first, by call or by reference. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::slot_after_drop(kept, slot)` |  |  |  | slot_after_drop: where a slot lands once only these functions stay: `kept` is each kept function's `[start, end]`. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::linked_only(program, starts)` |  |  |  | linked_only: a linked program with the functions nothing reaches removed, as libbpf links one: each call, reference, relocation and function row moved to its new slot. A program that reaches them all comes back as it was. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::program_of(o, name?, opts?)` |  |  |  | program_of: a decoded object linked into a program, as libbpf links one: the section named, or the first program section, then the functions of `.text` it reaches; a function nothing reaches is dropped, where the verifier would refuse it. `opts` {keep_unreached: true} links the whole of `.text` when anything reaches it. Its maps, functions and BTF come from the object, and a program type its section does not name is nil. An object for another machine raises. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::assoc_machine(o)` |  |  |  | assoc_machine: a decoded object said to be for another machine, to show `program_of` refuses it. |
| `kaaneru::erufu::sample_program` |  |  |  | sample_program: a compiled program with a map and a global subprogram, for tests and examples. |

## kakehashi

`kakehashi` 0.1.2 · needs: deeta, hadome, hikisuu, jisho, junjo, kansuu, kazu, kyohi, moji, retsu, settei, shisutemu, shuugou, tesuto

kakehashi (架け橋, a bridge) — blue speaks Nix, Nix runs blue, and a rebuild does only the work that changed.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kakehashi::hyouka::nix_bin` |  |  |  | nix_bin: the nix hyouka runs, HYOUKA_NIX when set, so a test or a pinned nix can stand in for the one on PATH. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::installable(flake, attr)` |  |  |  | installable: the flake reference nix builds or evaluates for one attribute of a flake. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::store_dir` |  |  |  | store_dir: the prefix every Nix store path starts with. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::store_path?(p)` |  |  |  | store_path?: the text is a Nix store path by its fixed layout: the store prefix, a 32-character hash, a dash and a name. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::store_hash(p)` |  |  |  | store_hash: the hash part of a store path, nil for text that is not one. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::store_name(p)` |  |  |  | store_name: the name part of a store path, without the hash or any path inside it; nil for text that is not one. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::failure_class(text)` |  |  |  | failure_class: the kind of failure nix's message names (credential, fetch, builder, timeout, memory, assertion, eval, disk), else unknown. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::failure_of(text)` |  |  |  | failure_of: the refusal for a failed nix run: its class, with the last line nix printed as the reason. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::capture(argv)` |  |  | `B0083` | capture: run a command and keep what it printed and its status, nil when it cannot start, so a missing tool is an answer and not a crash. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::run_nix(args)` |  |  |  | run_nix: run nix with the arguments and answer its status, stdout and stderr; status -1 when nix cannot start. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::answer(out, read)` |  |  |  | answer: the kyohi result of one nix run: what `read` makes of stdout on success, else a refusal classified from what nix said. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::eval_json(inst, extra)` |  |  |  | eval_json: evaluate an installable with `nix eval --json` and answer the parsed value, or the refusal. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::eval_raw(inst, extra)` |  |  |  | eval_raw: evaluate an installable with `nix eval --raw` and answer its text, or the refusal. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::eval_args(flag, inst, extra)` |  |  |  | eval_args: the arguments of `nix eval` printing `inst` in the format `flag`, then `extra`. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::eval_with(flag, inst, extra, read)` |  |  |  | eval_with: run `nix eval` with `eval_args` and answer what `read` makes of its output. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::target_of(o)` |  |  |  | target_of: one target of a dry build's JSON: its drv path and its out path. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::targets(text)` |  |  |  | targets: every target a dry build's JSON names; none when the text is not a list. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::section_of(line)` |  |  |  | section_of: which part of a dry run's report a line opens or belongs to: the build list, the fetch list, a store path inside one, or other. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::work_step(acc, line)` |  |  |  | work_step: read one line of a dry run's report into the build and fetch lists gathered so far. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::work(text)` |  |  |  | work: what a dry run will build and fetch, read from nix's report. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::plan_build(inst, extra)` |  |  |  | plan_build: dry-run a build of an installable and answer its targets and what nix will build and fetch, or the refusal. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::first_out(plan)` |  |  |  | first_out: the out path of a plan's first target, nil when the plan has none. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::info_pairs(parsed)` |  |  |  | info_pairs: the [path, info] pairs of `nix path-info --json` whose path the store holds. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::path_info(paths, store)` |  |  |  | path_info: which of the paths a store holds and which it lacks; the local store when `store` is nil. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::resolve(link)` |  |  |  | resolve: the store path a link such as /run/current-system points at, nil when it cannot be read. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::sample_dry_run` |  |  |  | sample_dry_run: a real dry run's report, one drv to build and two paths to fetch, for tests and examples. |
| `kakehashi::hyouka::sample_targets` |  |  |  | sample_targets: a real dry build's JSON with one target, for tests and examples. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::fleet_repo(version)` |  |  |  | fleet_repo: the fleet-repo standard at `version`, as the config an instance's kijun.b starts from: the top-level allowlist, the node registry, import routes, declarers and the trio factory, with no waivers. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::allow_top(cfg, entries)` |  |  |  | allow_top: an instance names extra top-level entries the standard does not. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::configurations(cfg, names)` |  |  |  | configurations: an instance names the configurations the tree plane evaluates. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::append_field(cfg, key, xs)` |  |  |  | append_field: `cfg` with `xs` appended to the list at `key`. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::waive(cfg, rule, glob, reason)` |  |  |  | waive: exempt one path from one rule with a reason. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::disable(cfg, rule, reason)` |  |  |  | disable: switch one rule off for an instance, with the reason it is off; the rule then runs nowhere. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::finding(rule, file, line, subject, message)` |  |  |  | finding: one violation of a rule: where it is, what it is about, and why it fails. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::at(f, doc, rule, start, subject, message)` |  |  |  | at: a finding inside a parsed file, located at the line that holds the offset `start`. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::segments(path)` |  |  |  | segments: the parts of a repository path, the unit every layout rule reads. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::top_entries(files)` |  |  |  | top_entries: the repository's top-level entries, a directory marked with a trailing slash. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::allowed_top?(cfg, entry)` |  |  |  | allowed_top?: a top-level entry is one the standard's allowlist, or the instance's, matches. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_top(repo)` |  |  |  | check_top: the layout/top-level rule: one finding per top-level entry the standard does not allow. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::node_dirs(files)` |  |  |  | node_dirs: the directory names under nodes/ that hold at least one file. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::node_dir_of(text)` |  |  |  | node_dir_of: the node directory a registry's nodeDir path names, or nil when it names none. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::declared_nodes(repo)` |  |  |  | declared_nodes: the node directories the registry declares through the node constructor; none when there is no registry. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_nodes(repo)` |  |  |  | check_nodes: the layout/node-shape rule: a node directory with no default.nix, and a registry-declared node with no layers.nix. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::shared_modules(files)` |  |  |  | shared_modules: the names of the shared options modules, one file each under modules/pleme/shared, its default.nix aside. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::trio_calls(repo, path)` |  |  |  | trio_calls: the calls of the trio factory in one file of the repository. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::spec_kind(c)` |  |  |  | spec_kind: the kind a trio call declares: its literal kind, "binary" when none is set, "computed" when it is not a literal. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::self_rendering?(repo, m)` |  |  |  | self_rendering?: a shared module that calls the trio factory renders itself, since its module arm picks the platform. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::factory_proven?(repo, m)` |  |  |  | factory_proven?: a shared module whose every trio call is a binary, package or service kind is proven by substrate's matrix, so it needs no harness of its own. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_proof(repo)` |  |  |  | check_proof: the layout/shared-has-proof rule: a shared module with no parts/ harness that the factory does not prove. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::route_files(repo)` |  |  |  | route_files: the repository's files that the config names as import routes. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_reachable(repo)` |  |  |  | check_reachable: the layout/shared-reachable rule: a shared module no renderer, import route or trio call reaches. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::binding_finding(rule, f, b, message)` |  |  |  | binding_finding: a finding about one attribute binding, located at the binding and named by its dotted path. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::module_bindings(f)` |  |  |  | module_bindings: the attribute-set bindings of a parsed file, the ones a module sets. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::starts?(path, prefix)` |  |  |  | starts?: an option path begins with the given prefix of segments. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_system_calls(f, repo)` |  |  |  | check_system_calls: the expr/node-system-only-via-mkNode rule: a nixosSystem or darwinSystem call outside the node engine. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::identity?(b)` |  |  |  | identity?: a binding sets a node's identity: its hostname or hostId, a disk by UUID, or a secret from a node's directory. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_profile_identity(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_profile_identity: the expr/profile-no-identity rule: a profile that sets a node's identity. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::declaration_findings(rule, f, where)` |  |  |  | declaration_findings: one finding of `rule` per option a file declares, saying where the declaration sits. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_lib_data(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_lib_data: the expr/lib-is-data rule: an option declaration or a module function in lib/. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::platform_prefixes` |  |  |  | platform_prefixes: the config paths only a platform renderer may emit, since each exists on one platform only. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::platform_path?(path)` |  |  |  | platform_path?: a binding path lands under a platform prefix; an option declaration never does. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::emits_platform?(b)` |  |  |  | emits_platform?: a binding is the outermost one that emits platform config, so a nested emission is named once. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_shared_emission(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_shared_emission: the expr/shared-declares-only rule: a shared module that emits platform config. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_shared_platform_question(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_shared_platform_question: the expr/shared-asks-no-platform rule: a shared module that asks isDarwin or isLinux. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_node_options(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_node_options: the expr/nodes-declare-no-options rule: an option declared in a node file. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_angle(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_angle: the expr/no-angle-paths rule: an angle path, which NIX_PATH resolves and the flake lock does not pin. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::fetchers` |  |  |  | fetchers: the fetch functions the unpinned-fetch rule reads. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::pin_attrs` |  |  |  | pin_attrs: the attributes any one of which pins a fetch to its content. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::unpinned?(c)` |  |  |  | unpinned?: a fetch call carries no pin: a bare URL, or an attribute set with no hash or rev. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_fetch(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_fetch: the expr/no-unpinned-fetch rule: a fetch that takes whatever the server serves today. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::script_body(c)` |  |  |  | script_body: the argument of a script-writing call that holds its shell text, which sits at a different place for each writer. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::script_lines(body)` |  |  |  | script_lines: the non-blank lines of a literal script body; 0 when the body is not a literal string. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::scripts` |  |  |  | scripts: the nixpkgs writers that carry shell text, the calls the no-shell rule reads. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_shell(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_shell: the expr/no-shell rule: a script writer carrying more than three lines of shell. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_parses(f, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_parses: the expr/parses rule: the first parse error of a file, since a file the parser refuses is checked by no rule. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::own_file(tree, file)` |  |  |  | own_file: a store path from an evaluated tree, as a path in this repository; nil when it lies outside the repository's own source. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_pleme_declarations(tree, repo)` |  |  |  | check_pleme_declarations: the tree/pleme-options-declared-in-shared rule: a pleme.* option declared outside the declarer globs. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_hostname(tree, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_hostname: the tree/hostname-from-node rule: networking.hostName defined anywhere but under nodes/. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::declared_at(tree, option)` |  |  |  | declared_at: the repository file that declares an option in an evaluated tree, or "(external)" when no own file does. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_option_evaluates(tree, _repo)` |  |  |  | check_option_evaluates: the tree/option-evaluates rule: one finding per option the tree probe could not evaluate, at its declaration. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::unit_kinds` |  |  |  | unit_kinds: the kinds of concept the trio rule counts. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::unit(kind_of, name, file, files)` |  |  |  | unit: one concept the fleet manages: its kind, its name, the file or directory that holds it, and its nix files. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::nix_under(files, prefix)` |  |  |  | nix_under: the nix files under a path prefix. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::dir_units(files, root, kind_of, skip, pool)` |  |  |  | dir_units: the concepts directly under `root`: each nix file one unit, each subdirectory one unit, minus the files in `skip`. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::returned_set(n)` |  |  |  | returned_set: the attribute set an expression evaluates to, looking through lambdas, let and parentheses; nil when it is not one. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::overlay_units(repo)` |  |  |  | overlay_units: one concept per package an overlay file adds, named by its top-level attribute. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::resolve(segs, acc)` |  |  |  | resolve: path segments applied to a directory, "." and ".." resolved. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::relative_to(file, rel)` |  |  |  | relative_to: the repository path a relative import in `file` names. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::imported_files(f)` |  |  |  | imported_files: the repository files a parsed file imports by relative path. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::spec_names(c)` |  |  |  | spec_names: the concept names a trio call covers: its literal name and packageAttr. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::concept_facts(repo)` |  |  |  | concept_facts: what the trio rule reads of the repository: the files that call the factory, the names their specs cover, what they import, and what the configuration layers import. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::registry_units(files, facts)` |  |  |  | registry_units: one concept per lib/ file a configuration layer imports; a registry nobody reads as configuration is not one. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::concept_units(repo, facts)` |  |  |  | concept_units: every concept the fleet manages: shared modules, packages, overlay-added packages and consumed registries. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::covered?(u, facts)` |  |  |  | covered?: a concept is declared through the trio factory: one of its files calls it, a spec names it, or a trio file imports the registry. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::check_concepts(repo)` |  |  |  | check_concepts: the trio/every-concept rule: a concept not declared through the module-trio factory. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::rule(id, plane, tier, scope, why, fix, check)` |  |  |  | rule: one rule of the standard: its id, the plane it reads, whether it gates or advises, the paths it covers, why it holds, how to fix a finding, and the check that finds them. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::rules` |  |  |  | rules: the standard itself: every rule, in the order `kijun rules` lists them. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::disabled_ids(cfg)` |  |  |  | disabled_ids: the ids of the rules an instance switched off. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::active(cfg, plane)` |  |  |  | active: the rules of one plane that run for an instance, its disabled ones left out. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::in_scope?(r, path)` |  |  |  | in_scope?: a rule reads a path when one of its scope globs matches it. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::file_facts(path, source)` |  |  |  | file_facts: one nix file parsed once for every expression rule: its path, syntax tree, bindings and calls. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::expression_findings(repo)` |  |  |  | expression_findings: every active expression rule run over each nix file in its scope; a file no rule covers is not parsed. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::layout_findings(repo)` |  |  |  | layout_findings: every active layout rule run over the repository's file list. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::tree_findings(repo, trees)` |  |  |  | tree_findings: every active tree rule run over each evaluated configuration tree. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::waiver_refusal(w)` |  |  |  | waiver_refusal: why a waiver is refused: its reason is not a typed `skip-<rule>:` line, or it names no rule of the standard; nil when it is sound. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::valid_waivers(cfg)` |  |  |  | valid_waivers: the waivers that hold; a refused one covers nothing. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::config_refusals(cfg)` |  |  |  | config_refusals: every refused waiver and every disable naming no rule, each refused alone. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::waived?(ws, x)` |  |  |  | waived?: a finding is covered by a waiver of its rule whose glob matches its file. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::tier_of(id)` |  |  |  | tier_of: whether a rule gates or only advises. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::rule_field(id, key)` |  |  |  | rule_field: the value at `key` of the rule whose id is `id`; an unknown id raises. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::key_of(x)` |  |  |  | key_of: the baseline key of a finding, its file and subject, so a moved line keeps its row. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::measurements(xs)` |  |  |  | measurements: findings counted per key, as the ratchet's measurements. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::rule_ids(xs)` |  |  |  | rule_ids: the rules that have findings, each once, in first-seen order. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::baseline_text(gates)` |  |  |  | baseline_text: the kijun.baseline a set of gate findings records. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::baseline_with(gates, kept)` |  |  |  | baseline_with: the baseline for this run's gate findings, keeping the rows of planes that did not run. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::ran_ids(cfg, tree)` |  |  |  | ran_ids: the ids of the rules this run executes; the tree plane runs only with --tree. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::split_rows(rows, ids)` |  |  |  | split_rows: baseline rows split into those of rules that ran, which are judged, and the rest, which are kept as they are. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::judge(gates, rows)` |  |  |  | judge: the ratchet's verdict: gate findings outside the baseline are new, baseline rows no finding holds are orphans, and rows whose count fell can shrink. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::nix_string(s)` |  |  |  | nix_string: a text as a nix string literal, quotes, backslashes and interpolation escaped. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::tree_expr(excluded)` |  |  |  | tree_expr: the nix function the tree probe applies to a configuration: the declarations and definitions of every pleme.* option and of networking.hostName, skipping the options already known not to evaluate. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::failing_option(stderr)` |  |  |  | failing_option: the option whose evaluation failed, read from the last kijun-option tag in nix's trace; nil when no tag is there. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::last_error(stderr)` |  |  |  | last_error: the innermost error message in nix's stderr, the one that names the cause. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::own_source(root)` |  |  | `B0083` | own_source: the store path of the flake's own source, which tells this repository's files apart from its inputs' in an evaluated tree; "" when nix cannot say. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::entries(xs)` |  |  |  | entries: the option entries of the tree probe's JSON, each with its path, declarations, definitions and whether it threw. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::or_list(xs)` |  |  |  | or_list: a value read from JSON as a list, or [] when it is absent or not a list. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::tree_of(root, own, configuration)` |  |  |  | tree_of: the evaluated option tree of one configuration, from a probe that starts with no option known to fail. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::probe(root, own, configuration, blind)` |  |  | `B0083` | probe: evaluate one configuration's option tree with nix; an option whose evaluation fails is set aside as blind and the probe runs again, so one bad option never hides the rest. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::tracked(root)` |  |  | `B0083` | tracked: the files git tracks under root, the set a flake sees; nil when root is not a work tree. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::source_of(repo, path)` |  |  |  | source_of: the text of one repository file, through the repository's own reader. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::on_disk(root, files)` |  |  |  | on_disk: a repository read from a checkout: its tracked files, each read from disk when a rule asks. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::in_memory(sources)` |  |  |  | in_memory: a repository held as [path, text] pairs, so every rule runs without a checkout. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::evaluate(cfg, source, trees)` |  |  |  | evaluate: the standard applied to a repository and its evaluated trees: findings split into gates, advice and waived, with the config's refusals. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::evaluate_timed(cfg, source, trees, clock)` |  |  |  | evaluate_timed: evaluate, with each plane's time read from `clock`. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::parse_args(args)` |  |  |  | parse_args: kijun's command line: check, baseline or rules, then its flags; a bad line is an error field, never a raise. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::flags(args, acc)` |  |  |  | flags: the flags after the command, read into `acc` until the first error. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::rule_line(r)` |  |  |  | rule_line: one rule as `kijun rules` lists it: id, plane, tier and scope, then why and fix. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::rules_text(cfg)` |  |  |  | rules_text: the whole standard as `kijun rules` prints it, headed by its name, version and rule count. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::finding_line(x)` |  |  |  | finding_line: one finding as a report line, file:line first so an editor can jump to it. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::counts(xs)` |  |  |  | counts: the number of findings per rule, ordered by rule id. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::plain(x)` |  |  |  | plain: a finding as the JSON report carries it: only its rule, file, line, subject and message. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::plane_of(id)` |  |  |  | plane_of: which plane a rule reads: layout, expression or tree. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::plane_counts(result, timing)` |  |  |  | plane_counts: per plane, how many findings, gates, advice and waivers it produced and how long it took. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::report_json(cfg, result, verdict, timing)` |  |  |  | report_json: the `kijun check --json` report: the verdict, every finding, the counts and the timing. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::timing_line(result, timing)` |  |  |  | timing_line: the report's last line: wall time, then each plane's findings and time. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::report_text(cfg, result, verdict, timing)` |  |  |  | report_text: the `kijun check` report for people: totals, counts per rule, refusals, disabled rules, blind trees, what the baseline can shed, and the gate findings outside it. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::run(cfg, args)` |  |  |  | run: one kijun command against a checkout with a given standard: list the rules, check against the baseline, or rewrite the baseline. A bad line or a gate finding outside the baseline raises. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::main` |  |  | `B0083` | main: the kijun command's entry point: a repository's own kijun.b runs in its place when there is one, else the fleet-repo standard v1. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::tree_case(file, kind_of)` |  |  |  | tree_case: an evaluated tree the tree-rule tests read, with one option at `file`: a pleme.x declaration, a blind pleme.x, or a hostname definition. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::example(sources, trees)` |  |  |  | example: one fixture case: the repository's sources and its evaluated trees. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::module_with(body)` |  |  |  | module_with: a NixOS module source around the given body, for fixtures. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::concept_sources(declared)` |  |  |  | concept_sources: a repository with one concept of each kind, every one declared through the trio factory when `declared`, none otherwise. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::trio_source(name, kind_of)` |  |  |  | trio_source: a shared module file that declares its concept through mkModuleTrio, of the given kind or none. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::fixtures` |  |  |  | fixtures: one violating and one clean case per rule, with how often the violating one fires; a rule cannot land without both. |
| `kakehashi::kijun::fired(cfg, c, id)` |  |  |  | fired: the findings of one rule on one fixture case. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::attr_for(kind, host)` |  |  |  | attr_for: the flake attribute that builds a host's system on its platform. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::policy(base_s, cap_s)` |  |  |  | policy: the backoff after a failed switch, from a base and a cap in seconds. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::backoff_ms(failures, pol)` |  |  |  | backoff_ms: how long a system waits after this many failures in a row: the base, doubled each failure, up to the cap; never with no failure or a zero base. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::entry(out, outcome, at_ms, why)` |  |  |  | entry: one outcome of a switch attempt for the history: the system, the outcome, when, and why. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::streak_step(acc, e)` |  |  |  | streak_step: count one history entry into a system's run of failures: a failure adds one, a success clears it, another system's entry changes nothing. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::streak(history, out)` |  |  |  | streak: a system's failures in a row at the end of the history, and when the last one was. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::decide(desired, current, history, now_ms, pol)` |  |  |  | decide: whether to switch to the desired system, skip it because it already runs, hold it while its failures back off, or refuse a desired value that is no system. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::hold_for(key, history, now_ms, pol)` |  |  |  | hold_for: the hold for a system whose failures still back off at this time, nil when it may be tried. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::hex40?(t)` |  |  |  | hex40?: the text is a full 40-character git revision. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::pinned?(flake)` |  |  |  | pinned?: the flake reference names an exact revision, so the same reference always evaluates to the same system. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::label(p)` |  |  |  | label: a short name for a system: the start of its hash and its name, since two systems often share a name. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::parse_entry(line)` |  |  |  | parse_entry: one history line read back as an entry, nil for a line that is not JSON. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::history(path)` |  |  |  | history: every readable entry of the switch history file, oldest first; none when the file is absent. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::entry_line(e)` |  |  |  | entry_line: an entry as the one JSON line the history file holds for it. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::keep_last(xs, n)` |  |  |  | keep_last: the newest n items of a list. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::record(path, e)` |  |  |  | record: append one outcome to the switch history file, keeping its newest 200 entries, and answer the entry. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::plan(flake, host, kind, current_link, hist, now_ms, pol)` |  |  |  | plan: what a system switch would do, as data; a pinned revision still backing off is held before anything evaluates. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::evaluated(flake, inst, current_link, hist, now_ms, pol)` |  |  | `B0084` | evaluated: the plan from a dry build of the installable: the decision against the running system with the build and fetch counts, or a refusal when the system does not evaluate. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::assoc_all(m, pairs)` |  |  |  | assoc_all: a map with every key and value pair set on it. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::config_defaults` |  |  |  | config_defaults: every kirikae setting and its default, grouped by concern. settei lays ~/.config/kirikae/kirikae.yaml (or the file KIRIKAE_CONFIG names) over it, then KIRIKAE_<GROUP>__<KEY> (KIRIKAE_PHASES__PLAN=0, KIRIKAE_FORCE=1); a value of the wrong type is refused. An empty host, kind, history, events or narrate means the computed default. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::config` |  |  |  | config: kirikae's settings as settei resolves them: defaults, then the config file, then the environment; read by path, config.guard.branch. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::env_aliases` |  |  |  | env_aliases: each grouped setting's environment name and the flat name it had before, which still sets it (sentinela passes KIRIKAE_HISTORY and KIRIKAE_BACKOFF_BASE_S). |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::or_computed(v, computed)` |  |  |  | or_computed: the setting, or the computed default when the setting is empty. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::state_file(name)` |  |  |  | state_file: where kirikae keeps a state file of this name, under the user's ~/.local/state/kirikae. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::settings` |  |  |  | settings: the settings one run works from: the resolved config with empty host, kind, history and events filled by their computed defaults. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::stdout_of_host` |  |  | `B0083` | stdout_of_host: the machine's short host name as `hostname -s` prints it, the default host to plan for. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::kind_of_host` |  |  | `B0083` | kind_of_host: the platform of the machine kirikae runs on, nixos on Linux and darwin otherwise. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::checkout_path(flake)` |  |  |  | checkout_path: the local directory a flake reference reads from, nil for a pinned or remote reference that has no checkout. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::git_out(dir, args)` |  |  | `B0083` | git_out: what a git command in the directory printed, trimmed, or nil when it failed; git never prompts for credentials. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::checkout_of(dir, branch)` |  |  |  | checkout_of: the facts the guard reads of a checkout: its HEAD, its uncommitted changes, and origin's rev of the branch. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::fast_forwardable?(c, flag)` |  |  |  | fast_forwardable?: true when the checkout is clean, readable, and only behind origin's rev, so a fast-forward cannot lose work; `flag` "0" turns fast-forwarding off. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::fast_forward(dir, c)` |  |  |  | fast_forward: bring a clean checkout that is only behind origin up to origin's rev before the guard judges it, and say so; anything else is returned as it was. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::checkout_refusal(c)` |  |  |  | checkout_refusal: why a checkout may not be planned: it is not a checkout, it has uncommitted changes, origin cannot be read, or it is not at origin's rev; nil when it may. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::base_name(t)` |  |  |  | base_name: the last part of a path, so a command is named without where it lives. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::writer_line?(l, host)` |  |  |  | writer_line?: the process line is a rebuild, or a nix build or eval of this host's system, which would race a switch. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::writers(ps_text, host)` |  |  |  | writers: the lines of a process listing that write this host's system. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::ps_bin` |  |  |  | ps_bin: the ps that lists processes, /bin/ps when it exists so a narrowed PATH still finds it. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::busy_refusal(ws)` |  |  |  | busy_refusal: the refusal naming the first other writer of the machine and the rev it builds, nil when there is none. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::writer_of(line)` |  |  |  | writer_of: a writer's process line, shortened to its command and the rev it builds. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::writer_rev(line)` |  |  |  | writer_rev: the pinned revision a writer's process line builds, or nil when it builds a moving ref or a path. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::heartbeat_stale?(hb, now, stale_ms)` |  |  |  | heartbeat_stale?: true when the gitops heartbeat names no time or was written longer ago than stale_ms, so a loop that hung mid-build is not taken for a live writer. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::gitops_handoff(f, s, hb_text)` |  |  |  | gitops_handoff: the revision the gitops loop is building when it is the busy writer and builds the checkout's published rev or one after it, so kirikae can leave the switch to it; nil otherwise. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::heartbeat_noop(hb_text, head, current)` |  |  |  | heartbeat_noop: the no-op plan when the gitops heartbeat says the loop already runs the checkout's rev unchanged, so nothing evaluates; nil otherwise. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::busy_hold(r, now)` |  |  |  | busy_hold: the plan that holds a minute because another writer is busy. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::refused_plan(r)` |  |  |  | refused_plan: the plan that refuses for a guard's refusal. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::survey(s)` |  |  | `B0083` | survey: the facts the guard judges: the checkout and its refusal, and a refusal naming another writer of the machine. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::guard_of(f, s)` |  |  | `B0084` | guard_of: the guard's verdict on surveyed facts: a refusal, a busy hold, the loop's no-op, or nil to go on and plan. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::guard(s)` |  |  |  | guard: the guard's verdict on the machine as it is now: refuse, hold for a busy writer, the loop's no-op, or nil to plan. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::judged(s)` |  |  |  | judged: the guard's surveyed facts with its verdict on them, so the guard's closing line can name both. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::after_wait(rev, wp, g0, host)` |  |  |  | after_wait: the verdict planning continues from: a hand-off when a gitops rev was handed over and nothing was followed, the guard's own when nothing waited, nothing after a followed build went stale, else what the wait ended on, which is nil when the writer cleared and nothing holds the machine. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::planned(s)` |  |  |  | planned: guard, wait and plan, each told as a phase when the narration is full, and the plan they reach with each phase's time and the fix for a refusal. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::plan_off?(g, s)` |  |  |  | plan_off?: true when the guard said go and KIRIKAE_PHASES__PLAN is 0; a guard verdict or an unset plan setting keeps today's path. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::unplanned_note` |  |  |  | unplanned_note: why the plan phase does not run when KIRIKAE_PHASES__PLAN is 0. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::unplanned` |  |  |  | unplanned: the plan a run carries when KIRIKAE_PHASES__PLAN is 0: switch, with no store path to verify against. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::handoff_note(rev, host)` |  |  |  | handoff_note: why kirikae leaves a switch to the gitops loop: it already builds the published rev and activates the machine itself. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::switch_skip(action, host)` |  |  |  | switch_skip: why the switch phase does not run, by the action the plan reached. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::heartbeat_path` |  |  |  | heartbeat_path: where the gitops loop writes its heartbeat, KIRIKAE_GITOPS__HEARTBEAT when set. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::fix_for(p, s)` |  |  |  | fix_for: what to do about a plan that refuses or holds, as the command or setting that clears it, or nil when there is nothing to fix. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::gitops_verdict(log_lines, rev)` |  |  |  | gitops_verdict: what the gitops loop's log says became of a revision it was building: deployed, failed or deferred, or nil while it says nothing final. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::log_line(l)` |  |  |  | log_line: a gitops log line as it reads in the narration: colour codes and the timestamp removed. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::follow(s, rev)` |  |  |  | follow: tell the gitops loop's log as it builds a revision, until the log says it deployed, failed or deferred it, the build ends, or the wait's ceiling passes. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::following(s, rev, seen, deadline_ms, started, said_ms)` |  |  |  | following: one poll of a followed gitops build: tell the log lines after the first seen ones, or once a minute that the build goes on while its log is quiet, and stop on a verdict, when the heartbeat no longer says it builds the rev, or at the deadline. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::plan_now(s)` |  |  |  | plan_now: the plan for the settings, guarded and waited as a run would be. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::plan_unguarded(s)` |  |  |  | plan_unguarded: the plan for the settings with no guard: a dry build compared with the running system and its history. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::summary(p)` |  |  |  | summary: the one line a brief narration prints for a plan: its action and why, the work it planned, and the fix for a refusal. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::node_step(acc, a)` |  |  |  | node_step: read one argument after `rebuild` toward the node it names, skipping flags and each `--nix-option` with its two values. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::node_arg(cmd)` |  |  |  | node_arg: the node a rebuild command names, nil when it names none. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::settings_for(cmd)` |  |  |  | settings_for: the settings for a rebuild command, with the host it names in place of this machine's. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::busy_hold?(p)` |  |  |  | busy_hold?: true when the guard's plan holds because another writer is busy; a nil plan, the guard's go, never holds. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::keep_waiting?(p, now, deadline_ms)` |  |  |  | keep_waiting?: the plan holds for another busy writer and the wait has not reached its deadline; a backoff hold is never waited out. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::say_wait?(why, said, now, said_ms)` |  |  |  | say_wait?: true when the waiting line changed or a minute passed since it was printed, so a long wait shows it is alive. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::waiting_note(writer, left_ms)` |  |  |  | waiting_note: the line a long wait repeats: who holds the machine and how long kirikae will still wait. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::await_writers(s, p, deadline_ms, said, said_ms)` |  |  |  | await_writers: wait while another writer holds the machine and the deadline allows, saying so now and then, and answer the guard's verdict when it ends. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::run(cmd)` |  |  |  | run: guard and plan a switch, run the rebuild command when the plan says to, verify the system it activated and record the outcome. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::switched(s, p, cmd, started)` |  |  | `B0083` | switched: hand the planned switch to the rebuild command, verify it, record the outcome, and close the run. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::finish(s, verb, p, out, text, started)` |  |  |  | finish: close a plan or a run: append its event to the log, print the closing line with the fix and where the log is, and return the outcome. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::event(s, verb, p, outcome, status, ms)` |  |  |  | event: one line of the event log: when, which verb and host, what was decided and why, the fix, the system and its work, each phase's time, and how it ended. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::log_event(path, keep, ev)` |  |  |  | log_event: append one event to the log as a JSON line, keeping only the newest lines so the log stays bounded; a log that cannot be written is reported once on stderr and never stops the rebuild. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::settle(s, p, status, v, ran)` |  |  |  | settle: record a finished rebuild in the history and answer its outcome; a command that exited 0 but did not switch is a failure. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::narrate_level(t)` |  |  |  | narrate_level: the KIRIKAE_NARRATE text as a level: full tells each phase before and after it runs, brief only the verdict, off nothing. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::phases` |  |  |  | phases: the five phases of a run, in the order they run. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::phase_line(state, name, note, ms)` |  |  |  | phase_line: one phase's step record: its state, its place among the phases, its name, a note and the time it took. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::tell(s, state, name, note, ms)` |  |  | `B0084` | tell: one phase line on stderr when the narration is full, and nothing otherwise. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::say(s, node)` |  |  | `B0084` | say: a whole line on stderr when the narration is full, painted as the phase lines are. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::phase(s, name, d, work)` |  |  |  | phase: run one phase between the line that says what it will do and the line that says what it found, and return its result and the time it took. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::expected_plan(hist)` |  |  |  | expected_plan: the median time of the last five timed plans in a history and how many there were, or nil when none was timed. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::lesson(name, d)` |  |  |  | lesson: what a phase is about to do and why it matters, as one sentence for the line that opens it. |
