| `kakehashi::kirikae::finding(name, d)` |  |  |  | finding: what a phase found, as the state its closing line shows and one sentence. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::opening(s)` |  |  |  | opening: the first line of a narrated run: what is rebuilt, from where, and the phases ahead. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::closing(text, status, ms, fix, log)` |  |  |  | closing: the end of a narrated run: a coloured circle for the verdict, the outcome and how long the whole run took, then the fix when there is one and where the event log is. |

| `kakehashi::kirikae::exit_not_switched` |  |  |  | exit_not_switched: the exit status of a rebuild that ran but left the old system running. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::exit_refused` |  |  |  | exit_refused: the exit status of a refused switch, which needs a fix before a retry. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::outcome_of(status, text)` |  |  |  | outcome_of: a command's outcome: the exit status a caller branches on and the text it prints. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::exit_status_of(answer)` |  |  |  | exit_status_of: the exit status of an answer; plain text exits 0. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::text_of(answer)` |  |  |  | text_of: the text an answer prints. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::outcome_field(answer, key, bare)` |  |  |  | outcome_field: the value at `key` of an `outcome_of` map, or `bare` for an answer that is plain text. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::main` |  |  | `B0083` | main: kirikae's command line: print the answer to its arguments and exit with its status. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::answer(args)` |  |  | `B0084` | answer: the answer to one kirikae command: plan, demo, config, run or record; a wrong use raises :usage. |
| `kakehashi::kirikae::demo_lines(kind)` |  |  |  | demo_lines: a whole narrated run on a scripted plan, line by line, built by the same words a real run prints with: a switch behind a writer that clears, a hand-off to the gitops loop, a followed gitops build, or a refusal with its fix. |

## kansuu

`kansuu` 0.1.0 · needs: —

kansuu (関数) — functions about functions.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kansuu::identity(x)` |  | yes |  | identity: return the argument unchanged. |
| `kansuu::constantly(x)` |  |  |  | constantly: The constant function. Useful wherever a callback is required and ignored. |
| `kansuu::compose(f, g)` |  | yes |  | compose(f, g) applies g FIRST, then f — the mathematical order. |
| `kansuu::pipe(f, g)` |  | yes |  | pipe(f, g) applies f FIRST — reading order. |
| `kansuu::flip(f)` |  | yes |  | flip a two-argument function's arguments. |
| `kansuu::iterate_n(f, x, n)` |  |  |  | iterate_n: Apply f to x, n times. |
| `kansuu::compose3(f, g, h)` |  |  |  | compose3: Three-place versions of the two orders. Written out rather than expressed as |
| `kansuu::pipe3(f, g, h)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kansuu::pipe_all(fs)` |  |  |  | pipe_all folds a LIST of functions into one, left to right. |
| `kansuu::compose_all(fs)` |  |  |  | compose_all: The mathematical order: compose_all([f, g, h]) is f∘g∘h, so h runs first. |
| `kansuu::curry2(f)` |  |  |  | curry2 turns f(a, b) into f(a)(b), so the first argument can be fixed and the |
| `kansuu::uncurry2(g)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kansuu::apply_twice(f, x)` |  |  |  | apply_twice: f(f(x)). The general form is iterate_n; this exists because doubling up is |
| `kansuu::iterated(f, n)` |  |  |  | iterated: The combinator form of iterate_n: n is fixed, the value is not. |
| `kansuu::on(op, key)` |  |  |  | on: Haskell's `on`: compare/combine two values THROUGH a key function. |
| `kansuu::juxt(fs)` |  | yes |  | juxt(fs) applies EVERY function to ONE value and collects the results. |
| `kansuu::juxt_with(q, fs)` |  |  |  | juxt_with(q, fs) returns a function of x that hands q the list fs and a |
| `kansuu::both_results(f, g)` |  |  |  | both_results: The two-function case, named, because a pair is what most callers want and |
| `kansuu::fork(op, f, g)` |  |  |  | fork is both_results with the pair already combined: op(f(x), g(x)). |
| `kansuu::tap(f)` |  | yes |  | tap runs f for its effect and hands the ORIGINAL value on. |
| `kansuu::apply_when(p, f)` |  |  |  | apply_when: Apply f only where p holds; elsewhere pass the value through unchanged. |
| `kansuu::maybe(x, default, f)` |  |  |  | maybe: `default` when x is nil, else f(x); the nil check every optional value needs, written once. |

| `kansuu::until_stable(f, x, max_steps)` |  |  |  | until_stable: Apply f until the value stops changing, or until max_steps applications. |

| `kansuu::stable_steps(f, x, max_steps)` |  |  |  | stable_steps: How many applications until stable; max_steps if it never got there. |

## khipu

`khipu` 0.1.0 · needs: deeta, kueri, kyohi, moji, retsu

khipu — blue's knowledge graph (docs/KHIPU.md): RDF terms as a closed sum, quads, and N-Triples and N-Quads read and written.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `khipu::control_or_space?(c)` |  |  |  | control_or_space? tells whether one character is a space or an ASCII control character. |
| `khipu::letter?(c)` |  |  |  | letter? tells whether one character is an ASCII letter. |
| `khipu::digit?(c)` |  |  |  | digit? tells whether one character is an ASCII digit. |
| `khipu::iri_problem(text)` |  |  |  | iri_problem: Why `text` is not an absolute IRI N-Triples accepts, or nil when it is one. |
| `khipu::lang_problem(tag)` |  |  |  | lang_problem: Why `tag` is not a BCP 47 language tag, or nil when it is one: a primary subtag of 1 to 8 letters, then subtags of 1 to 8 letters or digits. |
| `khipu::refuse(why)` |  |  |  | refuse raises `:rdf_refused` with the reason. |
| `khipu::iri(text)` |  |  |  | iri: An IRI term; a text that is not an absolute IRI is refused. |
| `khipu::blank(scope, label)` |  |  |  | blank: A blank node: its label and the scope of the document it was read from. |
| `khipu::literal(text)` |  |  |  | literal: A plain literal; RDF 1.1 reads it as an `xsd:string`. |
| `khipu::lang(text, tag)` |  |  |  | lang: A language-tagged literal. Its datatype is `rdf:langString` by construction, so it has no datatype field; the tag is kept in lower case, as tags compare without case. |
| `khipu::typed(text, datatype)` |  |  |  | typed: A literal with a datatype IRI. `xsd:string` is the plain literal, and `rdf:langString` needs a tag, so it is refused here. |
| `khipu::term?(t)` |  |  |  | term? tells whether a value is one of the five terms. |
| `khipu::quad(s, p, o, g)` |  |  |  | quad: A statement in a graph. The subject is an IRI or a blank node, the predicate an IRI, the object any term, and the graph nil (the default graph), an IRI or a blank node. |
| `khipu::escaped(text)` |  |  |  | escaped is a literal's text with its backslashes, quotes and line breaks escaped for N-Triples. |
| `khipu::write_term(t, scope)` |  |  |  | write_term: A term in N-Triples. A blank node from `scope` keeps its label; one from another scope gets its scope's hash in front, so two documents' labels never meet. |
| `khipu::write_quad(q, scope)` |  |  |  | write_quad: One N-Quads line, ending in ` .`; a quad in the default graph is an N-Triples line. |
| `khipu::write(quads, scope)` |  |  |  | write: Quads as N-Quads text, one line each. |
| `khipu::term_id(t)` |  |  |  | term_id: A term's content address: the BLAKE3 of its N-Triples form, its scope included for a blank node, so equal terms share an id on every machine. |
| `khipu::at(cs, i)` |  |  |  | at is the character at index i, or nil past the end. |
| `khipu::ok(v, i)` |  |  |  | ok is a reading that succeeded: its value and the index after it. |
| `khipu::no(why)` |  |  |  | no is a reading that was refused, with its reason. |
| `khipu::skip_ws(cs, i)` |  |  |  | skip_ws is the index of the first character at or after i that is not a space or a tab. |
| `khipu::uchar(cs, i)` |  |  |  | uchar reads a `\\u` or `\\U` escape whose letter is at i: the character it names. |
| `khipu::iri_text(cs, i, acc)` |  |  |  | iri_text reads an IRI's characters up to its closing `>`, escapes decoded. |
| `khipu::read_iri(cs, i)` |  |  |  | read_iri reads `<...>` at i as an IRI term, refusing one that is not absolute. |
| `khipu::label_char?(c)` |  |  |  | label_char? tells whether a character can be part of a blank node label. |
| `khipu::label_end(cs, i)` |  |  |  | label_end is the index just after the label characters that start at i. |
| `khipu::read_blank(cs, i, scope)` |  |  |  | read_blank reads `_:label` at i as a blank node of `scope`; a final `.` ends the statement, not the label. |
| `khipu::string_text(cs, i, acc)` |  |  |  | string_text reads a literal's characters up to its closing quote, escapes decoded. |
| `khipu::tag_end(cs, i)` |  |  |  | tag_end is the index just after the language tag characters that start at i. |
| `khipu::read_literal(cs, i)` |  |  |  | read_literal reads a quoted literal at i with its tag or datatype, refusing one with both. |
| `khipu::read_node(cs, i, scope, kinds)` |  |  |  | read_node reads the term at i when its kind is one of `kinds`, and refuses any other. |
| `khipu::read_rest(cs, i)` |  |  |  | read_rest accepts what follows a statement's dot: nothing, or a comment. |
| `khipu::then(r, f)` |  |  |  | then passes a refused reading through, and gives a successful one to f. |
| `khipu::read_graph(cs, i, scope, with_graphs)` |  |  |  | read_graph reads an N-Quads graph label at i, or nil when there is none or the text is N-Triples. |
| `khipu::read_end(cs, i, made)` |  |  |  | read_end requires the statement's dot at i, then nothing but a comment. |
| `khipu::read_statement(cs, scope, with_graphs)` |  |  |  | read_statement reads one line's subject, predicate, object, optional graph and dot as a quad. |
| `khipu::blank_line?(cs)` |  |  |  | blank_line? tells whether a line holds no statement: only spaces, or a comment. |
| `khipu::read(text, scope, with_graphs)` |  | yes |  | read: The statements of N-Triples (`with_graphs` false) or N-Quads text. Each line is judged alone: a bad line is a refusal with its line number and reason, and the lines around it are still read. `scope` names the document, for its blank nodes. |
| `khipu::rdf_refused?(f)` |  |  |  | rdf_refused? tells whether calling f raises `:rdf_refused`. |
| `khipu::nt(text)` |  |  |  | nt reads N-Triples text in the scope `t`. |
| `khipu::one(text)` |  |  |  | one is the single quad an N-Triples line reads to, asserting nothing was refused. |
| `khipu::refused_why(text)` |  |  |  | refused_why is the reason the single line of `text` is refused. |
| `khipu::encoded(text)` |  |  |  | encoded: A text made safe to stand in an IRI: each forbidden character and `%` written as its percent escape, so `decoded` gives the text back. |
| `khipu::decoded(text)` |  |  |  | decoded: The text `encoded` was given. |
| `khipu::store_row(q)` |  |  |  | store_row is one quad as the JSON row a store holds: each term in N-Triples, the default graph as "". |
| `khipu::load(path, quads)` |  |  |  | load writes `quads` to `path` as a store, one JSON row per distinct quad (a dataset is a set, so a repeated statement is held once), and answers the store: its path and quad count. DuckDB reads the file; blue holds no index of it. |
| `khipu::var(name)` |  |  |  | var: A variable of a basic graph pattern, by name. |
| `khipu::var?(t)` |  |  |  | var? tells whether a pattern position is a variable. |
| `khipu::pattern(s, p, o)` |  |  |  | pattern: One triple pattern; each position a term or a variable. It matches a quad of any graph. A subject that is a literal, or a predicate that is not an IRI, could match nothing and is refused. |
| `khipu::question(vars, where, absent, opts)` |  |  |  | question: What to ask a store: the variables answered, the patterns that must all match, the pattern groups that must not match (each a NOT EXISTS), and the row budget (BUDGET unless `opts[:budget]`). |
| `khipu::bind_group(patterns, prefix, bound)` |  |  |  | bind_group adds one group of patterns, aliased `prefix0`, `prefix1`..., to a binding: each variable's first column, and an equality for every other position. |
| `khipu::bind_position(st, t, column)` |  |  |  | bind_position is one pattern position: a constant becomes an equality, a bound variable an equality with its column, a new variable a binding. |
| `khipu::from_list(n, prefix)` |  |  |  | from_list is the FROM list of a group: the store once per pattern. |
| `khipu::absent_sql(patterns, k, bound)` |  |  |  | absent_sql is one group that must not match, as NOT EXISTS over the outer binding. |
| `khipu::question_sql(path, q)` |  |  |  | question_sql lowers a question to one DuckDB query over the store at `path`: a self-join per pattern, a NOT EXISTS per absent group, DISTINCT rows ordered by every answered variable, one row past the budget so an overrun is seen. A question with no pattern, or one answering a variable no pattern binds, is refused. |
| `khipu::read_term(text)` |  |  |  | read_term reads one stored N-Triples term back. |
| `khipu::ask(store, q)` |  |  | `B0084` | ask: The answers of a question over a store, each a map from variable name to term, distinct and ordered. More rows than the budget is refused as `:khipu_budget`, naming the bound, never a partial answer; an empty store answers [], and a store DuckDB cannot read raises `:kueri_query`. |
| `khipu::scratch(name)` |  |  |  | scratch is a path under TMPDIR for a test's store. |
| `khipu::ex(local)` |  |  |  | ex is a test IRI in the http://e/ space. |
| `khipu::fixture_store(path)` |  |  |  | fixture_store loads the tests' five quads: two definitions reach the network, one the clock, a test exercises one of the two, and one carries a gloss. |
| `khipu::untested(caps)` |  |  |  | untested is the question "which subjects reach `caps` and are exercised by nothing?". |

## kodou

`kodou` 0.1.0 · needs: ibuki, jisho, kyohi, moji, retsu

kodou (鼓動) — the dual-pulse wire: a Flash channel paced by ibuki's token bucket and a Tide channel paced by ibuki's LEDBAT controller from the one-way delay each acknowledgement measures, over one UDP socket the host frames (0x02 Flash, 0x01 Tide); and the gossip of crash proofs on it: an alert on Flash, the artifact on Tide when a peer asks for it, verified by its content hash and kept in kensa's `kensa-immunity.v1` file.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kodou::schema` |  |  |  | schema: the immunity file's schema, as kensa reads it. |
| `kodou::kensa_capabilities` |  |  |  | kensa_capabilities: the host capabilities a `kensa-immunity.v1` entry can name; a crash that breached any other is refused, so one entry never makes the whole file unreadable. |
| `kodou::part_room` |  |  |  | part_room: the bytes of artifact text one Tide part carries, under the 1271 a Tide frame holds less the part's header. |
| `kodou::link(addr, opts)` |  | yes | `B0083` | link: one end of the wire bound to `addr`, with the channels `channels:` names (`%i[flash tide]`), a Flash bucket from `flash:` and a Tide controller from `tide:` (ibuki's defaults), and the Tide ring's bounds from `ring:`. |
| `kodou::address(l)` |  |  | `B0083` | address: the host:port a link's socket is bound to, which a peer sends to. |
| `kodou::emit(l, peer, payload)` |  |  | `B0083` | emit: the link after one Flash frame to `peer`, sent when the bucket holds its bytes (the payload and its framing byte) and after waiting out the bucket when it does not; `sent:` names when and how many bytes. A frame past the MTU is a :bad_option raise and sends nothing. Under a real clock the host reads its clock a moment after the bucket did, so at the margin it can refuse what the bucket passed: emit waits the host's `wait_ms` once and sends again, and a second refusal raises. |
| `kodou::queue(l, peer, body)` |  |  | `B0083` | queue: the link with one Tide data frame for `peer` in the host's ring, counting the oldest frames the ring evicted to hold it. |
| `kodou::pump(l)` |  |  | `B0083` | pump: the link after the ring released what the Tide window allows: the controller's window less the bytes in flight, each frame stamped with the clock. |
| `kodou::acked(l, delay_ms, bytes)` |  |  |  | acked: the link after an acknowledgement of `bytes` that measured `delay_ms` of one-way delay: the controller moves its window, and the bytes leave flight. |
| `kodou::take(l, timeout_ms)` |  | yes | `B0083` | take: the next Tide frame within `timeout_ms`: a data frame is acknowledged at once with the delay it measured and its body delivered; an acknowledgement moves the window; `idle:` when none came. |
| `kodou::ack_of(payload)` |  |  |  | ack_of: the delay and bytes an acknowledgement's payload names, or nil for anything that is not one. |
| `kodou::parts(text, room)` |  |  |  | parts: a text cut into pieces of at most `room` UTF-8 bytes, never inside a character. |
| `kodou::part_body(hash, index, count, part)` |  |  |  | part_body: the Tide body of the `index`th of `count` parts of the artifact named `hash`. |
| `kodou::read_body(body)` |  |  |  | read_body: a gossip body read back: `{kind: :want, hash:}`, `{kind: :part, hash:, index:, count:, part:}`, or nil for anything else. |
| `kodou::hash_ok?(h)` |  |  |  | hash_ok?: whether a text is a BLAKE3 hash as kensa writes one: `b3:` and 64 lowercase hex digits. |
| `kodou::crash_hash(canon)` |  |  |  | crash_hash: a crash's content address: `b3:` and the BLAKE3 hash of its canonical JSON, the digest tameshi and kensa compute. |
| `kodou::violation_of(doc)` |  |  |  | violation_of: what a crash breached: its `violation` block when it has one (blue-trace version 3), else the capability breach its tape ends on (`class`, `capability`, `host`), or nil. |
| `kodou::alert_of(canon)` |  |  |  | alert_of: the Flash alert a crash's canonical JSON makes: its crash hash, the crashed definition's def_hash, its violation class and its size. |
| `kodou::entry_of(canon)` |  |  |  | entry_of: the `kensa-immunity.v1` entry a crash's canonical JSON makes, or a :refused raise naming why: a crash that breached no lattice ceiling, a capability the file cannot name, or a def_hash that is not one. |
| `kodou::be32(n)` |  |  |  | be32: a count as four big-endian bytes. |
| `kodou::length_prefixed(fields)` |  |  |  | length_prefixed: texts as one byte list, each its UTF-8 length in four big-endian bytes then its bytes, so no two lists of texts encode alike (tameshi's `length_prefixed`). |
| `kodou::immunity_digest(entries)` |  |  |  | immunity_digest: the digest a `kensa-immunity.v1` file carries over its entries: BLAKE3 over the schema and the canonical entries, length-prefixed. |
| `kodou::immunity_text(entries)` |  |  |  | immunity_text: the canonical text of a `kensa-immunity.v1` file holding `entries`. |
| `kodou::immunity_entries(text)` |  |  |  | immunity_entries: the entries of a `kensa-immunity.v1` file's text, empty for nil, or a :refused raise when its schema, its order or its digest does not read. |
| `kodou::immunity_add(entries, entry)` |  |  |  | immunity_add: entries with one more, sorted by crash hash; the same entries when one already holds its crash hash. |
| `kodou::immunize(path, canon)` |  |  |  | immunize: the entry a crash's canonical JSON makes, appended to the `kensa-immunity.v1` file at `path` (created when absent) after the file's own digest is checked. |
| `kodou::node(l, immunity)` |  |  |  | node: a gossip node on a link, keeping its immunity file at `immunity`: the crashes it holds to serve, the alerts it wants the artifact of, the parts it has of each, and the crash hashes its file already holds. |
| `kodou::publish(n, peer, text)` |  |  |  | publish: the node after alerting `peer` on Flash to a crash it read (`text`, any `.bluecrash`), holding the crash's canonical JSON to serve when the peer asks. |
| `kodou::heed(n, timeout_ms)` |  |  | `B0083` | heed: the node after the next Flash frame within `timeout_ms`: an alert for a crash it neither holds, wants nor is immune to is wanted, and the want goes to its sender on Tide. |
| `kodou::tend(n, timeout_ms)` |  |  |  | tend: the node after the next Tide frame within `timeout_ms`: a want for a crash it holds queues every part to the asker; a part of a wanted crash is kept, and the last part verifies the whole against its hash and the alert and immunizes it; `idle:` when no frame came. |
| `kodou::served(n, peer, hash)` |  |  |  | served: the node after queueing every part of the crash `hash` it holds for `peer` and pumping the window; the same node when it holds no such crash. |
| `kodou::gathered(n, m)` |  |  |  | gathered: the node after keeping one part of a wanted crash; with every part in hand the artifact is checked (its hash, its canonical form, the alert's def_hash and class) and immunized, or refused with why. |
| `kodou::refusal_of(text, hash, a)` |  |  |  | refusal_of: why an artifact gathered for the alert `a` is refused, or nil: its hash, its canonical form, the alert's def_hash and class, and the entry it makes must all hold. |
| `kodou::step(n, timeout_ms, rounds)` |  |  |  | step: the node after one round: up to `rounds` Flash frames heeded and up to `rounds` Tide frames tended, each waiting at most `timeout_ms`, then the window pumped. |
| `kodou::fixture_crash(capability, def_hash)` |  |  |  | fixture_crash: the canonical JSON of a blue-trace version 3 crash of `%root/reader` that breached `capability` through `read_file`, for the tests beside this file. |

## kousei

`kousei` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, retsu

kousei (構成) — a system composed entry by entry: each entry checked, configured off, tuned or refused alone, then judged into what stands.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `kousei::verdict(ok, why)` |  |  |  | verdict: nil when ok, else why. |
| `kousei::flag` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::text` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::one_of(choices)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::int_in(lo, hi)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::list_of(check)` |  |  |  | list_of: Every element passes `check`; the first failure is the why. |
| `kousei::first_why(check, xs)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::field(key, check)` |  |  |  | field: A field of a record: its key and its check, optional unless `required`. |
| `kousei::required(f)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::record_whys(table, m)` |  |  |  | record_whys: Why a record breaks its table: missing required fields, bad values, and keys the table does not know. An `:open` table holds any record, because the host that reads it checks its fields. |
| `kousei::loosened(table)` |  |  |  | loosened: A record table that checks only the fields it is given. An `:open` table stays open. |
| `kousei::entry(kind, name, value)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::kind_of(e)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::name_of(e)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::value_of(e)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::config(group, fields)` |  |  |  | config: An entry that sets fields of one config group of the host; the host checks each field alone. |
| `kousei::off(kind, name)` |  |  |  | off: Declared, and configured off: it stays in the declaration and does nothing. |
| `kousei::tune(kind, name, fields)` |  |  |  | tune: Fields laid over an entry declared earlier. |
| `kousei::where(e)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::refuse(fault, e, why)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::overlay(base, fields)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::targets?(t, e)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::duplicate_marks(es)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::entry_marks(schema, es)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::tune_marks(schema, t, es)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::tuned(e, tunes)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::zipped(xs, ys)` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::judge(schema, entries)` |  |  |  | judge: The declaration judged against `schema`: `accepted` (tuned, not off), `off`, and `refusals`, each refusal scoped to its entry. |
| `kousei::of_kind(judged, kind)` |  |  |  | of_kind: The accepted entries of one kind. |
| `kousei::schema_fixture` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::fixture` |  |  |  |  |
| `kousei::faults(j)` |  |  |  |  |

## maakuappu

`maakuappu` 0.1.0 · needs: kyohi, retsu

maakuappu (マークアップ) — XML as data: a document parsed by the runtime's xml_parse (quick-xml) into element maps {name, attributes, children, text}, the queries a reader of a scanner export asks of it (children and descendants by name, attributes, text, a path of element names), and the element rendered back with every value escaped.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `maakuappu::parse(text)` |  |  |  | parse: the root element of an XML document. Raises :xml_malformed when the text is not well-formed and :xml_refused when it declares or references a DTD entity. |
| `maakuappu::render(el)` |  |  |  | render: the element as XML text that parse reads back equal, attributes in key order and every value escaped. |
| `maakuappu::escape(text)` |  |  |  | escape: text made safe inside an XML element or a double-quoted attribute, with &, <, > and " written as entities, for XML built as text rather than through render. |
| `maakuappu::element(name, attributes, children, text)` |  |  |  | element: a new element with this name, attributes map, children and text, the shape parse returns. |
| `maakuappu::children(el, name)` |  | yes |  | children: the child elements of el named name, in document order; [] when el is nil. |
| `maakuappu::child(el, name)` |  |  |  | child: the first child element of el named name, or nil. |
| `maakuappu::descendants(el, name)` |  |  |  | descendants: every element below el named name, in document order, at any depth; [] when el is nil. |
| `maakuappu::attr(el, key)` |  |  |  | attr: the value of el's attribute key, or nil when el is nil or has no such attribute. |
| `maakuappu::text_of(el)` |  |  |  | text_of: el's own text with surrounding whitespace trimmed, or nil when el is nil. |
| `maakuappu::find(el, path)` |  | yes |  | find: every element reached from el by following path, a list of element names, one level per name (the XPath a/b/c); [el] for the empty path, [] when el is nil. |

## okite

`okite` 0.1.1 · needs: junjo, kyohi, moji, ran, retsu

okite (掟) — blue's decisions, each enforced by laws written in blue.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `okite::law_full_path_reaches_every_depth` | `ok_law_full_path_reaches_every_depth` (old) |  |  | law_full_path_reaches_every_depth holds that a full path names a definition at each depth of this bidama. |
| `okite::law_one_use_reaches_every_level_below` | `ok_law_one_use_reaches_every_level_below` (old) |  |  | law_one_use_reaches_every_level_below holds that `use("okite/rung")` alone brings `okite/rung/deep/floor`, two levels down, by its full path. |
| `okite::law_listed_name_is_bare` | `ok_law_listed_name_is_bare` (old) |  |  | law_listed_name_is_bare holds that a name the file lists from a level is written bare. |
| `okite::gen(seed, depth)` | `ok_gen` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::kind_count(depth)` | `ok_kind_count` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::gen_kind(k, s, depth)` | `ok_gen_kind` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::gen_kind_more(k, s, depth)` | `ok_gen_kind_more` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::gen_kind_rest(k, s, depth)` | `ok_gen_kind_rest` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::half(s)` | `ok_half` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::gen_list(s, n, depth, acc)` | `ok_gen_list` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::gen_map(s, n, depth, acc)` | `ok_gen_map` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::values(seed, n)` | `ok_values` (old) |  |  | values: `n` generated values from `seed`, each up to two levels deep. |
| `okite::values_from(s, n, acc)` | `ok_values_from` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::pairs(seed, n)` | `ok_pairs` (old) |  |  | pairs: Consecutive pairs of generated values, so pairs mix kinds. |
| `okite::all(f, xs)` | `ok_all` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::law_record_equality_includes_the_name` | `ok_law_record_equality_includes_the_name` (old) |  |  | law_record_equality_includes_the_name holds that two records are equal only with one name and equal fields, and never equal a plain map. |
| `okite::law_record_predicate_is_the_name_and_the_fields` | `ok_law_record_predicate_is_the_name_and_the_fields` (old) |  |  | law_record_predicate_is_the_name_and_the_fields holds that a predicate answers true only for its own name with exactly its fields, so a map that gains or loses a key is plain. |
| `okite::law_record_update_is_a_copy` | `ok_law_record_update_is_a_copy` (old) |  |  | law_record_update_is_a_copy holds that an update by spread makes a new record and leaves the old one as it was. |
| `okite::law_record_refuses_by_name` | `ok_law_record_refuses_by_name` (old) |  |  | law_record_refuses_by_name holds that a construction known only at run time raises :record, naming the record and each missing and unknown field. |
| `okite::law_record_crosses_json` | `ok_law_record_crosses_json` (old) |  |  | law_record_crosses_json holds that a record written as JSON reads back equal, with its name and fields under "$record". |
| `okite::law_record_prints_its_call` | `ok_law_record_prints_its_call` (old) |  |  | law_record_prints_its_call holds that a record prints as the call that builds it. |
| `okite::law_equal_reflexive` | `ok_law_equal_reflexive` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::law_equal_symmetric` | `ok_law_equal_symmetric` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::law_not_equal_is_negation` | `ok_law_not_equal_is_negation` (old) |  |  |  |
| `okite::law_numbers_by_value` | `ok_law_numbers_by_value` (old) |  |  |  |

## seikika

`seikika` 0.1.0 · needs: baito, ran, retsu, shinsuu

seikika (正規化) — canonicalization: one byte form for every blue value, and the root that names it.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `seikika::kind(v)` |  |  |  | kind: the kind of a value: `:nil`, `:bool`, `:int`, `:float`, `:str`, `:symbol`, `:keyword`, `:list`, `:map` or `:record` for one that has an encoding, and `:nan`, `:infinity`, `:function`, `:foreign`, `:channel`, `:fiber`, `:promise`, `:error` or `:unknown` for one that has none. |
| `seikika::u32(n)` |  |  |  | u32: a count or length as four big-endian bytes. |
| `seikika::text_body(s)` |  |  |  | text_body: a text as its four-byte length and its UTF-8 bytes. |
| `seikika::scale(x, k)` |  |  |  | scale: a float times two to the power `k`, moved fifty powers at a time so every step is exact. |
| `seikika::unit_exponent(a, e)` |  |  |  | unit_exponent: the exponent `e` with `a` over two to the `e` in [1, 2), from a first guess `e`. |
| `seikika::float_bits(x)` |  |  |  | float_bits: the IEEE-754 binary64 bits of a finite float, as an Int; -0.0 keeps its sign bit. |
| `seikika::bits_float(bits)` |  |  |  | bits_float: the float whose IEEE-754 binary64 bits these are; bits of NaN or an infinity raise `:decode_error`. |
| `seikika::refuse(found, path)` |  |  |  | refuse: the `:render_error` for a value with no encoding at `path`. |
| `seikika::record_parts(r)` |  |  |  | record_parts: a record's name and its field names in declared order, read from the name it carries. |
| `seikika::field_value(r, name)` |  |  |  | field_value: the value of the record field named by its text. |
| `seikika::encode_at(v, path)` |  |  |  | encode_at: the tagged bytes of a value found at `path`, without the format byte. |
| `seikika::encode_map(m, path)` |  |  |  | encode_map: a map's tagged bytes: its count, then each key and value, keys in the order of their bytes. |
| `seikika::encode_record(r, path)` |  |  |  | encode_record: a record's tagged bytes: its name, its field count, then each field's name and value in declared order. |
| `seikika::encode(v)` |  |  |  | encode: the canonical bytes of a value: the format byte, then its tagged form. A value with no encoding raises `:render_error` with `{path:, kind:}`. |
| `seikika::root(v)` |  |  |  | root: the identity of a value, `b3:` and the BLAKE3 hash of its canonical bytes. |
| `seikika::bad(at, why)` |  |  |  | bad: the `:decode_error` for bytes that are not a canonical encoding, naming where. |
| `seikika::byte_at(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | byte_at: the byte at a position, raising `:decode_error` past the end. |
| `seikika::read_count(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | read_count: a four-byte count at a position and where its body starts, refused when the count exceeds the bytes left. |
| `seikika::read_text(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | read_text: a length-prefixed UTF-8 text at a position and where it ends. |
| `seikika::named(k, s, at)` |  |  |  | named: the symbol or keyword a text names, when the reader gives back exactly that one; else `:decode_error`. |
| `seikika::read_items(bytes, at, n)` |  |  |  | read_items: `n` values in a row from a position, and where they end. |
| `seikika::read_map(bytes, at, n)` |  |  |  | read_map: `n` key and value pairs from a position, keys strictly ascending by their bytes, as a map and where it ends. |
| `seikika::read_record(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | read_record: a record's name, fields and values from a position, as the record and where it ends. |
| `seikika::read_value(bytes, at)` |  |  |  | read_value: the value tagged at a position, and where its bytes end. |
| `seikika::decode(bytes)` |  |  |  | decode: the value whose canonical bytes these are. Bytes that are not exactly one canonical encoding raise `:decode_error` naming the byte. |
| `seikika::same?(a, b)` |  |  |  | same?: whether two values are one value, kind for kind, read with the runtime's predicates and not with this package's `kind`: the oracle the tests hold `root` to. |
| `seikika::draw(seed, xs)` |  |  |  | draw: one element of a list chosen by a seed, and the next seed. |
| `seikika::gen_many(seed, depth, n)` |  |  |  | gen_many: `n` generated values at a depth, and the next seed. |
| `seikika::gen_scalar(seed)` |  |  |  | gen_scalar: a generated scalar, any kind a map key may be, and the next seed. |
| `seikika::gen(seed, depth)` |  |  |  | gen: a generated value to `depth` levels of nesting: scalars, lists, maps, lists of pairs and records, and the next seed. |
| `seikika::corpus` |  |  |  | corpus: the hand-picked values whose roots a lossy encoding shares: one text as a string, symbol and keyword; an Int, a Float and a string as keys; a list of pairs beside a map; empty containers nested; a record beside its plain map. |
| `seikika::caught(f)` |  |  |  | caught: the kind and data of the error calling `f` raises, or nil when it returns. |

| `settei::has?(config, path)` |  | yes |  | has?: whether a setting is defined. |
| `settei::need(config, path)` |  | yes |  | need: one setting by dotted path, raising not_found. |
| `settei::serving?` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::current` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::flag(settings, name)` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::setting(settings, name)` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::no_env(_name)` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::env_of(pairs)` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::knob_types` |  |  |  | knob_types: the types a knob may hold. |
| `settei::layers` |  |  |  | layers: the layers above the defaults, lowest first. |
| `settei::number_check` |  |  |  | number_check: the kousei check that a bound is a number. |
| `settei::annotation_table` |  |  |  | annotation_table: the kousei field table every knob's annotations are judged by. |
| `settei::fits?(t, v)` |  |  |  | fits?: whether a value is of a knob type; a whole number fits a float. |
| `settei::range_text(k)` |  |  |  | range_text: the legal values of a knob as a reader says them. |
| `settei::why_not(k, v)` |  |  |  | why_not: why a value is not legal for a knob, or nil when it is. |
| `settei::knob_whys(k)` |  |  |  | knob_whys: why a knob's annotations do not agree with each other or with its default. |
| `settei::knob(default, ann?)` |  |  |  | knob: one setting of a core model: its default and its annotations (type, doc, min, max, choices, unit, secret, since, deprecated, set_by). The type is the default's unless type: names it; set_by lists the layers that may set it, every layer when absent. A bad annotation raises :bad_option, naming it. |
| `settei::knob?(v)` |  |  |  | knob?: whether a value of a core model is a knob rather than a group. |
| `settei::dotted_key(path)` |  |  |  | dotted_key: a knob path as the dotted key people write. |
| `settei::knobs(model, path?)` |  |  |  | knobs: every knob of a core model with its path, groups walked in key order. |
| `settei::put_in(rec, path, v)` |  |  |  | put_in: a record with the value at a path set, groups made as needed. |
| `settei::defaults_of(model)` |  |  |  | defaults_of: the plain record of a core model's defaults, the shape read and resolve take. |
| `settei::text_get(m, key)` |  |  |  | text_get: the value of a map under a key compared as text, so a file's "port" finds a model's :port. |
| `settei::doc_at(doc, path)` |  |  |  | doc_at: the value at a knob path in a record, nil when any step is absent. |
| `settei::fault(key, layer, refused)` |  |  |  | fault: one refusal of settle, a kyohi refusal scoped to the key and the layer that gave the bad value. |
| `settei::strays(model, doc, path)` |  |  |  | strays: a fault for each key of a config file the model lacks, and for a group given as a plain value. |
| `settei::offer_of(app, layer, p, k, doc, lookup, given)` |  |  |  | offer_of: what one layer offers for one knob, the value and where it came from, or nil when the layer says nothing. |
| `settei::settle_knob(app, acc, row, doc, lookup, given)` |  |  |  | settle_knob: one knob taken through every layer: each legal offer sets it, each bad one is refused for this key alone. |
| `settei::shown_value(k, v)` |  |  |  | shown_value: a knob's value as explain, docs and refusals print it: <secret> for a secret, unset for nil. |
| `settei::settle(app, model, doc, lookup, given?)` |  |  |  | settle: a core model resolved through its layers, defaults, then the file, then the environment, then flags, merged per key. A bad value is refused for its key alone (the key, the layer, the range, the value kept) and every other key still loads. Returns config, sources (dotted key to layer), refusals and notices. |
| `settei::flag_word(path)` |  |  |  | flag_word: the command-line spelling of a knob path, groups joined by a dash. |
| `settei::flag_key(path)` |  |  |  | flag_key: the key a knob's flag has in a hikisuu spec: the knob's own key at the top, its path joined by underscores inside a group. |
| `settei::flag_kind(k)` |  |  |  | flag_kind: the hikisuu kind of a knob's flag: a switch for bool, int for int, text otherwise. |
| `settei::by_flag?(k)` |  |  |  | by_flag?: whether a flag may set a knob. |
| `settei::explain_flag` |  |  |  | explain_flag: the --explain KEY flag every program with a core model has. |
| `settei::knob_flags(model, below)` |  |  |  | knob_flags: the hikisuu flags of a model's knobs, each defaulting to its value in below, or to nil when below is :unset. |
| `settei::spec_with(model, frame, below)` |  |  |  | spec_with: a frame with its knob flags and --explain added, refusing a frame flag the model already renders. |
| `settei::cli_spec(model, frame, below?)` |  |  |  | cli_spec: the hikisuu spec of a program: its frame (name, about, commands, positionals) with a flag for each knob a flag may set, typed, floored and documented from the model, defaulting to the value below the flag layer, and --explain KEY. |
| `settei::given_flags(model, parsed)` |  |  |  | given_flags: the knob values a parsed line set, by dotted key; a flag left out is absent. |
| `settei::explain_of(args)` |  |  |  | explain_of: the key a line asks to explain and the line without it, or nil when it asks none. |
| `settei::source_text(app, r, layer)` |  |  |  | source_text: the layer a value came from as explain says it: the env name or the flag when one set it. |
| `settei::explain(app, model, settled, key)` |  |  |  | explain: one setting as a person reads it: its value, the layer it came from, its default, range, type, unit, doc, the layers that may set it, its environment name and flag, since and deprecated. A secret's value and default read <secret>; an unknown key raises :usage naming the settings. |
| `settei::usage(frame, spec, text)` |  |  |  | usage: a usage refusal of a line: exit 64, the fault, the usage line and where help is. |
| `settei::quietly_given(model, frame, args)` |  |  |  | quietly_given: the knob flags a line sets, or none when the line does not parse, so explain still answers. |
| `settei::run_opts(model, _frame, parsed, config)` |  |  |  | run_opts: the record a program runs with: the parsed line without its knob flags and --explain, with the settled config laid over it. |
| `settei::line(app, model, frame, args, doc, lookup)` |  |  |  | line: what one command line asks of a program with a core model, resolved through every layer: help, completion, explain, a usage refusal with exit 64 (a bad flag names its key and range), or the record to run with and the warnings of every file or environment value refused alone. |
| `settei::command(app, model, frame, f)` |  |  | `B0083` | command: the door of a program with a core model: argv, the discovered config file and the environment resolved by `line`; help, completion and explain printed, a bad line refused with exit 64, each refused file or environment value warned on stderr, and f called with the record. |
| `settei::defaults_dir` |  |  |  |  |
| `settei::table_head` |  |  |  | table_head: the two heading lines of a settings table. |
| `settei::code_cell(text)` |  |  |  | code_cell: a table cell in code type, empty for nothing. |
| `settei::about_cell(k)` |  |  |  | about_cell: a knob's doc as the table's last cell, with its deprecation said. |
| `settei::table(app, model)` |  |  |  | table: the settings table of a core model as Markdown, one row per knob: key, type, default (hidden for a secret), range, unit, environment name, flag, the layers that may set it, since and the doc. A program's generated docs page is this table, gated fresh. |
| `settei::form_reads(f, param)` |  |  |  | form_reads: the keys one form and everything inside it read off param. |
| `settei::reads(forms, param)` |  |  |  | reads: every top-level key a program's forms read off the record named param, as `get(param, :k)`, `param[:k]` or `param.k`, sorted, each once. |
| `settei::frame_keys(frame)` |  |  |  | frame_keys: the keys a parsed line holds besides the knobs: command, help, every positional and every frame flag. |
| `settei::unmodelled(model, frame, forms, param)` |  |  |  | unmodelled: each key the code reads off its record that neither the core model nor the command-line frame declares; a test asserts it is empty, so a knob read in code and missing from the model fails the build. |
| `settei::unread(model, forms, param)` |  |  |  | unread: each top-level key of a core model the code never reads off its record: a knob nothing uses. |
| `settei::model_fixture` |  |  |  | model_fixture: the core model the tests read: switches, floors, a group with a range, choices and a secret, and a deprecated knob. |
| `settei::kinds_of(found)` |  |  |  | kinds_of: the kind of each fault, in order. |
| `settei::frame_fixture` |  |  |  | frame_fixture: the command-line frame the tests read: one command and the roots. |
| `settei::forms_fixture` |  |  |  | forms_fixture: resolved forms the tests read, with reads of modelled, framed and unmodelled keys. |

## shinsatsu

`shinsatsu` 0.1.1 · needs: hikisuu, moji, retsu

shinsatsu (診察) — the credential doctor: a provider, the failure classes its credential helper can show, and for each class a diagnosis, the remediation steps and a verify step.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `shinsatsu::failure(name, markers, title, diagnosis, steps)` |  |  |  | failure: one failure class: the error markers that identify it, a short title, the diagnosis and the remediation. |
| `shinsatsu::provider(name, label, programs, facts, failures)` |  |  |  | provider: one credential provider: the helper programs it owns, the facts it reads from their arguments, and its failure classes in match order. |
| `shinsatsu::known(pairs)` |  |  |  | known: the labelled facts whose value is present, as {label:, value:} records. |
| `shinsatsu::azure_facts(f)` |  |  |  | azure_facts: what kubelogin's arguments say about the login: tenant, server id, login mode, client id. |
| `shinsatsu::step(argv, flow)` |  |  |  | step: one remediation command and its flow. |
| `shinsatsu::azure_login(f)` |  |  |  | azure_login: sign out, then sign in by device code to the tenant and the server id's scope. |
| `shinsatsu::with_flag(name, value)` |  |  |  | with_flag: a flag and its value, or nothing when the value is absent. |
| `shinsatsu::scoped(server_id)` |  |  |  | scoped: the `.default` scope of a server id, or nil. |
| `shinsatsu::or_else(value, fallback)` |  |  |  | or_else: the value, or the fallback when it is nil. |
| `shinsatsu::aws_facts(f)` |  |  |  | aws_facts: what `aws eks get-token`'s arguments say: profile, region, cluster, role. |
| `shinsatsu::aws_login(f)` |  |  |  | aws_login: an AWS SSO sign-in by device code, for the profile when one is named. |
| `shinsatsu::gcp_login(_f)` |  |  |  | gcp_login: a gcloud sign-in in the browser. |
| `shinsatsu::nerai_facts(f)` |  |  |  | nerai_facts: the registry target a refusal points at. |
| `shinsatsu::none(_f)` |  |  |  | none: no facts and no steps. |
| `shinsatsu::providers` |  |  |  | providers: every provider the doctor knows, each with its failure classes in match order. |
| `shinsatsu::owns(p, program)` |  |  |  | owns: whether a provider owns a credential helper program. |
| `shinsatsu::matches(c, text)` |  |  |  | matches: whether any marker of a failure class appears in the text, ignoring case. |
| `shinsatsu::verify_of(f)` |  |  |  | verify_of: the credential helper itself, the step that proves a sign-in worked; nothing for a refusal. |
| `shinsatsu::record(p, c, f)` |  |  |  | record: the plain record a host draws for one diagnosis. |
| `shinsatsu::diagnose(facts)` |  |  |  | diagnose: the record for one failed connect, or nil when no class matches. `facts` takes {program:, args:, message:, cluster:, server:, target:}. |
| `shinsatsu::first_match(pool, f)` |  |  |  | first_match: the first diagnosis any provider of the pool gives, or nil. |
| `shinsatsu::classes` |  |  |  | classes: every failure class, as {provider:, class:} records. |
| `shinsatsu::incomplete` |  |  |  | incomplete: every failure class that lacks a marker, a title, a diagnosis or a remediation. Remediation is a step, or the nerai answer to open the registry target. |
| `shinsatsu::diagnose_json(text)` |  |  |  | diagnose_json: `diagnose` over JSON text, for a host that speaks JSON. |
| `shinsatsu::keyed(m)` |  |  |  | keyed: the facts a host sent, read from a JSON map. |
| `shinsatsu::classes_json` |  |  |  | classes_json: `classes` as JSON text. |
| `shinsatsu::incomplete_json` |  |  |  | incomplete_json: `incomplete` as JSON text. |
| `shinsatsu::url_like?(w)` |  |  |  | url_like?: whether a word is an http or https URL. |
| `shinsatsu::code_like?(w)` |  |  |  | code_like?: whether a word looks like a device code: four or more capitals, digits or dashes. |
| `shinsatsu::tokens(text)` |  |  |  | tokens: the words of a text without a trailing full stop or comma. |
| `shinsatsu::prompt_of(text)` |  |  |  | prompt_of: the URL and the code a device or browser flow printed, each nil until it appears. |
| `shinsatsu::prompt_json(text)` |  |  |  | prompt_json: `prompt_of` as JSON text. |

## tesuto

`tesuto` 0.1.1 · needs: hadome, moji, retsu

tesuto (テスト) — testing: errors and snapshots as assertions, and the policy that holds every definition to a test beside it.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tesuto::raises?(kind, f)` |  |  |  | raises?: whether f raises that kind of error. |
| `tesuto::literal(value)` |  |  |  |  |
| `tesuto::skip_for(why)` |  |  |  | skip_for: end this test as skipped when `why` names a reason, and go on when it is nil. `blue test` counts it apart from a pass and prints the reason; `BLUE_TEST_SKIP=refuse` makes it a failure. |
| `tesuto::snapshot_path(opts)` |  |  |  |  |
| `tesuto::snapshot(value, opts)` |  |  |  | snapshot: compare a value with its stored copy; update on request. |
| `tesuto::scratch` |  |  |  |  |
| `tesuto::fresh_dir(label)` |  |  |  | fresh_dir: A new, empty directory under TMPDIR whose name starts with `label`, made now: one per test that needs a directory. The run owns it (`temp_dir`), so it is gone when the run ends, however the run ends. |
| `tesuto::caught(f)` |  |  |  |  |
| `tesuto::around(setup, teardown, f)` |  |  |  | around: f(v) between setup and teardown, where v is what setup() answers; teardown(v) runs whether f returned or raised, and f's value or error is kept. |
| `tesuto::changes?(probe, f)` |  |  |  | changes?: whether running f changes what probe() answers. |
| `tesuto::fixture(dir, name)` |  |  |  | fixture: the text of the file `name` kept in `dir` beside the test. A fixture that is not there raises `:fixture_missing`, never an empty text. |
| `tesuto::breaches(provider, contract)` |  |  |  | breaches: the keys of a contract a provider breaks: each key the provider lacks or holds as nil, or whose value the contract's predicate refuses. A contract is a map of key to predicate. |
| `tesuto::conforms?(provider, contract)` |  |  |  | conforms?: whether a provider keeps every key of a contract. |
| `tesuto::recorded_calls(m, seen)` |  |  |  | recorded_calls: a spy recorder's step: a `[:call, x]` message appends x to what it has seen, and `[:calls, pid]` sends `[:calls, seen]` to pid and keeps seen. |
| `tesuto::spy(f)` |  |  |  | spy: a stand-in that records each argument it is called with and answers what f answers. Call it as `s[:call](x)`; `calls(s)` reads the arguments back in order. |
| `tesuto::calls(s)` |  |  |  | calls: the arguments a spy was called with, in order. |
| `tesuto::describe(name, cases)` |  |  |  | describe: run each case of a group, `[label, f]`, and raise `:described` naming every case that raised, each as `name label: message`; answers how many cases ran. |
| `tesuto::uncovered(coverage)` |  |  |  | uncovered: The definitions no test reached, from one record of |
| `tesuto::levels` |  |  |  | levels: The three levels a package can be held at, from weakest to strongest. |
| `tesuto::level(scope, value, why)` |  |  |  | level: A policy entry: definitions in `scope` (a package name, or "*" for every |
| `tesuto::waive(name, why, until)` |  |  |  | waive: A policy entry: the qualified definition `name` may go untested until the |
| `tesuto::iso_date?(s)` |  |  |  | iso_date?: True for a YYYY-MM-DD shaped string; anything else is false. |
| `tesuto::before?(a, b)` |  |  |  | before?: True when date string `a` sorts strictly before `b`. |
| `tesuto::entry_refusal(e, today)` |  |  |  | entry_refusal: Why one policy entry is refused, or nil when it stands. `today` is passed |
| `tesuto::settle(entries, today)` |  |  |  | settle: Every entry judged alone: a refused entry is listed with its reason and |
| `tesuto::level_of(settled, package)` |  |  |  | level_of: The level a package is held at: its own entry, else the "*" entry, else |
| `tesuto::waived?(settled, name)` |  |  |  | waived?: True when a standing waiver in `settled` names the qualified definition `name`. |
| `tesuto::beside(settled, package, names)` |  |  |  | beside: One package's judgement: its level, the untested names no waiver covers, |
| `tesuto::stale(settled, names)` |  |  |  | stale: Waivers that cover no untested name: a stale waiver would hide the next |
| `tesuto::axis_states(axes)` |  |  |  | axis_states: Every state a closed set of axes can take, one map per combination, in axis order. `axes` is `[[name, [variant, …]], …]`. |
| `tesuto::variants(axes, axis)` |  |  |  | variants: The variants one axis of a closed set of axes can take, nil for an axis the set does not name. |
| `tesuto::row_key(axes, s)` |  |  |  | row_key: A state's row in a decision matrix: its variants in axis order, tab-separated. |
| `tesuto::matrix_header(axes)` |  |  |  | matrix_header: The header line of a decision matrix: the axis names, then `outcome`. |
| `tesuto::matrix_text(axes, verdict, states)` |  |  |  | matrix_text: A decision matrix as text: the header, then one line per state, its row key and `verdict(state)`. Write it to a fixture and gate it with `matrix_findings`; never edit it by hand. |
| `tesuto::matrix_rows(axes, text)` |  |  |  | matrix_rows: The data lines of a matrix text: every non-blank line but the header. |
| `tesuto::matrix_row(axes, line)` |  |  |  | matrix_row: One data line split into its row key and its outcome. |
| `tesuto::matrix_findings(axes, verdict, text, states)` |  |  |  | matrix_findings: What a committed matrix text gets wrong against `states` and `verdict`: a missing row, a row naming no state, a duplicate row, and a row whose outcome disagrees. An empty list is the all-variants proof. |
| `tesuto::duplicate_rows(rows)` |  |  |  | duplicate_rows: A `[:duplicate_row, key]` finding for every row whose key an earlier row already had. |
| `tesuto::coverage_names(text)` |  |  |  | coverage_names: The definitions no test reached, from the text `blue test --coverage --json` printed: every coverage record's `uncovered` list, in order. Lines that are not a record (the tally) are passed over. |
| `tesuto::coverage_records(text)` |  |  |  | coverage_records: The coverage records in the text `blue test --coverage --json` printed: each line that is a JSON object holding an `uncovered` list, whatever key it starts with. |
| `tesuto::coverage_verdict(settled, rows, m)` |  |  |  | coverage_verdict: One package's verdict under the settled policy and the recorded debt rows (hadome): off, green, red or blind, with the reason. A measurement is `{package:, blind:, names:}`; blind is never green. |
| `tesuto::coverage_arms(text)` |  |  |  | coverage_arms: The arms of decisions no test took, from the text `blue test --coverage --json` printed: every record's `untaken` list (`def#site:arm`), in order; a record from a blue that records no arms holds none. |
| `tesuto::judge_coverage(entries, baseline, measured, day)` |  |  |  | judge_coverage: The whole judgement over every measured package: each verdict, every refused entry, every stale waiver, the red verdicts, and the measurements a debt record is rendered from. `baseline` is hadome text (`untested: <pkg> <n>` rows); `day` is today as YYYY-MM-DD. |
| `tesuto::coverage_red?(r)` |  |  |  | coverage_red?: Whether a judgement fails: an enforced red package, a refused entry or a stale waiver. |
| `tesuto::coverage_report(r, baseline, home)` |  |  |  | coverage_report: The lines a judgement prints: each red package, refused entry and stale waiver, then, when debt shrank, the rows to record in `home`. |

| `todokede::kurashi::look(fields)` |  |  |  | look: the seat's look fields: its accent, its bar height, its focus ring. |
| `todokede::kurashi::idle(fields)` |  |  |  | idle: how long the seat waits with no input: `blank_s` to darken the panel, `lock_s` to lock. |
| `todokede::kurashi::sleep(fields)` |  |  |  | sleep: what the seat does around a system sleep: `lock_before` locks it first. |
| `todokede::kurashi::notes(fields)` |  |  |  | notes: how the seat shows notifications: `ms` on screen, `max` at once, `enabled`. |
| `todokede::kurashi::keyboard(layouts)` |  |  |  | keyboard: the layouts a seat cycles through, first one first, each `layout` or `layout+variant`. |
| `todokede::kurashi::lock_with(argv)` |  |  |  | lock_with: the program the seat starts to hold its lock, as an argv. |
| `todokede::kurashi::nord(band)` |  |  |  | nord: a band of the Nord palette as bare hex, the spelling a lock client's flags take. |
| `todokede::kurashi::swaylock(field_band)` |  |  |  | swaylock: the argv that runs swaylock in a cold field: Nord night, a small ring, nothing else moving. |
| `todokede::kurashi::command(name, action)` |  |  |  | command: a named thing the seat can do; a binding runs it by its name. A list is a program's argv, and no shell reads it. |
| `todokede::kurashi::action_of(action)` |  |  |  | action_of: what a command runs: a list becomes the action that starts that program, and anything else stands as written. |
| `todokede::kurashi::deed(verb)` |  |  |  | deed: the action that runs one of the seat's own verbs. |
| `todokede::kurashi::volume(step_pct)` |  |  |  | volume: the action that moves the default output's volume by a step in percent. |
| `todokede::kurashi::mute` |  |  |  | mute: the action that toggles the default output's mute. |
| `todokede::kurashi::backlight(step_pct)` |  |  |  | backlight: the action that moves the panel's brightness by a step in percent. |
| `todokede::kurashi::screenshot(dir)` |  |  |  | screenshot: the action that writes the screen to a PNG in a folder. |
| `todokede::kurashi::bind(chord, action, opts?)` |  |  |  | bind: a chord and the verb or named command it runs; `{locked: true}` lets it run behind a lock. |
| `todokede::kurashi::bar_module(name, fields)` |  |  |  | bar_module: a `.b` file under `bar/`, by its file name without `.b`, shown in the bar's right zone. |
| `todokede::kurashi::capabilities` |  |  |  | capabilities: the host capabilities a bar module may ask for. |
| `todokede::kurashi::command_fields` |  |  |  | command_fields: the fields each kind of command takes. |
| `todokede::kurashi::schema` |  |  |  | schema: the kinds a world may declare and the fields each takes. A config group's own fields, and a command's, are checked on the way in. |
| `todokede::kurashi::command_why(action)` |  |  |  | command_why: why a command's action is malformed, or nil. |
| `todokede::kurashi::open_fields(entries)` |  |  |  | open_fields: entries with a config group's fields and a command's action set aside, so the judge never reads them. |
| `todokede::kurashi::restored(e)` |  |  |  | restored: a judged entry with its set-aside fields back in its value. |
| `todokede::kurashi::standing_commands(accepted)` |  |  |  | standing_commands: the commands whose action is well formed, each bad one refused alone, in the order they came. |
| `todokede::kurashi::declare(entries)` |  |  |  | declare: a world's entries judged: `accepted`, `off` and `refusals`, each refusal scoped to its entry. |
| `todokede::motenashi::config(group, fields)` |  |  |  | config: an entry that sets fields of one config group of the host; the host checks each field alone. |
| `todokede::motenashi::look(fields)` |  |  |  | look: the panel's appearance fields: width, opacity, colours, corner radius, sizes. |
| `todokede::motenashi::spacing(fields)` |  |  |  | spacing: the panel's geometry fields: padding and the gaps. |
| `todokede::motenashi::motion(fields)` |  |  |  | motion: the host's motion fields: how long and how far its surfaces move. |
| `todokede::motenashi::font(family, size)` |  |  |  | font: the face and size the host draws its text in. |
| `todokede::motenashi::empty_text(words)` |  |  |  | empty_text: what the panel says when a query matches nothing. |
| `todokede::motenashi::more_text(words)` |  |  |  | more_text: what the panel says when more rows exist than it shows. |
| `todokede::motenashi::placeholder(words)` |  |  |  | placeholder: the text an empty input shows. |
| `todokede::motenashi::section(provider_name, fields)` |  |  |  | section: a heading over one provider's rows, and how much those rows weigh. |
| `todokede::motenashi::rank(weights)` |  |  |  | rank: the ranking weights: `frecency` over text relevance. |
| `todokede::motenashi::bind(chord, action)` |  |  |  | bind: a key chord and the action it runs. |
| `todokede::motenashi::command(name, fields)` |  |  |  | command: a small command of the host, written in blue; `bind` runs it by name, and it calls the function named `call`, by default its own name. |
| `todokede::motenashi::provider(name, fields)` |  |  |  | provider: a provider file under `providers/`, by its file name without `.b`. |
| `todokede::motenashi::off(kind, name)` |  |  |  | off: a declared entry, or a built-in provider, configured off. |
| `todokede::motenashi::without(builtin)` |  |  |  | without: a provider built into the host, configured off. |
| `todokede::motenashi::tune(kind, name, fields)` |  |  |  | tune: fields laid over an entry declared earlier. |
| `todokede::motenashi::actions` |  |  |  | actions: the words a binding may run. |
| `todokede::motenashi::capabilities` |  |  |  | capabilities: the host capabilities a provider may ask for. |
| `todokede::motenashi::number_field` |  |  |  | number_field: the check of a field that must be a number. |
| `todokede::motenashi::schema` |  |  |  | schema: the kinds a world may declare and the fields each takes. A config group's own fields are the host's to check. |
| `todokede::motenashi::declare(entries)` |  |  |  | declare: a launcher world's entries judged: `accepted`, `off` and `refusals`, each refusal scoped to its entry. |
| `todokede::motenashi::host_kinds` |  |  |  | host_kinds: the kinds every host's world takes. A host's own schema checks a config group and a command, and a binding's action is text the host resolves. |
| `todokede::motenashi::action_word` |  |  |  | action_word: the check of a binding's action in a host world: a word or a text, the host resolves it. |
| `todokede::motenashi::judged_for(kinds, entries)` |  |  |  | judged_for: a host's world judged against the kinds it takes, each refusal as a `kind` and a `why`, ready for the host to read. |
| `todokede::motenashi::open_fields(entries)` |  |  |  | open_fields: entries with a config group's fields set aside, so the judge never reads them. |
| `todokede::motenashi::restored(e)` |  |  |  | restored: a judged entry with its config group's fields back in its value. |
| `todokede::motenashi::row(title, fields)` |  |  |  | row: one answer of a provider: its title and any of `subtitle`, `icon`, `score`, `action`. |
| `todokede::motenashi::open_url(url)` |  |  |  | open_url: the action that opens a URL. |
| `todokede::motenashi::copy(words)` |  |  |  | copy: the action that puts text on the clipboard. |
| `todokede::motenashi::launch(path)` |  |  |  | launch: the action that opens a path: a desktop entry, a file. |
| `todokede::motenashi::run(argv)` |  |  |  | run: the action that runs a command, its argv as a list. |
| `todokede::motenashi::channel(cs, at)` |  |  |  | channel: the two hex digits at a position of a colour's characters, as a number. |
| `todokede::motenashi::color(hex_text)` |  |  |  | color: a `#rrggbb` colour as the unit channels a host's config takes. Anything else passes through for the host to refuse alone. |

## toride

`toride` 0.1.0 · needs: deeta, fairu, kyohi, retsu, shisutemu, tesuto, ukai

toride (砦) — a fort at the border: split-horizon DNS for a machine that roams between sites.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `toride::axes` |  |  |  | axes: The closed set of axes one entry's answer depends on. `site` is the entry's site against the site the machine stands on. |
| `toride::axis_names` |  |  |  | axis_names: The names of the axes, in table order. |
| `toride::state(m)` |  |  |  | state: An entry state over the axes; a value outside its axis is refused by name. |
| `toride::decide(s)` |  |  |  | decide: What one entry resolves to: its LAN address, its tailnet address, withheld with a reason, or blind when an axis could not be read. |
| `toride::outcome_label(o)` |  |  |  | outcome_label: An answer as one word, the matrix's outcome column. |
| `toride::verdict(s)` |  |  |  | verdict: The outcome label of `decide` over `s`, the cell a matrix row holds. |
| `toride::ip_int(ip)` |  |  |  | ip_int: A dotted IPv4 address as one integer. |
| `toride::int_ip(n)` |  |  |  | int_ip: An integer as a dotted IPv4 address. |
| `toride::span(prefix)` |  |  |  | span: How many addresses a prefix length covers. |
| `toride::parse_cidr(text)` |  |  |  | parse_cidr: `a.b.c.d/n` as its network and prefix, or nil when it is not one. |
| `toride::inside?(ip, net)` |  |  |  | inside?: Whether an address lies in a network. |
| `toride::mask_prefix(hex)` |  |  |  | mask_prefix: The prefix length of a netmask written in hex, as ifconfig prints it. |
| `toride::hex_digits` |  |  |  | hex_digits: The sixteen hex digits in lower case, in order; a digit's position is its value. |
| `toride::normal_mac(text)` |  |  |  | normal_mac: A MAC address in lower case with two digits an octet, or nil when it is not one. darwin's arp drops leading zeros. |
| `toride::redacted?(mac)` |  |  |  | redacted?: macOS shows an unprivileged reader every MAC as 02:00:00:00:00:00; that is no reading at all. |
| `toride::defaults` |  |  |  | defaults: Every setting's typed default; the Rust daemon's literals live here and nowhere else. |
| `toride::settings` |  |  |  | settings: Each setting's reader: its key and the kind of value it takes. |
| `toride::read_setting(kind, v)` |  |  |  | read_setting: A setting's JSON value read as its kind: `[:ok, value]`, or `[:bad, why]`. |
| `toride::words?(v)` |  |  |  | words?: Whether a value is a non-empty list of non-empty strings. |
| `toride::ports?(v)` |  |  |  | ports?: Whether a value is a non-empty list of whole numbers from 1 to 65535. |
| `toride::domain?(d)` |  |  |  | domain?: Whether text can stand in a dnsmasq `address=/name/ip` line. |
| `toride::domains?(v)` |  |  |  | domains?: Whether a value is a non-empty list in which each item passes `domain?`. |
| `toride::read_site(doc, taken)` |  |  |  | read_site: One site entry: `[:ok, site]` or `[:bad, refusal]`. The gateway defaults to the network's first address; the MAC is optional and, when given, must be one. |
| `toride::label_of(name)` |  |  |  | label_of: The name a refusal shows for an entry. An empty name shows as `(unnamed)`. |
| `toride::site_named(sites, name)` |  |  |  | site_named: The first site in `sites` with the name `name`, or nil. |
| `toride::read_node(doc, sites)` |  |  |  | read_node: One node: name, site, lan and tailnet addresses, domains. Its LAN address must lie in its site's network. |
| `toride::read_service(doc, sites)` |  |  |  | read_service: One service: domains, site, its LAN address, and whether a subnet route reaches it off site (`subnet_routed`, default false). |
| `toride::tagged(tag, rs)` |  |  |  | tagged: the values of the `[tag, value]` pairs in `rs` whose tag is `tag`, in order. |
| `toride::doc_list(doc, key)` |  |  |  | doc_list: The list at `key` in a JSON document. A key that is absent or holds no list gives the empty list. |
| `toride::read_sites(docs)` |  |  |  | read_sites: Each site document read by `read_site`, in order. A name that an earlier good site already holds is refused as named twice. |
| `toride::load(text)` |  |  |  | load: A config from its JSON text. Each setting, site, node and service is read alone: a malformed one is refused by name and its siblings stand. Text that is not a JSON object is unreadable, never an empty config. |
| `toride::object?(doc)` |  |  |  | object?: Whether a parsed JSON value is an object: the empty object, or a list of pairs whose keys are strings. |
| `toride::default_gateway(routes)` |  |  |  | default_gateway: The first default route through an IPv4 gateway, as `{gateway:, iface:}`, or nil. |
| `toride::parse_ifconfig(text)` |  |  |  | parse_ifconfig: The interface's first IPv4 address and prefix from darwin's `ifconfig IFACE`. |
| `toride::parse_ip_addr(text, iface)` |  |  |  | parse_ip_addr: The interface's IPv4 address and prefix from `ip -4 -o addr`. |
| `toride::parse_arp(text)` |  |  |  | parse_arp: darwin's `arp -an` as `{ip:, mac:, iface:}` rows; an incomplete entry is no row. An unprivileged reader is shown no rows at all. |
| `toride::arp_row(fs)` |  |  |  | arp_row: One `arp -an` line, split into fields, as an `{ip:, mac:, iface:}` row, or nil when it has no IPv4 address or no MAC. |
| `toride::parse_ip_neigh(text)` |  |  |  | parse_ip_neigh: `ip -4 neigh` as `{ip:, mac:, iface:}` rows; a FAILED or INCOMPLETE entry carries no lladdr and is no row. |
| `toride::neigh_row(fs)` |  |  |  | neigh_row: One `ip -4 neigh` line, split into fields, as an `{ip:, mac:, iface:}` row, or nil when it has no IPv4 address or no lladdr. |
| `toride::mac_of(rows, ip, iface)` |  |  |  | mac_of: The gateway's MAC from the neighbour rows on its interface, or :unread when there is no usable row. |
| `toride::match_site(candidates, mac)` |  |  |  | match_site: The configured site the machine stands on, from the sites whose network holds the interface address and whose gateway is the default gateway. A MAC, where a site names one, decides between them; when it is needed and unread the answer is :unread, and two sites left standing are :ambiguous. Neither ever picks one. |
| `toride::debounce(reachable, streak, threshold)` |  |  |  | debounce: The gateway axis from one probe and the failures before it: a down probe holds `up` until `threshold` fail in a row. Answers `[axis, streak]`. |
| `toride::assemble(cfg, obs, prior)` |  |  |  | assemble: The machine's run state from its observations: which site it stands on, whether the gateway answers, and the local network. |
| `toride::relation(entry_site, site)` |  |  |  | relation: An entry's site against the site the machine stands on. |
| `toride::entry_state(e, run)` |  |  |  | entry_state: The state of one entry in a run: its site against the run's site, the run's gateway, the entry's kind, whether its LAN address lies in the local network, and the platform. |
| `toride::answer(e, run)` |  |  |  | answer: One entry's decision with the address it is served at, nil when it is withheld or blind. |
| `toride::render(run, answers)` |  |  |  | render: The dnsmasq conf text for a run: one `address=` line per served domain, and a comment naming each withheld one with its reason. |
| `toride::converge(cfg, run, io)` |  |  |  | converge: Render the answers for a run and, when the text differs from the file, write it atomically and reload dnsmasq; a dry run only says so. Blind writes nothing. |
| `toride::reloaded(io, argv)` |  |  |  | reloaded: The exit status of the reload command `argv`, run through the `exec` of `io`. |
| `toride::write_atomic(path, text)` |  |  |  | write_atomic: Write `text` to `path` through a temporary file and a rename, so a reader never sees half a file. It makes the parent directory, and gives true on success and false on any failure. |
| `toride::routes_argv(cfg)` |  |  |  | routes_argv: The command that reads the main route table on a platform. |
| `toride::local_argv(cfg, iface)` |  |  |  | local_argv: The command that reads an interface's address on a platform. |
| `toride::neigh_argv(cfg, iface)` |  |  |  | neigh_argv: The command that reads the neighbour table on a platform. |
| `toride::probe(addr, ports, timeout_ms)` |  |  |  | probe: Whether the gateway accepts a TCP connection on any of the ports, the timeout shared between them; a probe that cannot be made is bad, never down. |
| `toride::reach(addr, ports, per)` |  |  | `B0083` | reach: Whether `addr` accepts a TCP connection on one of `ports`, each tried in order within `per` milliseconds. No ports means false. |
| `toride::live` |  |  | `B0084` | live: The machine's own effects: commands run by `capture`, the gateway reached by `probe`. A test hands `observe` and `run` its own. |
| `toride::observe(cfg, io)` |  |  |  | observe: Read the route table, the default interface's address, the neighbour table and the gateway probe through `io`. What cannot be read is bad, so the run is blind, never empty. |
| `toride::read_prior(path)` |  |  |  | read_prior: The streak, run count and last line from the state file at `path`. A missing or unreadable file gives zero runs, a zero streak and an empty line. |
| `toride::outcome_line(c, run, refusals)` |  |  |  | outcome_line: The run's one line: result, site, gateway, and how many names were served, withheld and refused. |
| `toride::loud?(line, prior_line, runs, heartbeat)` |  |  |  | loud?: Whether a run prints its line: when the line changed, and on every heartbeat. |
| `toride::run(cfg, io)` |  |  |  | run: One pass through `io`: observe, decide every entry, converge the dnsmasq conf, record the state file, and print the outcome line when it changed or on a heartbeat. An unreadable config is refused and writes nothing. |
| `toride::main` |  |  |  | main: One live run with the config at `TORIDE_CONFIG`, or at `/etc/toride/config.json` when the variable is not set. With no file at that path the config is unreadable, and the run is refused. |
| `toride::config` |  |  |  | config: The config fixture of toride, loaded by `load`. |
| `toride::alfa_commands` |  |  |  | alfa_commands: The fixture answers of a nixos machine at the alfa site: each pair is a command line and the text it prints. |
| `toride::bravo_commands(arp)` |  |  |  | bravo_commands: The fixture answers of a darwin machine at the bravo site, with `arp` naming the fixture that `arp -an` prints. |
| `toride::fake(commands, probed)` |  |  |  | fake: An `io` for tests. Its `exec` answers a command from `commands` by its joined command line, and a command not in the list does not start; its `probe` always gives `probed`. |
| `toride::at(cfg, commands, probed, streak)` |  |  |  | at: The run state that `assemble` builds from a `fake` observation of `commands` and `probed`, with `streak` as the prior streak. |
| `toride::served(cfg, run)` |  |  |  | served: Each entry's name mapped to its answer in `run`: the address it is served at, or the outcome label when it is withheld or blind. |
| `toride::darwin(cfg)` |  |  |  | darwin: The config `cfg` with its platform set to darwin. |
| `toride::without_site(cfg, name)` |  |  |  | without_site: The config `cfg` with the site `name` and every entry on that site taken out. |
| `toride::site_mac(cfg, name, mac)` |  |  |  | site_mac: The config `cfg` with the gateway MAC of the site `name` set to `mac`. |
| `toride::in_dir(cfg)` |  |  |  | in_dir: The config `cfg` with its dnsmasq conf and state file moved into a fresh directory, and its reload command set to `/usr/bin/true`. |

## toriyose

`toriyose` 0.1.2 · needs: deeta, eigyou, fairu, gamen, hizuke, isshou, jisho, kansuu, kiwari, kousei, kyohi, moji, ran, retsu, shomei, tesuto

toriyose (取り寄せ, fetching on order) — what a frontend asks for and keeps.

| definition | other name | shares a builtin's name | waived | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `toriyose::danpen::graphql` |  |  |  | graphql is the October 2021 GraphQL grammar as a kiwari spec, with the keywords and punctuation an editor paints. |
| `toriyose::danpen::punctuation_roles` |  |  |  | punctuation_roles pairs each GraphQL punctuator with the highlight role an editor paints it in. |
| `toriyose::danpen::directive_locations` |  |  |  | directive_locations lists every place a directive definition may name after `on`. |
| `toriyose::danpen::keyword_roles` |  |  |  | keyword_roles pairs each GraphQL keyword and builtin constant with its highlight role. |
| `toriyose::danpen::l(text)` |  |  |  | l is the grammar's short word for a literal token. |
| `toriyose::danpen::r(name)` |  |  |  | r is the grammar's short word for a reference to another rule. |
| `toriyose::danpen::o(item)` |  |  |  | o is the grammar's short word for an optional item. |
| `toriyose::danpen::s(items)` |  |  |  | s is the grammar's short word for a sequence of items. |
| `toriyose::danpen::described(items)` |  |  |  | described prefixes a type system rule with the optional description string GraphQL allows before it. |
| `toriyose::danpen::braced(open, item, close)` |  |  |  | braced is one or more items between an open and a close literal, as in a selection set. |
| `toriyose::danpen::separated(sep, item)` |  |  |  | separated is one or more items joined by a separator that may also lead, as in `= \| A \| B`. |
| `toriyose::danpen::graphql_rules` |  |  |  | graphql_rules holds every rule of the GraphQL grammar, each with the record kind it lowers to. |
| `toriyose::danpen::kinds` |  |  |  | kinds names every record kind the grammar lowers to, once each, in rule order. |
| `toriyose::danpen::table(keys)` |  |  |  | table turns a list of keys into a lookup map, so the lexer asks set membership in one step. |
| `toriyose::danpen::name_start_chars` |  |  |  | name_start_chars holds the characters a GraphQL name may begin with. |
| `toriyose::danpen::digit_chars` |  |  |  | digit_chars holds the decimal digits a GraphQL number is written in. |
| `toriyose::danpen::lexer(text)` |  |  |  | lexer prepares text for tokenizing: its characters and the character sets each token class tests against. |
| `toriyose::danpen::at(x, i)` |  |  |  | at reads the character at index `i` of a lexer's text, nil past its end. |
| `toriyose::danpen::slice(x, i, j)` |  |  |  | slice cuts the lexer's text from index `i` up to, not including, index `j`. |
| `toriyose::danpen::lex_error(line, col, message)` |  |  |  | lex_error raises a parse error that names the line and column where reading stopped. |
| `toriyose::danpen::tokens(text)` |  |  |  | tokens reads GraphQL text into its tokens, each with its kind, value, line and column; blanks, commas and comments drop out. |
| `toriyose::danpen::lex_from(x, i, line, ls, cur, done)` |  |  |  | lex_from is the tokenizing loop from index `i`, collecting tokens in chunks of 256 so a long document stays cheap to grow. |
| `toriyose::danpen::simple(kind, value, stop)` |  |  |  | simple is a one-line token read up to index `stop`. |
| `toriyose::danpen::token_at(x, i, c, line, col)` |  |  |  | token_at reads the one token that starts with character `c` at index `i`, choosing the reader by that character. |
| `toriyose::danpen::span_end(x, i, set)` |  |  |  | span_end: The index after the run of characters from `i` that the lexer's character set `set` (`:part`, `:digit`, ...) holds. |
| `toriyose::danpen::name_end(x, i)` |  |  |  | name_end is the index after the name characters that continue from `i`. |
| `toriyose::danpen::digits_end(x, i)` |  |  |  | digits_end is the index after the digits that continue from `i`. |
| `toriyose::danpen::number_at(x, i, line, col)` |  |  |  | number_at reads an Int or Float token from `i`, refusing a leading zero, a bare sign and a name glued to the number. |
| `toriyose::danpen::string_end(x, j, line, col)` |  |  |  | string_end finds the index of the quote that closes a string, skipping quotes escaped by an odd run of backslashes. |
| `toriyose::danpen::backslashes(x, i, n)` |  |  |  | backslashes counts the backslashes that end at index `i`, so a caller knows whether the next quote is escaped. |
| `toriyose::danpen::string_at(x, i, line, col)` |  |  |  | string_at reads a one-line string token from its opening quote at `i`, with its escapes decoded. |
| `toriyose::danpen::unescape(raw, line, col)` |  |  |  | unescape decodes the escape sequences of a string's raw text, refusing one GraphQL does not define. |
| `toriyose::danpen::braced_escapes(raw, line, col)` |  |  |  | braced_escapes rewrites each braced `\u{41}` escape as the JSON `A` form the decoder reads. |
| `toriyose::danpen::hex_value(hex, line, col)` |  |  |  | hex_value reads the hex digits of a braced escape as a number, refusing an empty, overlong or non-hex text. |
| `toriyose::danpen::hex4(n)` |  |  |  | hex4 writes a number below 65536 as four uppercase hex digits. |
| `toriyose::danpen::utf16_escape(hex, line, col)` |  |  |  | utf16_escape writes one Unicode scalar value as JSON escapes, a surrogate pair above the basic plane. |
